- `2024-01` **[Adapting Large Language Models for Document-Level Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06468)** — Wu et al.<br>Fine-tunes three LLM backbones with two methods on 18 document-level translation tasks across nine language pairs; specialized models sometimes beat GPT-4 but suffer off-target translation.
- `2024` **[Towards Demonstration-Aware Large Language Models for Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.824/)** — Li et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024*<br>Adds sentence- and document-level demonstrations in training and retrieved demonstrations at inference to tuned translation LLMs, improving domain-specific and document-level translation.
- `2024` **[LexMatcher: Dictionary-centric Data Curation for LLM-based Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.866/)** — Yin et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024*<br>LexMatcher: curates MT fine-tuning data for LLMs by bilingual-dictionary sense coverage, retrieving and augmenting data for infrequent senses; with LLaMA2 it beats baselines on WMT2022 and terminology translation.
- `2024` **[LANDeRMT: Dectecting and Routing Language-Aware Neurons for Selectively Finetuning LLMs to Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.656/)** — Zhu et al. · *ACL 2024*<br>LANDeRMT: detects language-general and language-specific neurons, then selectively finetunes and routes them for LLM translation to reduce parameter interference and catastrophic forgetting.
- `2024` **[Domain Dynamics: Evaluating Large Language Models in English-Hindi Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.icon-1.19/)** — Bhattacharjee et al. · *ICON 2024 (workshop)*<br>English-Hindi translation test suite spanning judicial, education, religious literature and noisy user-generated domains (about 250-300 sentences each) to expose limitations of LLM-based translation.
- `2024` **[Creative and Context-Aware Translation of East Asian Idioms with GPT-4](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.544/)** — Tang et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024*<br>Finds Pareto-optimal GPT-4 prompting strategies for creative, context-aware translation of East Asian idioms that outperform Google and DeepL and yield more high-quality translations per idiom than humans.
- `2024` **[Benchmarking and Improving Long-Text Translation with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.428/)** — Wang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024*<br>Builds an instruction-based multilingual, multi-domain document-level MT benchmark; finds LLM translation quality drops as documents grow and improves it with extrapolation strategies.
- `2024` **[Analysing Translation Artifacts: A Comparative Study of LLMs, NMTs, and Human Translations](https://aclanthology.org/2024.wmt-1.116/)** — Sizov et al. · *WMT 2024 (workshop)*<br>Uses Leave-One-Out and Integrated Gradients to find lexical features separating human, LLM and NMT translations from original text; LLM and NMT outputs still differ from native writing.
- `2023-12` **[Simul-LLM: A Framework for Exploring High-Quality Simultaneous Translation with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.567/)** — Agostinelli et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.04691)]<br>Simul-LLM: open-source fine-tuning and evaluation framework for LLMs in simultaneous translation, validating classical SimulMT practices and adapting NMT-fine-tuned LLMs to SimulMT.
- `2023-12` **[Fine-tuning Large Language Models for Adaptive Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.12740)** — Moslem et al.<br>Fine-tunes Mistral 7B on 20,000 segments mixing zero-shot and one-shot medical translation prompts; for Spanish-to-English it beats ChatGPT zero-shot and matches NLLB 3.3B.
- `2023-11` **[On-the-Fly Fusion of Large Language Models and Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.35/)** — Hoang et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.08306)]<br>Ensembles an NMT model with a prompted LLM on the fly across 4 language pairs; even a weaker-at-translation LLM improves NMT output, beating ensembles of two stronger MT models.
- `2023-11` **[Improving Machine Translation with Large Language Models: A Preliminary Study with Cooperative Decoding](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.786/)** — Zeng et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02851)]<br>Compares commercial NMT systems with MT-oriented LLMs and proposes Cooperative Decoding, using NMT as pretranslation and LLMs for complex cases beyond NMT.
- `2023-11` **[Direct Preference Optimization for Neural Machine Translation with Minimum Bayes Risk Decoding](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-short.34/)** — Yang et al. · *NAACL 2024 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.08380)]<br>Uses DPO to fine-tune multilingual LLMs so they obtain MBR decoding gains for translation without extra inference compute, using only a small monolingual fine-tuning set.
- `2023-11` **[Assessing Translation Capabilities of Large Language Models involving English and Indian Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2024.eamt-1.19/)** — Mujadia et al. · *EAMT 2024 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.09216)]<br>Evaluates raw, in-context and LoRA or fully fine-tuned LLMs for translation between English and 22 Indian languages; two-stage fine-tuned LLaMA-13b performs best among the LLMs tested.
- `2023-10` **[Translation Performance from the User's Perspective of Large Language Models and Neural Machine Translation Systems](https://doi.org/10.3390/info14100574)** — Son & Kim · *Inf. 2023*<br>Compares ChatGPT with Google Translate and Microsoft Translator on WMT 2018 and 2020 data; the NMT systems usually score higher, ChatGPT wins some pairs, and into-English translation is better.
- `2023-10` **[Steering Large Language Models for Machine Translation with Finetuning and In-Context Learning](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.744/)** — Alves et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13448)]<br>Shows LoRA fine-tuning for MT matches full fine-tuning with 50 times fewer trained parameters but degrades few-shot ability; adding few-shot examples during fine-tuning recovers it across 10 language pairs.
- `2023-10` **[On Bilingual Lexicon Induction with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.595/)** — Li et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13995)]<br>Studies zero-shot and few-shot prompting plus fine-tuning of 18 open multilingual text-to-text LLMs (0.3B-13B) for bilingual lexicon induction on two standard benchmarks.
- `2023-10` **[Contextual Refinement of Translations: Large Language Models for Sentence and Document-Level Post-Editing](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-long.148/)** — Koneru et al. · *NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.14855)]<br>Adapts LLMs as automatic post-editors with LoRA instead of direct translators, finding Q-LoRA translation fine-tuning raised BLEU but lowered COMET, and extends post-editing to document-level translation.
- `2023-10` **[An Empirical Study of Translation Hypothesis Ensembling with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.733/)** — Farinhas et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.11430)]<br>Studies ensembling LLM translation hypotheses (ChatGPT, LLaMA, Alpaca); MBR decoding is very effective, few samples suffice, and instruction tuning changes the diversity-temperature relation.
- `2023-09` **[SCALE: Synergized Collaboration of Asymmetric Language Translation Engines](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.941/)** — Cheng et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.17061)]<br>SCALE: connects compact specialized translation models with LLMs through triplet in-context demonstrations, beating few-shot GPT-4 and NLLB in low-resource settings such as Xhosa-English.
- `2023-09` **[Mitigating Hallucinations and Off-target Machine Translation with Source-Contrastive and Language-Contrastive Decoding](https://aclanthology.org/2024.eacl-short.4/)** — Sennrich et al. · *EACL 2024 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.07098)]<br>Source- and language-contrastive decoding reduce translations with chrF2 below 10 by 67-83% across 57 directions in M2M-100/SMaLL-100, and suppress off-target translation in LLMs, without retraining.
- `2023-09` **[ChatGPT MT: Competitive for High- (but Not Low-) Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.40/)** — Robinson et al. · *WMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.07423)]<br>Evaluates ChatGPT MT on FLORES-200 across 204 languages; GPT models lag traditional MT for 84.1% of languages, with resource level the strongest predictor and African languages disadvantaged.
- `2023-09` **[A Paradigm Shift in Machine Translation: Boosting Translation Performance of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.11674)** — Xu et al. · *ICLR 2024*<br>ALMA: fine-tunes LLaMA-2 first on monolingual data then on a small high-quality parallel set, gaining over 12 BLEU and 12 COMET over zero-shot across 10 WMT directions.
- `2023-08` **[Translate Meanings, Not Just Words: IdiomKB's Role in Optimizing Idiomatic Translation with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.13961)** — Li et al. · *AAAI 2023*<br>IdiomKB: multilingual idiom knowledge base built with large LMs that improves idiomatic translation by smaller models such as BLOOMZ, Alpaca and InstructGPT by retrieving figurative meanings.
- `2023-08` **[TARJAMAT: Evaluation of Bard and ChatGPT on Machine Translation of Ten Arabic Varieties](https://aclanthology.org/2023.arabicnlp-1.6/)** — Kadaoui et al. · *ARABICNLP 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.03051)]<br>TARJAMAT: evaluates Bard and ChatGPT (GPT-3.5, GPT-4) on MT across ten Arabic varieties; LLMs beat commercial systems on dialects on average but trail Google Translate on Classical Arabic and MSA.
- `2023-08` **[Optimizing Machine Translation through Prompt Engineering: An Investigation into ChatGPT’s Customizability](https://aclanthology.org/2023.mtsummit-users.19/)** — Yamada · *MTSUMMIT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.01391)]<br>Shows that adding the translation purpose and target audience to ChatGPT prompts changes its translations and generally improves quality by industry standards, judged from a translator's viewpoint.
- `2023-08` **[Improving Translation Faithfulness of Large Language Models via Augmenting Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12674)** — Chen et al.<br>SWIE adds a global instruction representation and the OVERMISS dataset contrasts over- and miss-translations, improving BLOOM and LLaMA translation faithfulness, especially zero-shot and long text.
- `2023-07` **[TIM: Teaching Large Language Models to Translate with Comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.04408)** — Zeng et al. · *AAAI 2023*<br>TIM: teaches LLMs to translate by comparing correct and incorrect translation examples with an additional preference loss, evaluated on four WMT2022 directions and FLORES-200.
- `2023-06` **[Iterative Translation Refinement with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.eamt-1.17/)** — Chen et al. · *EAMT 2024 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.03856)]<br>Iteratively prompting an LLM to refine its own translation lowers string-based metric scores but keeps or improves neural metric scores, and humans rate outputs more fluent.
- `2023-05` **[Searching for Needles in a Haystack: On the Role of Incidental Bilingualism in PaLM’s Translation Capability](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-long.524/)** — Briakou et al. · *ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10266)]<br>Finds PaLM saw over 30 million incidental translation pairs across at least 44 languages; this bilingual content substantially aids translation, though its impact diminishes with scale.
- `2023-05` **[RAMP: Retrieval and Attribute-Marking Enhanced Prompting for Attribute-Controlled Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-short.126/)** — Sarti et al. · *ACL 2023 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17131)]<br>RAMP: attribute-controlled translation by prompting multilingual LLMs with retrieved similar examples marked with attribute labels, improving accuracy over standard prompting in few- and zero-shot settings.
- `2023-05` **[Leveraging GPT-4 for Automatic Translation Post-Editing](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.804/)** — Raunak et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14878)]<br>Uses GPT-4 to post-edit NMT outputs, improving quality and removing major errors on WMT-22 En-Zh, En-De, Zh-En and De-En, though it can produce hallucinated edits.
- `2023-05` **[Exploring Human-Like Translation Strategy with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.tacl-1.13/)** — He et al. · *TACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.04118)]<br>MAPS: prompts LLMs to extract keywords, topics and relevant demonstrations before translating, then uses quality estimation to select among candidates and filter unhelpful knowledge.
- `2023-05` **[Eliciting the Translation Ability of Large Language Models via Multilingual Finetuning with Translation Instructions](https://aclanthology.org/2024.tacl-1.32/)** — Li et al. · *TACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.15083)]<br>Fine-tunes XGLM-7.5B on multilingual translation instructions; quality depends on similarity to English and pretraining data amount, and relies on instruction understanding and cross-language alignment.
- `2023-05` **[Do GPTs Produce Less Literal Translations?](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-short.90/)** — Raunak et al. · *ACL 2023 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16806)]<br>GPT translations out of English are less literal than NMT outputs by alignment and monotonicity measures while scoring similar or better on MT quality metrics, confirmed by humans.
- `2023-05` **[Chain-of-Dictionary Prompting Elicits Translation in Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.55/)** — Lu et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.06575)]<br>CoD: adds chained multilingual dictionary entries for some input words to LLM translation prompts, gaining up to 13x chrF++ on FLORES-200 and helping ChatGPT surpass NLLB 3.3B.
- `2023-05` **[CTQScorer: Combining Multiple Features for In-context Example Selection for Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.519/)** — Kumar et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14105)]<br>CTQ Scorer: regression model combining multiple example features to select in-context examples for LLM translation, gaining over 2.5 COMET on average over BM25 retrieval.
- `2023-05` **[BigTranslate: Augmenting Large Language Models with Multilingual Translation Capability over 100 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.18098)** — Yang et al.<br>BigTranslate: LLaMA-13B continually trained on Chinese and 102-language parallel data, then translation-instruction tuned; comparable to ChatGPT and Google Translate in many languages.
- `2023-05` **[Augmenting Large Language Model Translators via Translation Memories](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-acl.653/)** — Mu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.17367)]<br>Prompting LLMs with translation memories greatly improves their translations, reaching results comparable to strong NMT systems trained on large in-domain bilingual data.
- `2023-04` **[Multilingual Machine Translation with Large Language Models: Empirical Results and Analysis](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.176/)** — Zhu et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.04675)]<br>Evaluates eight LLMs on massively multilingual MT: GPT-4 beats NLLB in 40.91% of directions but trails Google Translate, especially for low-resource languages; cross-lingual exemplars help.
- `2023-04` **[Large Language Models Effectively Leverage Document-level Context for Literary Translation, but Critical Errors Persist](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.41/)** — Karpinska & Iyyer · *WMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.03245)]<br>Human evaluation over 18 language pairs finds GPT-3.5 paragraph-level literary translation beats sentence-level, with fewer mistranslations, though critical errors such as content omissions persist; annotations released.
- `2023-04` **[Document-Level Machine Translation with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.1036/)** — Wang et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02210)]<br>Evaluates LLMs on document-level MT and discourse modelling; context-aware prompts matter, and GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 beat commercial MT systems in human evaluation.
- `2023-03` **[Towards Making the Most of ChatGPT for Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.373/)** — Peng et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13780)]<br>Shows ChatGPT MT improves with lower temperature and task- and domain-specific prompts, hallucinates on non-English-centric pairs, and degrades to word-by-word translation with chain-of-thought prompting.
- `2023-03` **[Investigating the Translation Performance of a Large Multilingual Language Model: the Case of BLOOM](https://aclanthology.org/2023.eamt-1.16/)** — Bawden & Yvon · *EAMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.01911)]<br>Evaluates BLOOM translation on WMT, Flores-101 and DiaBLa: zero-shot suffers from overgeneration and wrong-language output, while few-shot gives very good results for many language pairs.
- `2023-02` **[The unreasonable effectiveness of few-shot learning for machine translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.01398)** — García et al. · *ICML 2023*<br>Shows a self-supervised decoder-only model given five translation examples at inference matches supervised systems, beating the best WMT'21 English–Chinese news system, and can control regional variety and formality.
- `2023-02` **[How Good Are GPT Models at Machine Translation? A Comprehensive Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.09210)** — Hendy et al.<br>Evaluates ChatGPT, text-davinci-003 and text-davinci-002 on 18 translation directions; GPT models are competitive for high-resource languages but limited for low-resource ones, and hybrid systems help.
- `2023-02` **[Dictionary-based Phrase-level Prompting of Large Language Models for Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.07856)** — Ghazvininejad et al.<br>DiPMT: adds bilingual dictionary translation hints for selected input words to LLM MT prompts, outperforming baselines in low-resource and out-of-domain translation.
- `2023-01` **[Prompting Large Language Model for Machine Translation: A Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.07069)** — Zhang et al. · *ICML 2023*<br>Systematic study of prompting GLM-130B for translation: example number and quality matter, pseudo-parallel examples from monolingual data help, and examples transfer across settings.
- `2023-01` **[Is ChatGPT A Good Translator? Yes With GPT-4 As The Engine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.08745)** — Jiao et al.<br>Evaluates ChatGPT for MT: competitive with commercial systems on high-resource European languages, lags on low-resource or distant ones; pivot prompting through a high-resource language helps.
- `2023-01` **[Interactive-Chain-Prompting: Ambiguity Resolution for Crosslingual Conditional Generation with Interaction](https://aclanthology.org/2023.ijcnlp-main.31/)** — Pilault et al. · *IJCNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.10309)]<br>Interactive-chain prompting: a Translator model and a User model resolve ambiguity subproblems through question-answer steps before translating; releases ambiguity datasets for four languages.
- `2023-01` **[Adaptive Machine Translation with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.eamt-1.22/)** — Moslem et al. · *EAMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.13294)]<br>Shows GPT-3.5 in-context learning with in-domain sentence pairs and terminology enables real-time adaptive MT, surpassing strong encoder-decoder MT systems especially for high-resource languages.
- `2023` **[Translating a low-resource language using GPT-3 and a human-readable dictionary](https://aclanthology.org/2023.sigmorphon-1.2/)** — Elsner & Needle · *SIGMORPHON 2023 (workshop)*<br>Translates Inuktitut words with GPT-3 by combining human-readable dictionary definitions, reaching up to 18.5 BLEU; grammar information is mostly ineffective and morpheme-definition derivation hallucinates.
- `2023` **[ParroT: Translating during Chat using Large Language Models tuned with Human Translation and Feedback](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.1001/)** — Jiao et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02426)]<br>ParroT: instruction-tunes LLaMA for translation with human translations and feedback using translation, contrastive and error-guided instructions; error-guided data further improves results on Flores and WMT22.
- `2023` **[Machine Translation with Large Language Models: Prompting, Few-shot Learning, and Fine-tuning with QLoRA](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.43/)** — Zhang et al. · *WMT 2023 (workshop)*<br>Evaluates 15 public LLMs for MT with zero-shot prompting, few-shot learning and QLoRA fine-tuning; on French-English, QLoRA tuning 0.77% of parameters beats few-shot learning.
- `2023` **[Linguistically Motivated Evaluation of the 2023 State-of-the-art Machine Translation: Can ChatGPT Outperform NMT?](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.23/)** — Manakhimova et al. · *WMT 2023 (workshop)*<br>Applies a linguistically motivated test suite to WMT23 outputs in German-English, English-German and English-Russian; GPT-4 matches the best systems for German but falls to the second cluster for English-Russian.
- `2023` **[Leveraging Multilingual Knowledge Graph to Boost Domain-specific Entity Translation of ChatGPT](https://aclanthology.org/2023.mtsummit-users.7/)** — Zhang et al. · *MTSUMMIT 2023 (workshop)*<br>Adds bilingual entity pairs from a multilingual knowledge graph to ChatGPT translation prompts, raising zh-en domain BLEU by +6.21, +3.13 and +11.25 on three domains.
- `2023` **[Hallucinations in Large Multilingual Translation Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.tacl-1.85/)** — Guerreiro et al. · *TACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16104)]<br>Analyzes hallucinations of M2M NMT models and GPT LLMs across over 100 language pairs; they concentrate in low-resource and out-of-English directions, and LLMs hallucinate differently.
- `2023` **[Findings of the 2023 Conference on Machine Translation (WMT23): LLMs Are Here but Not Quite There Yet](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.1/)** — Kocmi et al. · *WMT 2023 (workshop)*<br>Reports WMT23 general MT task results for 8 language pairs (14 directions) across up to four domains, judged by professional DA+SQM human evaluation, including LLM-based systems.
- `2023` **[Do online Machine Translation Systems Care for Context? What About a GPT Model?](https://aclanthology.org/2023.eamt-1.39/)** — Castilho et al. · *EAMT 2023 (workshop)*<br>Tests online MT systems and ChatGPT on six context-related issues in document-level translation; ChatGPT seems to outperform the NMT systems but performs poorly for Irish.
- `2023` **[Dissecting In-Context Learning of Translations in GPT-3](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.61/)** — Raunak et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023*<br>Perturbs GPT-3 translation demonstrations: source-side changes barely matter while target-side ones sharply hurt quality; proposes Zero-Shot-Context to add this signal to zero-shot prompts.
- `2023` **[DecoMT: Decomposed Prompting for Machine Translation Between Related Languages using Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.279/)** — Puduppully et al. · *EMNLP 2023*<br>DecoMT: few-shot prompting that decomposes translation between related languages into word-chunk translations, beating few-shot BLOOM by 8 chrF++ on average.
- `2022-12` **[In-context Examples Selection for Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-acl.564/)** — Agrawal et al. · *Findings of ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.02437)]<br>Studies in-context example selection for LLM translation: a single noisy example can be catastrophic, while adding examples with n-gram overlap to the source consistently improves quality.
- `2022-11` **[Prompting PaLM for Translation: Assessing Strategies and Performance](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-long.859/)** — Vilar et al. · *ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.09102)]<br>Studies few-shot example selection for PaLM translation, finding example quality matters most; with optimized prompts PaLM still lags the best supervised MT systems.
- `2022-09` **[Bidirectional Language Models Are Also Few-shot Learners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.14500)** — Patel et al. · *ICLR 2022*<br>SAP (Sequential Autoregressive Prompting) lets bidirectional mT5 be prompted; its few- and zero-shot translations beat few-shot GPT-3 and XGLM with about 50% fewer parameters.
- `2021-06` **[The FLORES-101 Evaluation Benchmark for Low-Resource and Multilingual Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.03193)** — Goyal et al.<br>FLORES-101: 3001 English Wikipedia sentences professionally translated into 101 languages, multilingually aligned for evaluating low-resource and many-to-many translation.

[⬆ Back to top](#)

## Translation & Generation Evaluation with LLMs

- `2026-10` **[Large Language Models for Machine Translation Quality Annotation: Humans and Models Are Both Challenged](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.577/)** — Almaghout et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10918)]<br>Evaluates LLMs as MQM and ESA translation-quality annotators against humans on a 70-language-pair long-context set and WMT23/WMT25, comparing score and error-span agreement.
- `2026-10` **[Beyond Prompts: A Systematic Study of LLM-Based Machine Translation Evaluators](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.2404/)** — Popov et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33741)]<br>Studies how instructions, demonstrations, backbones and workflows shape LLM MT evaluators using new expert RATE annotations on WMT24/25 English-Russian, yielding ORBIT evaluators at roughly 20x lower cost.
- `2026-09` **[When Metrics Reward the Worst Translations: Internalizing Cultural Reasoning for Social Media Translation Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08156)** — Qiu et al.<br>Shows COMET, XCOMET, BERTScore and LLM judges fail on culturally loaded social-media translation, and proposes CuRIL, RL internalizing cultural reasoning, lifting Qwen3-8B to kappa 0.370.
- `2026-09` **[Can We Triage LLM Translation Errors in Classical Texts Without Human References? Source Novelty, GEMBA Scoring, and Budgeted Review through Pali-to-English Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14963)** — Metzger<br>Compares five reference-free error-triage signals on 15,493 LLM Pali-to-English translations; no-reference GEMBA scoring by a stronger-model panel works best, source novelty only as a prior.
- `2026-04` **[LQM: Linguistically Motivated Multidimensional Quality Metrics for Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.2012/)** — Magdy et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18490)]<br>LQM: a six-level linguistically grounded MT error taxonomy, with a 3,850-sentence parallel corpus across seven Arabic dialects and 6,113 expert-annotated error spans from six LLMs' zero-shot translations.
- `2026-02` **[Evaluating Extremely Low-Resource Machine Translation: A Comparative Study of ChrF++ and BLEU Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.17425)** — Kumar et al.<br>Compares BLEU and ChrF++ on LLM and NMT outputs for Magahi, Bhojpuri and Chhattisgarhi, finding BLEU adds complementary lexical-precision insight despite lower absolute scores.
- `2026` **[SwissGov-RSD: A Human-annotated, Cross-lingual Benchmark for Token-level Recognition of Semantic Differences Between Related Documents](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1437/)** — Wastl et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>SwissGov-RSD: 224 multi-parallel documents in English-German, -French and -Italian with token-level human annotations of semantic differences; LLMs and encoders perform poorly on it.
- `2026` **[STEEREVAL: Inference-time Interventions Strengthen Multilingual Generalization in Neural Summarization Metrics](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2486/)** — Casola et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15809)]<br>STEEREVAL: steering neural summarization metrics toward an English pivot with a single language-independent vector improves metric–human agreement across five languages, including for COMET.
- `2026` **[HalloMTBench: A Large-Scale Human-Verified Benchmark for Diagnosing Hallucination Triggers in LLM Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1546/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>HalloMTBench: 6,908 human-verified LLM translation hallucinations across 11 English-to-X directions, with a taxonomy separating Instruction Detachment from Source Detachment; evaluates 21 model variants.
- `2026` **[Exploring the Impact of Document Context on LLM-based Machine Translation Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1839/)** — Aur et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026*<br>Studies document context in LLM-based MT evaluation; effects vary across test sets, input repetition explains part of the gains, and fine-tuning helps more when context is available.
- `2026` **[DITING: Evaluating Sentence-Level Cultural Meaning in Web Novel Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1959/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>DITING: expert-annotated 18K+ Chinese-English web-novel sentence pairs for cultural meaning in translation, with the AgentEval multi-agent evaluator and MetricAlign meta-evaluation; zero-pronoun resolution remains the main bottleneck.
- `2026` **[DETECT: Determining Ease and Textual Clarity of German Text Simplifications](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.131/)** — Korobeynikova et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>DETECT: German text-simplification metric scoring simplicity, meaning preservation and fluency, trained on synthetic LLM-generated scores; correlates with human judgments better than common metrics.
- `2025-10` **[To eat or to feed: can large language models provide useful feedback in translation education?](https://doi.org/10.1080/1750399X.2025.2533074)** — Jiao et al.<br>Uses a GPT-4 pipeline to generate translation quality feedback on student translations; the feedback overlaps strongly with expert feedback and students respond positively.
- `2025-08` **[ALOPE: Adaptive Layer Optimization for Translation Quality Estimation using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07484)** — Sindhujan et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>ALOPE: improves LLM-based translation quality estimation with layer-wise LoRA adaptation, regression heads, dynamic multi-layer weighting and multi-head regression.
- `2025-07` **[Evaluating Text Style Transfer: A Nine-language Benchmark for Text Detoxification](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-358/)** — Protasov et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.15557)]<br>Benchmarks text detoxification evaluation metrics across nine languages, comparing neural metrics with LLM-as-a-judge; the proposed metrics correlate much better with human judgments than baselines.
- `2025-06` **[SSA-COMET: Do LLMs Outperform Learned Metrics in Evaluating MT for Under-Resourced African Languages?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.656/)** — Li et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04557)]<br>SSA-MTE: over 73,000 human MT-quality annotations for 14 African language pairs; SSA-COMET metrics trained on it beat AfriCOMET and rival Gemini 2.5 Pro, especially on Twi, Luo and Yoruba.
- `2025-05` **[LiTransProQA: An LLM-based Literary Translation Evaluation Metric with Professional Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1482/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05423)]<br>LiTransProQA: reference-free LLM question-answering metric for literary translation using professional translator insights, beating current metrics by over 15 points in adequacy assessment.
- `2025-04` **[ReMedy: Learning Machine Translation Evaluation from Human Preferences with Reward Modeling](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.217/)** — Tan & Monz · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13630)]<br>ReMedy: MT metric trained as a reward model on pairwise preferences; across WMT22-24 (39 language pairs) ReMedy-9B beats MetricX-13B, XCOMET-Ensemble and GEMBA-GPT-4.
- `2025-04` **[Do LLMs Understand Your Translations? Evaluating Paragraph-level MT with Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07583)** — Fernandes et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>TREQA: rates paragraph-level translations by how well they answer reading-comprehension questions on key source information; competitive with neural and LLM metrics on literary texts.
- `2025-04` **[An LLM-as-a-judge Approach for Scalable Gender-Neutral Translation Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.gitt-1.3/)** — Piergentili et al. · *GITT 2025 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11934)]<br>Tests LLMs as judges of gender-neutral translation across multiple languages with five models; prompting for phrase-level annotations before sentence-level verdicts consistently improves accuracy.
- `2025-01` **[When LLMs Struggle: Reference-less Translation Evaluation for Low-resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.loreslm-1.33/)** — Sindhujan et al. · *LORESLM 2025 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.04473)]<br>Evaluates LLMs zero/few-shot and instruction-tuned for MT quality estimation on low-resource pairs; fine-tuned encoder QE models win, with tokenization, transliteration and named-entity errors.
- `2025` **[RUBRIC-MQM : Span-Level LLM-as-judge in Machine Translation For High-End Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-industry.12/)** — Kim · *ACL 2025 (industry)*<br>PromptCUE meta-evaluation exposes label biases and failures on near-perfect translations in LLM MT judges such as GEMBA-MQM; Rubric-MQM adds span-level error quantification with rubric prompts.
- `2025` **[Are Large Reasoning Models Good Translation Evaluators? Analysis and Performance Boost](https://openreview.net/forum?id=cANLnJb1FX)** — Zhan et al. · *NeurIPS 2025*<br>Analyzes large reasoning models as MT judges, finding overthinking and score overestimation; training on synthetic human-like thinking cuts thinking budget ~35x and improves WMT24 correlations.
- `2024-10` **[What do Large Language Models Need for Machine Translation Evaluation?](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.214/)** — Qian et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03278)]<br>Tests which inputs (source, reference, error annotations, guidelines) and prompting methods LLMs need to evaluate MT for eight language pairs; references prove important, and larger models gain more from CoT.
- `2024-10` **[SpeechQE: Estimating the Quality of Direct Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.1218/)** — Han et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21485)]<br>SpeechQE: benchmark and systems for quality estimation of speech translation, including an end-to-end model built on a text LLM; end-to-end systems beat cascaded text-QE approaches.
- `2024-10` **[On the Implications of Verbose LLM Outputs: A Case Study in Translation Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00863)** — Briakou et al.<br>Shows verbose LLM translations in WMT 2024, triggered by safety, copyright and insufficient context, are unfairly penalized by both automatic and human evaluation.
- `2024-10` **[How Good Are LLMs for Literary Translation, Really? Literary Translation Evaluation with Humans and LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.548/)** — Zhang et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18697)]<br>LITEVAL-CORPUS: paragraph-level literary translations from 9 MT systems in four language pairs with 13k evaluated sentences; simpler schemes and expert evaluators give more adequate human evaluation.
- `2024-09` **[MQM-APE: Toward High-Quality Error Annotation Predictors with Automatic Post-Editing in LLM Translation Evaluators](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.374/)** — Lu et al. · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14335)]<br>MQM-APE: training-free framework where an LLM annotates errors, post-edits per error and verifies quality to filter non-impactful errors, improving the reliability of LLM error-span annotations.
- `2024-07` **[Machine Translation Hallucination Detection for Low and High Resource Languages using Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.564/)** — Benkirane et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16470)]<br>Evaluates LLM and embedding-based MT hallucination detection over 16 language directions; Llama3-70B gains up to 0.16 MCC on high-resource, while Claude Sonnet leads low-resource languages.
- `2024-06` **[xTower: A Multilingual LLM for Explaining and Correcting Translation Errors](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.892/)** — Treviso et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.19482)]<br>xTower: open LLM built on TowerBase that writes free-text explanations of translation errors to guide corrections, judged by expert translators and improving translation quality.
- `2024-06` **[PrExMe! Large Scale Prompt Exploration of Open Source LLMs for Machine Translation and Summarization Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.641/)** — Leiter & Eger · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18528)]<br>PrExMe: evaluates over 720 prompt templates for open-source LLM-based MT and summarization metrics (over 6.6M evaluations), finding rankings sensitive to small changes such as output format.
- `2024-01` **[Machine Translation Meta Evaluation through Translation Accuracy Challenge Sets](https://aclanthology.org/2025.cl-1.4/)** — Moghe et al. · *CL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16313)]<br>ACES: contrastive challenge set over 146 language pairs and 68 accuracy error types; benchmarking 47 WMT metrics shows LLM-based evaluators are unreliable and most metrics ignore the source.
- `2024-01` **[Lost in the Source Language: How Large Language Models Evaluate the Quality of Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.211/)** — Huang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06568)]<br>Finds LLM translation evaluators gain accuracy from reference translations, while source text is sometimes counterproductive, showing they do not fully exploit cross-lingual capability.
- `2024` **[Error Analysis Prompting Enables Human-Like Translation Evaluation in Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.520/)** — Lu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13809)]<br>EAPrompt: combines chain-of-thought and error analysis to prompt LLMs for MQM-style MT evaluation, improving segment- and system-level assessment on WMT22 metrics data.
- `2023-10` **[GEMBA-MQM: Detecting Translation Quality Error Spans with GPT-4](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.64/)** — Kocmi & Federmann · *WMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13988)]<br>GEMBA-MQM: fixed three-shot GPT-4 prompting with language-agnostic prompts to mark translation error spans for reference-free quality estimation, leading in system ranking in preliminary results.
- `2023-08` **[The Devil Is in the Errors: Leveraging Large Language Models for Fine-grained Machine Translation Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.wmt-1.100/)** — Fernandes et al. · *WMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.07286)]<br>AutoMQM: prompts LLMs such as PaLM-2 to identify and categorize translation errors, improving over score-only prompting and yielding error spans aligned with human annotations.
- `2023-05` **[SEAHORSE: A Multilingual, Multifaceted Dataset for Summarization Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.584/)** — Clark et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.13194)]<br>SEAHORSE: 96K summaries with human ratings on 6 quality dimensions across 6 languages, 9 systems and 4 datasets; metrics trained on it perform well on TRUE and mFACE.
- `2023-02` **[Large Language Models Are State-of-the-Art Evaluators of Translation Quality](https://aclanthology.org/2023.eamt-1.19/)** — Kocmi & Federmann · *EAMT 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.14520)]<br>GEMBA: zero-shot GPT prompting metric for translation quality with or without references; works only with GPT-3.5 and larger, reaching top system-level accuracy against WMT22 MQM labels.
- `2020-09` **[COMET: A Neural Framework for MT Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2020.emnlp-main.213/)** — Rei et al. · *EMNLP 2020* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.09025)]<br>COMET: framework that trains multilingual MT evaluation models from cross-lingual pretrained language models using source and reference; DA, HTER and MQM variants lead the WMT 2019 Metrics task.
