## Code-Switching, Dialects & Transliteration

- `2026-06` **[Code-Switching Reveals Language Anchoring in Multilingual LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1719/)** — Park et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19668)]<br>Introduces Anchor Bias, measuring whether code-switched hidden states align with source or target language, and CANVAS, an inference-time steering method that recovers code-switched QA performance.
- `2026-03` **[Benchmarking Bengali Dialectal Bias: A Multi-Stage Framework Integrating RAG-Based Translation and Human-Augmented RLAIF](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.9/)** — Sami et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21359)]<br>Benchmarks 19 LLMs on 4,000 question sets translated into nine Bengali dialects via RAG; comprehension drops with divergence, e.g., Chittagong scores 5.44/10 versus 7.68/10 for Tangail.
- `2026-02` **[Meenz bleibt Meenz, but Large Language Models Do Not Speak Its Dialect](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-258/)** — Bui et al. · *LREC 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.16852)]<br>Releases a 2,351-word Meenzerisch (Mainz dialect) dictionary with Standard German meanings; LLMs reach only 6.27% accuracy defining and 1.51% generating dialect words.
- `2026-02` **[Lost in Speech: Benchmarking, Evaluation, and Parsing of Spoken Bilingual Conversational Language Beyond Standard UD Assumptions](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1797/)** — Tyagi et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06307)]<br>SpokeBench: expert-annotated English-Spanish spoken bilingual parsing benchmark with a phenomenon taxonomy, the Flex-UD metric and DECAP, an agentic LLM parsing framework.
- `2026-01` **[PINGPONG: A Natural Benchmark for Multi-Turn Code-Switching Dialogues](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1854/)** — Farhansyah et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17277)]<br>PingPong: human-authored multi-party code-switching dialogues across five language combinations, some trilingual, with QA, summarization and topic classification tasks; language models remain limited on code-switched inputs.
- `2026-01` **[INDIC DIALECT: A Multi Task Benchmark to Evaluate and Translate in Indian Language Dialects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10388)** — Sharma et al.<br>INDIC-DIALECT: human-curated parallel corpus of 13k sentence pairs across 11 Hindi and Odia dialects with classification, MCQ and MT tasks; GPT-4o and Gemini 2.5 classify dialects poorly.
- `2026-01` **[DialectLLM: A Dialect-Aware Dialog[ue] Generation Framework Beyond Standard American English](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22888)** — Oh et al. · *NeurIPS 2026*<br>DialectLLM: generates dialect-parallel dialogue data across nine English dialects with linguist-validated lexical, orthographic and morphosyntactic rules; responses should not reproduce up to 90% of a dialect's grammatical features.
- `2026-01` **[ChiEngMixBench: Evaluating Large Language Models on Expert-Style Chinese-English Terminology Mixing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16217)** — Yang et al.<br>ChiEngMixBench: 1,706 Chinese-English code-mixed candidate pairs from AI/CS discussions; nine open-weight models give Chinese equivalents higher likelihood than source-attested English terms on most pairs.
- `2026-01` **[Can Large Language Models Understand, Reason About, and Generate Code-Switched Text?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07153)** — Winata et al.<br>CodeMixQA: human-annotated benchmark of 16 parallel code-switched language-pair variants in original and transliterated scripts, used to evaluate LLM understanding, reasoning and generation of code-switched text.
- `2026` **[Would LLMs be Good Historical Linguists and Chinese Dialect Learners?](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.831/)** — Liu et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>Builds a dataset covering Middle Chinese and 12 modern Chinese dialects for sound-change rule induction and dialectal IPA transcription; historical knowledge helps LLMs beat baselines including DeepSeek-V3.2.
- `2026` **[SwitchLingua V2: Agent-Driven Code-Switching via Digital Clones](https://openreview.net/forum?id=crYQ4XESFa)** — Xie et al. · *NeurIPS 2026 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>SwitchLingua V2: 260K LLM-agent-generated code-switching dialogues (1.04M turns) across 14 language pairs, converted into over 1,558 hours of audio for ASR.
- `2026` **[Do LLMs model human linguistic variation? A case study in Hindi-English Verb code-mixing](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.291/)** — Choudhary et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026*<br>Compares LLM perplexities with native speaker preferences on Hindi-English verb code-mixing, finding LLMs do not reliably match judgments; releases judgments on 30 verb pairs and ratios for 4,279.
- `2026` **[DiaLLM: An Investigation into the Robustness-Generation Gap in English Dialect Adaptation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2321/)** — Painter et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07669)]<br>DiaLLM: continual pretraining on the International Corpus of English plus post-training and alignment for Australian, Indian and Northern British English, revealing benchmarks miss alignment-driven dialectal generation changes.
- `2026` **[Alexandria: A Multi-Domain Dialectal Arabic Machine Translation Dataset for Culturally Inclusive and Linguistically Diverse LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1503/)** — Mekki et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13099)]<br>Alexandria: 107K-turn human-translated English-dialectal Arabic conversations covering 13 countries and 11 domains with city-level and gender metadata; LLMs still struggle across dialects.
- `2025-12` **[Script Gap: Evaluating LLM Triage on Indian Languages in Native vs Romanized Scripts in a Real World Setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10780)** — Khullar et al.<br>Benchmarks LLMs on real maternal and newborn health triage queries in five Indian languages and Nepali; romanized input lowers performance by up to 24 points versus native script.
- `2025-11` **[GRDD+: An Extended Greek Dialectal Dataset with Cross-Architecture Fine-tuning Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-245/)** — Chatzikyriakidis et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03772)]<br>GRDD+: Greek dialectal dataset of 6,374,939 words across 10 varieties, used to fine-tune Llama-3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B and Krikri-8B and compare them with frontier models.
- `2025-10` **[Idiom Understanding as a Tool to Measure the Dialect Gap](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.24/)** — Beauchemin et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05026)]<br>QFrCoRE, QFrCoRT and MFrCoE: Quebec and Metropolitan French idiom benchmarks; among 111 LLMs, 65.77% perform significantly worse on Quebec idioms, revealing a dialect gap.
- `2025-10` **[Analyzing Dialectical Biases in LLMs for Knowledge and Reasoning Benchmarks](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.1139/)** — Pan et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00962)]<br>Rewriting Standard American English multiple-choice questions into dialectal variants cuts LLM accuracy by up to 20%; three grammar rules (existential 'it', zero copula, y'all) explain most degradation.
- `2025-09` **[Tokenization and Representation Biases in Multilingual Models on Dialectal NLP Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1224/)** — Kanjirangat et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20045)]<br>Correlates Tokenization Parity and Information Parity with dialectal task performance for LLMs and encoders; TP better predicts syntax-reliant tasks, IP semantic tasks.
- `2025-09` **[Make Every Letter Count: Building Dialect Variation Dictionaries from Monolingual Corpora](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.762/)** — Litschko et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17855)]<br>DiaLemma: framework for dialect variation dictionaries from monolingual data, yielding 100K human-annotated German-Bavarian word pairs; nine LLMs do best on nouns and struggle to separate translations from inflected variants.
- `2025-09` **[Enhancing Cross-Lingual Transfer through Reversible Transliteration: A Huffman-Based Approach for Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.795/)** — Zhuang et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17493)]<br>Combines character transliteration with Huffman coding into a lossless framework for low-resource non-Latin-script languages, cutting token counts 50-80% without vocabulary expansion.
- `2025-07` **[CodeMixBench: Evaluating Code-Mixing Capabilities of LLMs Across 18 Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.109/)** — Yang & Chai · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18791)]<br>CodeMixBench: code-mixing benchmark of eight tasks in 18 languages from seven families, with GPT-4-based synthetic code-mixed data; LLMs consistently underperform on code-mixed data.
- `2025-07` **[A Tale of Two Scripts: Transliteration and Post-Correction for Judeo-Arabic](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.93/)** — González et al. · *EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.04746)]<br>Transliterates Judeo-Arabic from Hebrew to Arabic script via character mapping plus post-correction, gives the first LLM benchmark on the task, and enables Arabic tagging and MT tools.
- `2025-06` **[Parsing the Switch: LLM-Based UD Annotation for Complex Code-Switched and Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.863/)** — Kellert et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07274)]<br>BiLingua Parser: few-shot LLM prompting plus expert review for UD annotation of Spanish-English and Spanish-Guaraní code-switched text, releasing the first Spanish-Guaraní UD corpus; reaches 95.29% LAS after revision.
- `2025-06` **[Minimal Pair-Based Evaluation of Code-Switching](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.910/)** — Sterner & Teufel · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01840)]<br>Builds code-switching minimal pairs (natural vs. manipulated sentence), up to 1,000 for each of 11 language pairs; bilinguals prefer natural ones, and larger LLMs assign them higher probability more consistently.
- `2025-06` **[Lost in the Mix: Evaluating LLM Understanding of Code-Switched Text](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.2080/)** — Mohamed et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14012)]<br>Builds code-switched variants of reasoning and comprehension benchmarks; foreign tokens in English text hurt LLMs, embedding English into other languages often helps, and fine-tuning mitigates degradation more stably than prompting.
- `2025-06` **[Dialect Normalization using Large Language Models and Morphological Rules](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1215/)** — Dimakis et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08907)]<br>Combines rule-based morphological transformations with few-shot LLM prompting to normalize Greek dialect text without parallel data, applied to regional proverbs and evaluated by human annotators.
- `2025-03` **[COMI-LINGUA: Expert Annotated Large-Scale Dataset for Multitask NLP in Hindi-English Code-Mixing](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.422/)** — Sheth et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21670)]<br>COMI-LINGUA: 125K+ expert-annotated Hindi-English code-mixed instances in Devanagari and Roman scripts across five tasks; closed LLMs beat open models zero-shot, and fine-tuned LLMs reach 95.25 NER F1.
- `2025-02` **[Conditioning LLMs to Generate Code-Switched Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12924)** — Heredia et al. · *LREC 2025*<br>Fine-tunes LLMs on natural English-Spanish code-switched sentences paired with monolingual back-translations to generate code-switched text; traditional metrics do not correlate with human judgement, but LLM judges align better.
- `2025-01` **[Finding A Voice: Exploring the Potential of African American Dialect and Voice Generation for Chatbots](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1252/)** — Finch et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03441)]<br>Builds text and spoken chatbots with African American English and tests them with AAE speakers: text AAE chatbots often underperform, while spoken ones gain from an African American voice.
- `2025` **[Evaluating Large Language Models on Health-Related Claims Across Arabic Dialects](https://aclanthology.org/2025.abjadnlp-1.11/)** — Alharbi et al. · *ABJADNLP 2025 (workshop)*<br>Evaluates how LLMs, particularly GPT-4, handle health claims in Saudi, Egyptian, Lebanese and Moroccan Arabic with a Cultural Sensitivity Score; mixed and ambiguous claims are hardest.
- `2025` **[DialUp! Modeling the Language Continuum by Adapting Models to Dialects and Dialects to Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.989/)** — Bafna et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>DialUp: training-time adaptation of pretrained MT models with synthetic dialectal data plus inference-time adaptation of dialect input, improving translation for dialects from several language families.
- `2025` **[ConLoan: A Contrastive Multilingual Dataset for Evaluating Loanwords](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1453/)** — Ahmadi et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>ConLoan: contrastive sentences with and without loanwords in 10 languages; MT systems and language models systematically prefer loanwords over native terms, with performance varying by language.
- `2025` **[Benchmarking Open-Source Large Language Models on Code-Switched Tagalog-English Retrieval Augmented Generation](https://doi.org/10.12720/jait.16.2.233-242)** — Adoptante et al. · *Journal of Advances in Information Technology 2025*<br>Evaluates Mistral, SeaLLM, Falcon, Phi-3-mini and Gemma on code-switched Tagalog-English RAG; models do best on English, then Taglish, then Tagalog queries, with Mistral and SeaLLM strongest.
- `2025` **[Are Dialects Better Prompters? A Case Study on Arabic Subjective Text Classification](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.892/)** — Moudjari & Benamara · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>Evaluates 12 open LLMs on four Arabic subjective classification tasks and eight datasets; fine-tuned models with dialectal prompts in Arabic and Arabizi scripts perform best.
- `2025` **[AfroCS-xs: Creating a Compact, High-Quality, Human-Validated Code-Switched Dataset for African Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1601/)** — Olaleye et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>AfroCS-xs: small human-validated, LLM-generated code-switched agriculture dataset for Afrikaans, Sesotho, Yoruba, isiZulu and English; fine-tuning instruction-tuned LLMs on it improves code-switched translation.
- `2025` **[AL-QASIDA: Analyzing LLM Quality and Accuracy Systematically in Dialectal Arabic](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1137/)** — Robinson et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.04193)]<br>AL-QASIDA: evaluates nine LLMs on eight dialectal Arabic varieties for fidelity, understanding, quality and diglossia; models understand dialects better than they produce them, being reluctant to generate them.
- `2025` **[A Multi-Agent Framework for Mitigating Dialect Biases in Privacy Policy Question-Answering Systems](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1554/)** — Klisura et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>Multi-agent framework that rewrites dialectal English queries into Standard American English for privacy-policy QA, raising GPT-4o-mini zero-shot accuracy from 0.394 to 0.601 on PrivacyQA.
- `2024-10` **[LLM-based Code-Switched Text Generation for Grammatical Error Correction](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.942/)** — Potter & Yuan · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10349)]<br>Generates synthetic code-switched grammatical error correction data with LLMs and trains a model that corrects both monolingual and code-switched text from ESL learners.
- `2024-10` **[Can Code-Switched Texts Activate a Knowledge Switch in LLMs? A Case Study on English-Korean Code-Switching](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.1215/)** — Kim et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18436)]<br>EnKoQA: synthetic English-Korean code-switched QA dataset; code-switching activates knowledge in multilingual LLMs more faithfully than English, especially in language-specific domains.
- `2024-09` **[Predicting the Target Word of Game-playing Conversations using a Low-Rank Dialect Adapter for Decoder Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-short.2/)** — Srirag et al. · *NAACL 2025 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00358)]<br>LoRDD: combines task and contrastively trained dialect LoRA adapters in Mistral and Gemma, narrowing gaps between Indian or Nigerian English and American English on target word prediction.
- `2024-09` **[AraDiCE: Benchmarks for Dialectal and Cultural Capabilities in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.283/)** — Mousi et al. · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11404)]<br>AraDiCE: seven Arabic dialect and MSA datasets (about 45K post-edited samples) plus a Gulf, Egypt and Levant cultural benchmark; Jais and AceGPT beat multilingual models on dialect tasks.
- `2024-08` **[Leveraging LLM for Augmenting Textual Data in Code-Switching ASR: Arabic as an Example](https://doi.org/10.21437/syndata4genai.2024-6)** — Alharbi et al.<br>Uses LLMs to generate Arabic-English code-switched text for ASR training and introduces the Saudilang Code-switch Corpus; cuts perplexity by over 8% and WER by 5.5% relative.
- `2024-06` **[Evaluating Large Language Models along Dimensions of Language Variation: A Systematik Invesdigatiom uv Cross-lingual Generalization](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.1044/)** — Bafna et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13718)]<br>Models phonological, morphological and lexical distance as noise to create artificial languages, explaining LLM performance drops on unseen closely related languages and dialects.
- `2024-06` **[ArzEn-LLM: Code-Switched Egyptian Arabic-English Translation and Speech Recognition Using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18120)** — Heakl et al. · *CL 2024*<br>ArzEn-LLM: code-switched Egyptian Arabic-English MT with Llama and Gemma plus Whisper ASR, reporting 56% improvement in English translation and 9.3% in Arabic; models released.
- `2024-05` **[Evaluating Dialect Robustness of Language Models via Conversation Understanding](https://aclanthology.org/2025.sumeval-2.3/)** — Srirag et al. · *SUMEVAL 2025 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05688)]<br>M-MD3: target-word-masked taboo conversations in US and Indian English; Llama3, GPT-4 and GPT-3.5 perform significantly better on US English, though fine-tuning on dialectal data helps.
- `2024-05` **[Evaluating Code-Switching Translation with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.565/)** — Huzaifah et al. · *LREC-COLING 2024*<br>Benchmarks six LLMs on seven code-switching translation datasets; GPT-4 and GPT-3.5 are strong relative to supervised and commercial MT, and GPT-4 is robust across code-switching conditions.
- `2024-04` **[Does Generative AI speak Nigerian-Pidgin?: Issues about Representativeness and Bias for Multilingualism in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.85/)** — Adelani et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19442)]<br>Shows Nigerian Pidgin (Naija) and West African Pidgin English differ in word order and vocabulary, and generative AI operates only on the latter, leaving Naija underrepresented.
- `2024-03` **[DIALECTBENCH: A NLP Benchmark for Dialects, Varieties, and Closely-Related Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11009)** — FAISAL et al.<br>DIALECTBENCH: aggregates 10 text-level tasks covering 281 language varieties, showing performance disparities between standard and non-standard varieties.
- `2024-01` **[MedSumm: A Multimodal Approach to Summarizing Code-Mixed Hindi-English Clinical Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01596)** — Ghosh et al. · *European Conference on Information Retrieval 2024*<br>MMCQS: Hindi-English code-mixed medical queries paired with images, plus MedSumm, an LLM and VLM framework showing visual information improves medical question summaries.
- `2024` **[PhonATe: Impact of Type-Written Phonological Features of African American Language on Generative Language Modeling Tasks](https://openreview.net/forum?id=rXEwxmnGQs)** — Deas et al. · *COLM 2024*<br>PhonATe: synthetically adds type-written phonological features of African American Language to text, showing their impact on generative models; fine-tuning with them lowers perceived bias on downstream tasks.
- `2024` **[Can LLMs Handle Low-Resource Dialects? A Case Study on Translation and Common Sense Reasoning in Šariš](https://aclanthology.org/2024.vardial-1.11/)** — Ondrejová & Šuppa · *VARDIAL 2024 (workshop)*<br>Case study on Šariš, a Slovak dialect: uses LLMs zero-shot and for data augmentation in Slovak-Šariš translation and introduces ŠarišCOPA for commonsense reasoning.
- `2023-11` **[Task-Agnostic Low-Rank Adapters for Unseen English Dialects](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.487/)** — Xiao et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.00915)]<br>HyperLoRA: hypernetwork-generated LoRA adapters using expert linguistic knowledge to adapt LLMs to unseen English dialects task-agnostically; best or competitive across 5 dialects zero-shot.
- `2023-10` **[Representativeness as a Forgotten Lesson for Multilingual and Code-switched Data Collection and Preparation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.382/)** — Doğruöz et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.20470)]<br>Critical study of 68 code-switching datasets: most involve English and neglect location, socio-demographic and register variation; offers a checklist for representative data collection.
- `2023-10` **[Quantifying the Dialect Gap and its Correlates Across Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.481/)** — Kantharuban et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15135)]<br>Evaluates influential LLMs on MT and ASR across regional dialects of high- and low-resource languages, correlating the dialect gap with economic, social and linguistic factors.
- `2023-05` **[Multilingual Large Language Models Are Not (Yet) Code-Switchers](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.774/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14235)]<br>Benchmarks multilingual LLMs on code-switched sentiment analysis, MT, summarization and word-level language ID; zero- and few-shot LLMs underperform much smaller fine-tuned models.
- `2023-05` **[DADA: Dialect Adaptation via Dynamic Aggregation of Linguistic Rules](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.850/)** — Liu et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.13406)]<br>DADA: composes adapters for individual linguistic features to give SAE-trained models multi-dialectal English robustness, working for single-task and instruction-tuned models.
- `2023-03` **[CroCoSum: A Benchmark Dataset for Cross-Lingual Code-Switched Summarization](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.367/)** — Zhang & Eickhoff · *LREC-COLING 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.04092)]<br>CroCoSum: over 24,000 English technology news articles with 18,000 human-written Chinese summaries, over 92% containing code-switched phrases, for cross-lingual code-switched summarization.

[⬆ Back to top](#)

## Cross-lingual Knowledge, Factuality & Consistency

- `2026-09` **[Beyond Cross-Lingual Transfer: Benchmarking Propagation Boundaries in Multilingual LLM Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05976)** — Shao et al.<br>CLLPU: multilingual unlearning benchmark of 800 knowledge-unit pairs and 72,000 QA instances in ten languages; methods forget incompletely or over-propagate on Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct.
- `2026-09` **[$μ^2$-Bench: A Multilingual Machine Unlearning Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20945)** — Hwang et al.<br>μ2-Bench: multilingual machine unlearning benchmark covering memorization, unlearning and evaluation across many languages, including hold-out languages; finds successful unlearning needs methods reflecting multilingual characteristics.
- `2026-08` **[Latent-Space Intervention for Cross-Lingual Factual Consistency: Consistency Improvements without Accuracy Drops](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1206/)** — Ghorbanpour et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28860)]<br>Trains layer-specific autoencoders on parallel multilingual representations for inference-time latent intervention; improves cross-lingual factual consistency with English on open-ended and multiple-choice QA without hurting accuracy.
- `2026-07` **[Inference-Time Steering for Cross-Lingual Factual Consistency in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19243)** — Manev<br>Compares persona prompting, contrastive activation addition and DPO for making English-prompted Gemma 3 12B answer as if asked in German, Spanish or Bulgarian; persona prompting works best.
- `2026-07` **[Estimating Uncertainty from Reasoning: A Large-Scale Study of Multi- and Crosslingual MCQA Performance in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.865/)** — Alfarano et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06327)]<br>Evaluates nine uncertainty estimation methods across 22 languages; prompting models to reason in English on low-resource questions substantially improves estimation and closes the gap with high-resource languages.
- `2026-06` **[The Language Blind Spot: How Query Language and Brand Recognition Tier Shape AI-Constructed Brand Reputation Across Twelve European Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23165)** — Żatuchin<br>Queries three grounded LLMs about 66 brands in twelve European languages (35,640 responses); reputation is language-bound, and query language shifts brand recommendations far more than brand descriptions.
- `2026-06` **[Multilingual Unlearning in LLMs: Transfer, Dynamics, and Reversibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03291)** — Xiang et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>Extends TOFU to five languages; unlearning transfer is strongest between languages sharing scripts and families, and one steering direction recovers 50% (Qwen) and 90% (Gemma) of unlearned knowledge.
- `2026-06` **[Cross-Lingual Exploration for Parametric Knowledge](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.2443/)** — Diskind et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24579)]<br>Cross-lingual prompting strategies over 17 typologically diverse languages improve factual recall and cross-lingual consistency, giving a better compute Pareto frontier than native-language scaling.
- `2026-05` **[MultiHaluDet: Multilingual Hallucination Detection via LLM Hidden State Probing](https://aclanthology.org/2026.mellm-1.6/)** — Alvi et al. · *MELLM 2026 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24919)]<br>MultiHaluDet: stacks multi-layer hidden-state probes of frozen LLMs to detect hallucinations, reaching up to 98.55% AUROC on English HaluEval and TriviaQA and transferring to French, Bangla and Amharic.
- `2026-05` **[Evaluating Cross-lingual Knowledge Consistency in Code-Mixed vis-a-vis Indian Languages using IndicKLAR](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1655/)** — Mazumder et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29637)]<br>IndicKLAR: knowledge-consistency benchmark for 18 Indian languages with code-mixed variants for 11 pairs; native-vs-English gaps reach ~0.50, while code-mixed inputs come within ~0.05.
- `2026-03` **[Large Reasoning Models Struggle to Transfer Parametric Knowledge Across Scripts](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1387/)** — Bandarkar et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17070)]<br>Finds script match, not language or family, is the main predictor of cross-lingual knowledge transfer failure in reasoning LLMs on ECLeKTic and MultiLoKo; transliteration-reasoning SFT narrows it.
- `2026-02` **[Layer-Targeted Multilingual Knowledge Erasure in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22562)** — Li et al.<br>MUTE: restricts unlearning to intermediate language-agnostic layers found via CKA and LRDS; shallow interventions collapse held-out-language ability, deep ones fail to erase, giving robust multilingual erasure.
- `2026-02` **[Large language models show Dunning-Kruger-like effects in multilingual fact-checking](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-39046-w)** — Qazi et al. · *Scientific Reports 2026*<br>Evaluates nine LLMs on 5,000 professionally fact-checked claims in 47 languages; smaller models are overconfident and less accurate, with largest gaps for non-English and Global South claims.
- `2026-02` **[Halluverse-M^3: A multitask multilingual benchmark for hallucination in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06920)** — Abdaljalil et al.<br>Halluverse-M^3: hallucination detection dataset in English, Arabic, Hindi and Turkish for QA and dialogue summarization with entity-, relation- and sentence-level labels; detection is worst in Hindi.
- `2026-01` **[Multilingual Amnesia: On the Transferability of Unlearning in Multilingual LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.260/)** — Farashah et al. · *EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05641)]<br>Studies data and concept unlearning in Aya-Expanse 8B across ten languages: unlearning is more stable in high-resource languages, transfers asymmetrically, and tracks syntactic similarity.
- `2026-01` **[Evaluating Cross-Lingual Unlearning in Multilingual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06675)** — Lizzo & Heck<br>Tests unlearning algorithms on translated TOFU in seven language/script variants; most fail to remove facts outside the training language, while subspace projection achieves strong cross-lingual forgetting.
- `2026-01` **[Evaluating Contextually Mediated Factual Recall in Multilingual Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.aacl-short.7/)** — Liu et al. · *AACL 2026 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12555)]<br>Studies factual recall when the entity is introduced only through context, across five languages and several model families: contextual mediation consistently degrades recall, and larger models are more robust.
- `2026` **[Tracing Multilingual Knowledge Acquisition Dynamics in Domain Adaptation: A Case Study of Biomedical Adaptation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.269/)** — Zhao et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>AdaXEval: builds multiple-choice QA from the bilingual biomedical training corpus to trace knowledge acquisition in multilingual domain adaptation; finds cross-lingual transfer remains challenging.
- `2026` **[The Fractured Interlingua: Geometric Bottlenecks in Cross-Lingual Knowledge Editing](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Ps9Yqs4apx)** — Verma et al. · *NeurIPS 2026*<br>Decomposes the cross-lingual propagation gap of ROME, MEMIT and AlphaEdit edits into key-space and residual terms, finding a large orthogonal residual component consistently limits propagation.
- `2026` **[Poly-FEVER: A Multilingual Hallucination Detection Benchmark](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.540/)** — Zhang et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026*<br>Poly-FEVER: 77,973 claims aligned across 11 languages for hallucination detection; LLMs judge factuality better in high-resource languages like English and Chinese than in Amharic or Tamil.
- `2026` **[Multilingual and Cross-Lingual Citation Needed Detection on Wikipedia for Lower-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1291/)** — Quaremba et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>MCN: citation-needed detection corpus in 18 languages across three resource levels; fine-tuned small decoder models beat prompted LLMs, including in cross-lingual transfer from English.
- `2026` **[Do LLM hallucination detectors suffer from low-resource effect?](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.136/)** — Datta et al. · *EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16766)]<br>Finds hallucination detectors' accuracy drops several times less than task accuracy in low-resource languages across four LLMs, but detectors fail cross-lingually without in-language supervision.
- `2026` **[Cross-lingual Biography Enrichment via Claim Extraction and Alignment](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.441/)** — Song et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>CLAW-4L: 300 English Wikipedia biographies of women paired with French, Chinese or Azerbaijani versions, with claim annotations, for claim-based cross-lingual biography enrichment; lower-resource settings remain hard.
- `2025-12` **[FAME: Fictional Actors for Multilingual Erasure](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-200/)** — Savelli et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15235)]<br>FAME: synthetic unlearning benchmark of 1,000 fictional actor biographies and 20,000 QA pairs in English, French, German, Italian and Spanish, supporting entity- and instance-level forgetting.
- `2025-11` **[MUCH: A Multilingual Claim Hallucination Benchmark](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-176/)** — Dentan et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17081)]<br>MUCH: claim-level uncertainty quantification benchmark of 4,873 samples in English, French, Spanish and German from four open LLMs, releasing 24 logits per token and a deterministic claim segmenter.
- `2025-11` **[LiveCLKTBench: Towards Reliable Evaluation of Cross-Lingual Knowledge Transfer in Multilingual LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.694/)** — Guo et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.14774)]<br>LiveCLKTBench: pipeline of time-sensitive factual questions isolating cross-lingual knowledge transfer; across five languages, transfer follows linguistic distance, is often asymmetric, and gains diminish with scale.
- `2025-10` **[When Models Lie, We Learn: Multilingual Span-Level Hallucination Detection with PsiloQA](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.626/)** — Rykov et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.04849)]<br>PsiloQA: span-level hallucination dataset in 14 languages built automatically with GPT-4o; fine-tuned encoder models detect hallucinations best across languages.
- `2025-10` **[Rethinking Cross-lingual Gaps from a Statistical Viewpoint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15551)** — Piratla et al.<br>Attributes cross-lingual knowledge gaps to higher response variance in the target language; test-time ensembling that reduces variance improves source-target transfer by up to 12 absolute points.
- `2025-10` **[On the Entity-Level Alignment in Crosslingual Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.10280)** — Liu et al.<br>Shows cross-lingual factual consistency correlates strongly with entity alignment; adding English translations of subjects to prompts improves recall and consistency via internal pivot-language processing.
- `2025-10` **[Multilingual Knowledge Graph Completion via Efficient Multilingual Knowledge Sharing](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.577/)** — Mao et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07736)]<br>KL-GMoE with iterative entity reranking for LLM-based multilingual knowledge graph completion, plus a 5-language mKG dataset; improves Hits@1 by 5.47% over prior methods.
- `2025-10` **[Confabulations from ACL Publications (CAP): A Dataset for Scientific Hallucination Detection](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-197/)** — Gamba et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22395)]<br>CAP: scientific hallucination dataset of 900 questions and over 7000 answers from 16 LLMs in five high-resource and four low-resource languages, labeled for factuality and fluency.
- `2025-10` **[Beyond the Final Layer: Intermediate Representations for Better Multilingual Calibration in Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.958/)** — Zhou et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.03136)]<br>Studies calibration across six model families and over 100 languages: non-English languages are worse calibrated, late-intermediate layers give better signals, and training-free LACE ensembles layers per language.
- `2025-07` **[CCL-XCoT: An Efficient Cross-Lingual Knowledge Transfer Method for Mitigating Hallucination Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.93/)** — Zheng et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.14239)]<br>CCL-XCoT: curriculum contrastive continued pretraining plus cross-lingual chain-of-thought instruction tuning, reducing hallucination rates by up to 62% and improving factual knowledge transfer across language pairs.
- `2025-07` **[Are Knowledge and Reference in Multilingual Language Models Cross-Lingually Consistent?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.267/)** — Ai et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.12838)]<br>Uses code-mixed coreferential statements to analyze cross-lingual consistency of factual knowledge, finding a bottleneck at a particular layer; code-switching training and cross-lingual word alignment objectives help most.
- `2025-06` **[CausalAbstain: Enhancing Multilingual LLMs with Causal Reasoning for Trustworthy Abstention](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.723/)** — Sun et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.00519)]<br>CausalAbstain: causal method for choosing which self-generated multilingual feedback to use when deciding to abstain, improving abstention in native-language and multilingual settings on two benchmarks.
- `2025-05` **[Tracing Multilingual Factual Knowledge Acquisition in Pretraining](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.113/)** — Liu et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14824)]<br>Traces factual recall and crosslingual consistency through OLMo-7B pretraining; both improve with fact frequency, and some low-frequency non-English facts benefit from crosslingual transfer from English.
- `2025-05` **[Shared Path: Unraveling Memorization in Multilingual LLMs through Language Similarities](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.978/)** — Luo et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15722)]<br>Studies memorization in multilingual LLMs across 95 languages with a graph-based metric using language similarity; among similar languages, those with fewer training tokens memorize more.
- `2025-05` **[OWL: Probing Cross-Lingual Recall of Memorized Texts via World Literature](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1314/)** — Srivastava et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22945)]<br>OWL: 31.5K aligned excerpts from 20 books in ten languages; LLMs recall memorized content across languages, with GPT-4o naming title and author 69% of the time on new translations.
- `2025-05` **[MultiHal: Multilingual Dataset for Knowledge-Graph Grounded Evaluation of LLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14101)** — Lavrinovics et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>MultiHal: multilingual multihop hallucination benchmark with 25.9k curated knowledge-graph paths (from 140k mined); KG-RAG beats vanilla QA across languages and models.
- `2025-04` **[Analyzing LLMs’ Knowledge Boundary Cognition Across Languages Through the Lens of Internal Representations](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1174/)** — Xiao et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13816)]<br>Probes LLM representations for knowledge-boundary perception across languages: encoded in middle to middle-upper layers with linear language differences; proposes training-free cross-lingual transfer and an evaluation suite.
- `2025-03` **[Poly-FEVER: A Multilingual Fact Verification Benchmark for Hallucination Detection in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16541)** — Zhang et al.<br>Poly-FEVER: 77,973 labeled factual claims in 11 languages for multilingual hallucination detection; topic distribution and web resource availability shape hallucination frequency across languages.
- `2025-03` **[Large Language Models for Multilingual Previously Fact-Checked Claim Detection](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.852/)** — Vykopal et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02737)]<br>Evaluates seven LLMs on previously fact-checked claim detection across 20 languages; they struggle on low-resource languages, where translating texts into English helps.
- `2025-03` **[HalluVerse25: Fine-grained Multilingual Benchmark Dataset for LLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07833)** — Abdaljalil et al.<br>HalluVerse25: fine-grained hallucination dataset in English, Arabic and Turkish built by LLM-injected errors in biographical sentences plus human annotation, used to test LLM hallucination detection.
- `2025-02` **[Truth Knows No Language: Evaluating Truthfulness Beyond English](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1507/)** — Figueras et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09387)]<br>Professionally translated TruthfulQA for Basque, Catalan, Galician and Spanish; 12 open LLMs score best in English and worst in Basque, but cross-language gaps are smaller than expected.
- `2025-02` **[Mass-Editing Memory with Attention in Transformers: A cross-lingual exploration of knowledge](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.347/)** — Tamayo et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02173)]<br>Examines knowledge editing across languages and the role of attention, proposing MEMAT, which gives a 10% gain in magnitude metrics and benefits languages absent from training data.
- `2025-02` **[Language Models' Factuality Depends on the Language of Inquiry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17955)** — Aggarwal et al.<br>Benchmark of 10,000 country-related facts in 13 languages with three recall and transferability metrics; LMs often know a fact in one language but fail to transfer it across languages.
- `2025-02` **[Edit Once, Update Everywhere: A Simple Framework for Cross-Lingual Knowledge Synchronization in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1196/)** — Wu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14645)]<br>X-KDE: cross-lingual edit instruction tuning plus target-language preference optimization propagates knowledge edits across languages, improving Bi-ZsRE and MzsRE results by +8.19% on average.
- `2025-02` **[ECLeKTic: a Novel Challenge Set for Evaluation of Cross-Lingual Knowledge Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.21228)** — Goldman et al.<br>ECLeKTic: closed-book QA set in 12 languages built from facts present in only one language's Wikipedia; 8 LLMs struggle to share knowledge across languages.
- `2025-02` **[Beyond Translation: LLM-Based Data Generation for Multilingual Fact-Checking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15419)** — Chung et al.<br>MultiSynFact: LLM-generated multilingual fact-checking dataset of 2.2M claim-source pairs for Spanish, German, English and other languages, built from Wikipedia with claim validation.
- `2025-01` **[TiEBe: Tracking Language Model Recall of Notable Worldwide Events Through Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07482)** — Almeida et al.<br>TiEBe: over 23,000 QA pairs on notable events across 10+ years, 23 regions and 13 languages; factual recall shows geographic disparities correlating above 0.7 with indicators like HDI.
- `2025` **[The Linguistic Connectivities Within Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.456/)** — Wang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>Studies LLMs across 27 languages and finds a linguistic map tied to resource level and language family; isolated or low-resource languages show less knowledge consistency and remain marginalized.
- `2025` **[Mitigating Negative Interference in Multilingual Knowledge Editing through Null-Space Constraints](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.460/)** — Sun et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>LangEdit: null-space constrained multilingual knowledge editing that projects each language's updates onto the orthogonal complement of other languages' subspaces, reducing interference across three architectures and six languages.
- `2025` **[How Much Do LLMs Hallucinate across Languages? On Realistic Multilingual Estimation of LLM Hallucination](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1481/)** — Islam et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12769)]<br>Estimates long-form QA hallucination across 30 languages and 6 LLM families with a translate-trained detector; in absolute terms LLMs hallucinate more tokens in high-resource languages because responses are longer.
- `2025` **[BabelEdits: A Benchmark and a Modular Approach for Robust Cross-lingual Knowledge Editing of Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.438/)** — Green et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>BabelEdits: cross-lingual knowledge editing benchmark covering 60 languages with entity aliases, plus BabelReFT, a modular representation-finetuning editor more robust to downstream collapse.
- `2025` **[BMIKE-53: Investigating Cross-Lingual Knowledge Editing with In-Context Learning](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.798/)** — Nie et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>BMIKE-53: cross-lingual in-context knowledge editing benchmark across 53 languages from zsRE, CounterFact and WikiFactDiff; scale and demonstration alignment matter, and non-Latin-script languages underperform amid language confusion.
- `2024-08` **[Beneath the Surface of Consistency: Exploring Cross-lingual Knowledge Representation Sharing in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.475/)** — Ifergan et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10646)]<br>Repurposes knowledge editing to measure cross-lingual sharing of factual representations; high consistency need not imply sharing, script similarity dominates, and full sharing could raise accuracy up to 150%.
- `2024-06` **[Teaching LLMs to Abstain across Languages via Multilingual Feedback](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.239/)** — Feng et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15948)]<br>Shows English abstention methods leave up to 20.5% gaps for low-resource languages; self-feedback in related languages improves LLM abstention by up to 9.2% for low-resource languages.
- `2024-06` **[Multilingual Knowledge Editing with Language-Agnostic Factual Neurons](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.385/)** — Zhang et al. · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16416)]<br>Finds that the same fact in different languages generally activates shared language-agnostic factual neurons and edits them (LU-LAFNs) for multilingual knowledge editing, evaluated on Bi-ZsRE and MzsRE.
- `2024-06` **[Crosslingual Capabilities and Knowledge Barriers in Multilingual Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16135)** — Chua et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>Finds LLMs show surface crosslingual ability in MT and embeddings but a crosslingual knowledge barrier on MMLU, a Harry Potter quiz and TOFU; mixed-language fine-tuning reduces the gap.
- `2024-06` **[1+1>2: Can Large Language Models Serve as Cross-Lingual Knowledge Aggregators?](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.743/)** — Huang et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14721)]<br>Aggregates knowledge across languages with a low-resource knowledge detector, language selection and answer replacement, improving LLM multilingual performance and reducing cross-language disparity.
- `2024-04` **[Multilingual Pretraining and Instruction Tuning Improve Cross-Lingual Knowledge Alignment, But Only Shallowly](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-long.339/)** — Gao et al. · *NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04659)]<br>CLiKA: assesses cross-lingual knowledge alignment at performance, consistency and conductivity levels; multilingual pretraining and instruction tuning help but do not substantially improve conductivity, which stays poor for all tested LLMs.
- `2024-04` **[MLaKE: Multilingual Knowledge Editing Benchmark for Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.301/)** — Wei et al. · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04990)]<br>MLaKE: knowledge editing benchmark of 4072 multi-hop and 5360 single-hop questions in five languages; editing methods succeed more in English and generalize poorly across languages.
- `2024-02` **[Multi-FAct: Assessing Factuality of Multilingual LLMs using FActScore](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18045)** — Shafayat et al. · *COLM 2024*<br>Multi-FAct: applies FActScore to evaluate long-form factuality of multilingual LLMs across languages and regionally diverse topics, including running the pipeline on non-English Wikipedia.
- `2024-02` **[MlingConf: A Comprehensive Study of Multilingual Confidence Estimation on Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.129/)** — Xue et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13606)]<br>MlingConf: multilingual LLM confidence-estimation benchmark with language-agnostic and language-specific tasks; English dominates on agnostic tasks, and native-tone prompting improves language-specific ones.
- `2024-02` **[Comparing Hallucination Detection Metrics for Multilingual Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10496)** — Kang et al.<br>Compares lexical and NLI-based hallucination detection metrics on generated biographical summaries across languages: NLI metrics track human judgments in many settings but fail for lower-resource languages.
- `2024-01` **[Cross-lingual Editing in Multilingual Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-eacl.140/)** — Beniwal et al. · *Findings of EACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.10521)]<br>Introduces cross-lingual model editing (XME), editing a fact in one language and testing propagation in BLOOM, mBERT and XLM-R; methods struggle across Latin and Indic scripts.
- `2024` **[Cross-Lingual Multi-Hop Knowledge Editing](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.701/)** — Khandelwal et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024*<br>CroLin-MQuAKE: parallel benchmark for cross-lingual multi-hop knowledge editing showing large gaps versus English-centric settings; proposed CLeVer-CKE gains up to 30% across three LLMs and eight languages.
- `2023-12` **[Retrieval-Augmented Multilingual Knowledge Editing](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.21/)** — Wang et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.13040)]<br>ReMaKE: retrieval-augmented multilingual knowledge editing that adds knowledge retrieved from a multilingual base to prompts, outperforming baselines; releases the MzsRE dataset in 12 languages.
- `2023-11` **[Language Representation Projection: Can We Transfer Factual Knowledge across Languages in Multilingual Language Models?](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.226/)** — Xu et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.03788)]<br>LRP2: two parameter-free modules that project non-English representations to English-like ones and back, improving mLAMA factual knowledge retrieval across non-English languages.
- `2023-10` **[Cross-Lingual Consistency of Factual Knowledge in Multilingual Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.658/)** — Qi et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10378)]<br>Proposes the RankC metric for cross-lingual consistency of factual knowledge; larger models probe more facts but are not more consistent, and edited facts transfer only to high-RankC languages.
- `2023-09` **[Cross-Lingual Knowledge Editing in Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.627/)** — Wang et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.08952)]<br>Translates ZsRE into Chinese to study cross-lingual knowledge editing, editing LLMs in English and evaluating in Chinese and vice versa for reliability, generality, locality and portability.
- `2023-08` **[DocTER: Evaluating Document-based Knowledge Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.09954)** — Wu et al. · *Information Processing & Management 2023*<br>DocTER: document-based knowledge editing benchmark measuring edit success, locality, reasoning and cross-lingual transfer; the best in-context method trails gold triples by 10 points in editing success.
- `2023-05` **[This Land is Your, My Land: Evaluating Geopolitical Bias in Language Models through Territorial Disputes](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-long.213/)** — Li et al. · *NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14610)]<br>BorderLines: 251 disputed territories with multiple-choice questions in each claimant's language (49 languages), showing LLMs answer geopolitical questions inconsistently across languages.
- `2023-05` **[Separating form and meaning: Using self-consistency to quantify task understanding across multiple senses](https://aclanthology.org/2023.gem-1.22/)** — Ohmer et al. · *GEM 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11662)]<br>Proposes measuring LLM understanding by consistency across senses, instantiated as multilingual self-consistency of ChatGPT on two tasks in three languages.
- `2023` **[Polyglot or Not? Measuring Multilingual Encyclopedic Knowledge in Foundation Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.691/)** — Schott et al. · *EMNLP 2023*<br>Polyglot or Not: 20-language dataset of 303k factual associations with counterfactuals; LLaMA scores highest but recalls facts poorly in non-English languages, showing foundation models are far from polyglots.
- `2023` **[Detecting and Mitigating Hallucinations in Multilingual Summarisation](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.551/)** — Qiu et al. · *EMNLP 2023*<br>mFACT: faithfulness metric for non-English summaries built by translation from English metrics; finds multilingual LLMs hallucinate often outside English and proposes faithfulness-weighted training loss.
- `2023` **[Confidently Wrong: Exploring the Calibration and Expression of (Un)Certainty of Large Language Models in a Multilingual Setting](https://aclanthology.org/2023.mmnlg-1.1/)** — Krause et al. · *MMNLG 2023 (workshop)*<br>Uses multilingual closed-book QA with GPT-3.5 to show accuracy drops in lower-resource languages and expressed confidence is exaggerated, not matching correctness or likelihood.

## Multilingual Retrieval, Embeddings & RAG

- `2026-03` **[Omnilingual SONAR: Cross-Lingual and Cross-Modal Sentence Embeddings Bridging Massively Multilingual Text and Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16606)** — Janeiro et al.<br>OmniSONAR: sentence embeddings for text, speech, code and math across thousands of language varieties; halves similarity-search error on 200-language FLORES and maps 177 spoken languages.
- `2026-03` **[Learning Retrieval Models with Sparse Autoencoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13277)** — Formal et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>SPLARE: learned sparse retrieval built on LLM sparse-autoencoder features; SPLARE-7B produces language-agnostic sparse embeddings and achieves top results on MMTEB multilingual and English retrieval.
- `2026-03` **[F2LLM-v2: Inclusive, Performant, and Efficient Embeddings for a Multilingual World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.19223)** — Zhang et al.<br>F2LLM-v2: open multilingual LLM-based embedding models in 8 sizes (80M-14B) for 200+ languages, trained on 60 million samples; the 14B model ranks first on 11 MTEB benchmarks.
- `2026-02` **[LEMUR: A Corpus for Robust Fine-Tuning of Multilingual Law Embedding Models for Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-srw.18/)** — Ahmadi et al. · *EACL 2026 (SRW)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09570)]<br>LEMUR: multilingual corpus of EU environmental legislation from 24,953 EUR-Lex PDFs in 25 languages, used to contrastively fine-tune three multilingual embedding models for legal retrieval.
- `2026-01` **[The Overlooked Role of Graded Relevance Thresholds in Multilingual Dense Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.382/)** — Wullach et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04395)]<br>Shows the optimal threshold for binarizing LLM-annotated graded relevance in multilingual dense retrieval fine-tuning varies across languages and tasks, often with resource level, and affects effectiveness and data needs.
- `2026-01` **[Enhancing Multilingual RAG Systems with Debiased Language Preference-Guided Query Fusion](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1353/)** — Park et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02956)]<br>Shows English preference in multilingual RAG is distorted by exposure, gold-availability and cultural priors in benchmarks, and introduces DELTA, which beats English pivoting via query-document monolingual alignment.
- `2026` **[When Does Mixing Help? Analyzing Query Embedding Interpolation in Multilingual Dense Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1455/)** — Zhu et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>Interpolating embeddings of parallel query translations in BGE-M3 on mMARCO beats the best monolingual query in 88/105 cases; pure English queries are best for indices containing English.
- `2026` **[The Language of Evidence: Robustness and Bias in Code-Switched RAG](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1830/)** — Hira et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Studies multilingual retrieval and RAG under code-switched queries; top retrievers prefer English evidence, and Language-Targeted Contrastive Training reduces this bias at a cost to cross-language coverage.
- `2026` **[MIMO: Multilingual Information Retrieval via Monolingual Objectives](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.156/)** — Jang et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>MIMO: two-stage embedding training anchored on an English semantic space, combining distillation with cross-lingual contrastive learning, outperforming cross-lingual training baselines on multilingual retrieval benchmarks.
- `2026` **[MILCO: Learned Sparse Retrieval Across Languages via a Multilingual Connector](https://openreview.net/forum?id=Z6dVYEqurT)** — Nguyen et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>MILCO: learned sparse retrieval mapping multilingual queries and documents into a shared English lexical space with a LexEcho source-language head, outperforming BGE-M3 and Qwen3-Embed on multilingual benchmarks.
- `2026` **[Language-Coupled Reinforcement Learning for Multilingual Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.700/)** — Qi et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>LcRL: multilingual search-augmented RL with language-coupled GRPO group sampling and an anti-consistency reward penalty to reduce knowledge bias and conflict in multilingual RAG.
- `2026` **[Improving Semantic Proximity in Information Retrieval through Cross-Lingual Alignment](https://openreview.net/forum?id=NvKvW5k6Kk)** — Hong et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>Shows multilingual retrievers favor unrelated English documents over relevant same-language documents in mixed pools, and introduces scenarios and metrics for cross-lingual alignment in retrieval.
- `2026` **[CORAL: Adaptive Retrieval Loop for Culturally-Aligned Multilingual RAG](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1356/)** — Lee et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>CORAL: agentic multilingual RAG loop that reselects corpora and rewrites queries for culturally grounded questions, improving accuracy up to 3.58%p on low-resource languages.
- `2026` **[CLEAR: Cross-Lingual Enhancement in Retrieval via Reverse-training](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.13/)** — Lee et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>CLEAR: reverse-training loss that uses English passages as a bridge for multilingual embedding models, improving cross-lingual retrieval by up to 15%, especially for low-resource languages.
- `2026` **[Breaking Language Preference in Multilingual RAG via Language-Controllable Retrieval and Language-Agnostic Reasoning](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.374/)** — Huo et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>Splits multilingual RAG into language-controllable retrieval and language-agnostic reasoning with cross-lingual consistency, reducing language preference bias without modifying existing retrievers or translators.
- `2026` **[All Languages Matter: Understanding and Mitigating Language Bias in Multilingual RAG](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.338/)** — Wang et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20199)]<br>Shows multilingual RAG rerankers favor English and the query language, suppressing answer-critical evidence in other languages; LAURA, a language-agnostic utility-driven reranker alignment, mitigates this.
- `2025-11` **[Llama-Embed-Nemotron-8B: A Universal Text Embedding Model for Multilingual and Cross-Lingual Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07025)** — Babakhin et al.<br>llama-embed-nemotron-8b: open-weights embedding model topping MMTEB as of October 21, 2025, trained on 16.1 million query-document pairs including 8.4 million LLM-generated examples.
- `2025-10` **[AfriMTEB and AfriE5: Benchmarking and Adapting Text Embedding Models for African Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.171/)** — Uemura et al. · *EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23896)]<br>AfriMTEB: MMTEB expansion covering 59 languages, 14 tasks and 38 datasets (six new); AfriE5 adapts mE5 to African languages via cross-lingual contrastive distillation, beating Gemini-Embeddings.
- `2025-09` **[MTEB-NL and E5-NL: Embedding Benchmark and Models for Dutch](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1236/)** — Banar et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.12340)]<br>MTEB-NL: Dutch embedding benchmark of existing and new datasets, plus a training set with LLM-generated synthetic data and compact E5-NL embedding models.
- `2025-09` **[Linguistic Nepotism: Trading-off Quality for Language Preference in Multilingual RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13930)** — Ki et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>Uses model internals to show multilingual RAG models across eight languages prefer citing English sources for English queries, sometimes trading document relevance for language preference.
- `2025-09` **[Evaluating Large Language Models for Cross-Lingual Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.612/)** — Zuo et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14749)]<br>Evaluates LLM rerankers in two-stage cross-lingual retrieval; multilingual bi-encoder first stages help, translation benefits shrink with stronger rerankers, and rerankers fall short without MT.
- `2025-06` **[IndicRAGSuite: Large-Scale Datasets and a Benchmark for Indian Language RAG Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01615)** — Prasanjith et al.<br>IndicRAGSuite: IndicMSMarco, 1,000 manually translated MS MARCO queries in 13 Indian languages for retrieval and generation, plus LLM-generated training data from Wikipedias of 19 Indian languages.
- `2025-05` **[XRAG: Cross-lingual Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.849/)** — Liu et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10089)]<br>XRAG: cross-lingual RAG benchmark from recent news; five LLMs struggle with response language under monolingual retrieval and with reasoning across languages under multilingual retrieval.
- `2025-05` **[Multilingual vs Crosslingual Retrieval of Fact-Checked Claims: A Tale of Two Approaches](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1480/)** — Ramponi et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22118)]<br>Compares multilingual and crosslingual retrieval of fact-checked claims across 47 languages (283 combinations); LLM-based re-ranking works best, and crosslingual retrieval behaves distinctly from multilingual.
- `2025-05` **[MIRACL-VISION: A Large, multilingual, visual document retrieval benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11651)** — Osmulski et al.<br>MIRACL-VISION: visual document retrieval benchmark in 18 languages extending MIRACL, with easy negatives removed; VLM-based embedding models show a gap in multilingual capability.
- `2025-04` **[On the Consistency of Multilingual Context Utilization in Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.mrl-main.15/)** — Qi et al. · *MRL 2025 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00597)]<br>Tests whether LLMs in multilingual RAG use relevant passages consistently regardless of passage language, respond in the expected language, and focus on the relevant passage.
- `2025-04` **[Multilingual Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Knowledge-Intensive Question Answering Task](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.35/)** — Ranaldi et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.03616)]<br>Compares multilingual RAG strategies for open-domain QA and proposes CrossRAG, which translates retrieved documents into one language before generation, helping both high- and low-resource languages.
- `2025-04` **[Improving Multilingual Retrieval-Augmented Language Models through Dialectic Reasoning Argumentations](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.461/)** — Ranaldi et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04771)]<br>Dialectic-RAG (DRAG): argumentative explanations that compare and resolve conflicting multilingual retrieved documents, used in-context or to build demonstrations for smaller models, improving RAG robustness.
- `2025-03` **[Gemini Embedding: Generalizable Embeddings from Gemini](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.07891)** — Lee et al.<br>Gemini Embedding: embedding model built on Gemini that leads MMTEB, over one hundred tasks across 250+ languages, on its multilingual, English and code benchmarks.
- `2025-02` **[mmE5: Improving Multimodal Multilingual Embeddings via High-quality Synthetic Data](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.433/)** — Chen et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08468)]<br>mmE5: multimodal multilingual embedding model trained on MLLM-synthesized data spanning tasks, modalities and languages; tops the MMEB Benchmark and performs strongly on multilingual XTD.
- `2025-02` **[WebFAQ: A Multilingual Collection of Natural Q&A Datasets for Dense Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20936)** — Dinzinger et al.<br>WebFAQ: 96 million natural QA pairs in 75 languages from schema.org FAQ markup, with 49 monolingual retrieval benchmarks and mined bilingual corpora.
- `2025-02` **[MMTEB: Massive Multilingual Text Embedding Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13595)** — Enevoldsen et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>MMTEB: community-driven expansion of MTEB with over 500 evaluation tasks across 250+ languages; the best public model is 560M multilingual-e5-large-instruct, not billion-parameter LLMs.
- `2025-02` **[Investigating Language Preference of Multilingual RAG Systems](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.295/)** — Park & Lee · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11175)]<br>Finds mRAG retrievers prefer high-resource and query languages and generators prefer the query language or Latin scripts; proposes DKM-RAG fusing translated passages with model knowledge.
- `2025-02` **[Examining Multilingual Embedding Models Cross-Lingually Through LLM-Generated Adversarial Examples](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.115/)** — Michail et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08638)]<br>CLSD: evaluates cross-lingual embedding models by ranking true parallel sentences above LLM-generated adversarial distractors; on German-French news, retrieval models benefit from pivoting through English.
- `2025-01` **[mFollowIR: a Multilingual Benchmark for Instruction Following in Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.19264)** — Weller et al. · *European Conference on Information Retrieval 2025*<br>mFollowIR: instruction-following retrieval benchmark built on TREC NeuCLIR narratives in Russian, Chinese and Persian; English instruction-trained retrievers transfer cross-lingually but drop in the multilingual setting.
- `2025` **[Multilingual Generative Retrieval via Cross-lingual Semantic Compression](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.575/)** — Huang et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025*<br>MGR-CSC: multilingual generative retrieval mapping equivalent multilingual keywords to shared identifiers, improving accuracy 6.83% on mMarco100k while cutting identifier length 74.51%.
- `2025` **[MaGiX: A Multi-Granular Adaptive Graph Intelligence Framework for Enhancing Cross-Lingual RAG](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.279/)** — Hieu et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025*<br>MaGiX: graph-based RAG for English–Vietnamese cross-lingual QA using a multi-granular cross-lingual knowledge graph and contrastively trained multilingual embeddings; outperforms prior graph RAG systems.
- `2025` **[MERIT: Multilingual Semantic Retrieval with Interleaved Multi-Condition Query](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ctsLGmMnCT)** — Chow et al. · *NeurIPS 2025*<br>MERIT: multilingual interleaved multi-condition semantic retrieval dataset of 320,000 queries over 135,000 products in 5 languages; the Coral fine-tuning framework improves MLLM retrievers by 45.9%.
- `2025` **[Few-Shot Multilingual Open-Domain QA from Five Examples](https://aclanthology.org/2025.tacl-1.24/)** — Jiang et al. · *TACL 2025*<br>FsModQA: pre-trains on WikiData then trains on multilingual QA data synthesized by few-shot prompting LLMs, outperforming baselines on multilingual open-domain QA and retrieval.
- `2025` **[Arctic-Embed 2.0: Multilingual Retrieval Without Compromise](https://openreview.net/forum?id=H6so82c2Sw)** — Yu et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>Arctic-Embed 2.0: open multilingual text embedding models with competitive multilingual and English retrieval and Matryoshka support, analyzing cross-lingual transfer and the English performance gap.
- `2024-12` **[jina-clip-v2: Multilingual Multimodal Embeddings for Text and Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08802)** — Koukounas et al.<br>jina-clip-v2: contrastive text-image embedding model trained with multilingual texts from 29 non-English languages and visually rich documents, improving text-only and crossmodal retrieval.
- `2024-11` **[Swan and ArabicMTEB: Dialect-Aware, Arabic-Centric, Cross-Lingual, and Cross-Cultural Embedding Models and Benchmarks](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.263/)** — Bhatia et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01192)]<br>Swan: Arabic-centric embedding models (Swan-Small on ARBERTv2, Swan-Large on ArMistral) plus ArabicMTEB, 94 datasets over eight tasks covering dialects, domains and cultures.
- `2024-10` **[Multilingual Retrieval Augmented Generation for Culturally-Sensitive Tasks: A Benchmark for Cross-lingual Robustness](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.219/)** — Li et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01171)]<br>BordIRLines: territorial-dispute queries with retrieved Wikipedia documents in 49 languages; retrieving multilingual documents improves response consistency and reduces geopolitical bias versus in-language RAG.
- `2024-10` **[MIRAGE-Bench: Automatic Multilingual Benchmark Arena for Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.14/)** — Thakur et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13716)]<br>MIRAGE-Bench: arena-based multilingual RAG benchmark over Wikipedia in 18 languages with a surrogate judge; benchmarks 19 multilingual LLMs, correlating at Kendall Tau 0.909 with GPT-4o.
- `2024-10` **[CLaMP 2: Multimodal Music Information Retrieval Across 101 Languages Using Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.27/)** — Wu et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13267)]<br>CLaMP 2: music information retrieval across 101 languages, pretrained on 1.5 million ABC-MIDI-text triplets, using LLMs to refine multilingual descriptions and balance language distribution.
- `2024-09` **[Multimodal LLM Enhanced Cross-lingual Cross-modal Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19961)** — Wang et al. · *ACM Multimedia 2024*<br>LECCR: uses a multimodal LLM to generate detailed visual descriptions, aggregated into semantic slots, to improve alignment between visual and non-English representations for cross-lingual cross-modal retrieval.
- `2024-08` **[Exploring Retrieval Augmented Generation in Arabic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07425)** — El-Beltagy & Ahmed · *CL 2024*<br>Arabic RAG case study comparing semantic embedding models and LLMs, including dialect mismatch between documents and queries; existing models can build effective Arabic RAG pipelines.
- `2024-07` **[mGTE: Generalized Long-Context Text Representation and Reranking Models for Multilingual Text Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-industry.103/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2024 (industry)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19669)]<br>mGTE: long-context (8192-token) multilingual text encoder, embedding model and reranker for retrieval, matching large BGE-M3 models and doing better on long-context retrieval.
- `2024-07` **[Steering Large Language Models for Cross-lingual Information Retrieval](https://doi.org/10.1145/3626772.3657819)** — Guo et al.<br>ASMR: combines multilingual dense passage retrieval with an activation-steered LLM reader for cross-lingual information retrieval, evaluated on XOR-TyDi QA and MKQA.
- `2024-07` **[Retrieval-augmented generation in multilingual settings](https://aclanthology.org/2024.knowllm-1.15/)** — Chirkova et al. · *KNOWLLM 2024 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01463)]<br>Builds a multilingual RAG baseline with queries and datastore in 13 languages; task-specific prompting is needed for generation in user languages and metrics must handle named-entity spelling variation.
- `2024-06` **[XL-HeadTags: Leveraging Multimodal Retrieval Augmentation for the Multilingual Generation of News Headlines and Tags](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.771/)** — Shohan et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03776)]<br>XL-HeadTags: news headline and tag generation dataset in 20 languages from 6 families, with plug-and-play multimodal-multilingual retrievers that use images and captions to guide instruction-tuned models.
- `2024-06` **[MINERS: Multilingual Language Models as Semantic Retrievers](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.155/)** — Winata et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07424)]<br>MINERS: benchmark of semantic retrieval with multilingual LMs, including bitext mining and retrieval-based classification, across over 200 languages with cross-lingual and code-switching settings.
- `2024-03` **[Unsupervised Multilingual Dense Retrieval via Generative Pseudo Labeling](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-eacl.49/)** — Huang et al. · *Findings of EACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03516)]<br>UMR: unsupervised multilingual dense retriever trained on pseudo labels from multilingual LM sequence likelihoods in an iterative two-stage framework, outperforming supervised baselines without paired data.
- `2024-03` **[Multilingual Sentence-T5: Scalable Sentence Encoders for Multilingual Applications](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.1034/)** — Yano et al. · *LREC-COLING 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.17528)]<br>m-ST5: NLI-based multilingual sentence encoder scaled to 5.7 billion parameters with LoRA; larger models help most for low-resource languages and those less similar to English.
- `2024-02` **[Pre-training Cross-lingual Open Domain Question Answering with Large-scale Synthetic Supervision](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.770/)** — Jiang et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16508)]<br>CLASS: trains a single encoder-decoder model for cross-lingual open-domain QA using synthetic supervision from Wikipedia cross-lingual links, outperforming comparable methods including MT-based ones.
- `2024-02` **[Multilingual E5 Text Embeddings: A Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05672)** — Wang et al.<br>Multilingual E5: small, base and large embedding models trained with contrastive pretraining on 1 billion multilingual text pairs plus fine-tuning, and an instruction-tuned variant.
- `2024-02` **[M3-Embedding: Multi-Linguality, Multi-Functionality, Multi-Granularity Text Embeddings Through Self-Knowledge Distillation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.137/)** — Chen et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03216)]<br>M3-Embedding: embedding model supporting retrieval in more than 100 languages with dense, multi-vector and sparse modes and inputs up to 8,192 tokens, trained with self-knowledge distillation.
- `2024` **[Enhancing Cross-lingual Sentence Embedding for Low-resource Languages with Word Alignment](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.204/)** — Miao et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2024*<br>Aligns words between English and eight low-resource languages with word-alignment objectives to improve cross-lingual sentence embeddings, yielding large bitext retrieval gains for low-resource languages.
- `2023-12` **[“Knowing When You Don’t Know”: A Multilingual Relevance Assessment Dataset for Robust Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.730/)** — Thakur et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.11361)]<br>NoMIRACL: human-annotated relevance assessment dataset for RAG robustness in 18 typologically diverse languages; LLAMA-2 and Orca-2 exceed 88% hallucination rate on non-relevant passages.
- `2023-12` **[Zero-Shot Cross-Lingual Reranking with Large Language Models for Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-short.59/)** — Adeyemi et al. · *ACL 2024 (short)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.16159)]<br>Studies LLM rerankers (RankGPT-4, RankGPT-3.5, RankZephyr) for English queries over Hausa, Somali, Swahili and Yoruba passages; cross-lingual reranking can rival reranking in African languages.
- `2023-11` **[Leveraging LLMs for Synthesizing Training Data Across Many Languages in Multilingual Dense Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-long.426/)** — Thakur et al. · *NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.05800)]<br>SWIM-IR: synthetic retrieval training data in 33 languages generated with summarize-then-ask prompting; SWIM-X retrievers trained on it are competitive with human-supervised models such as mContriever-X.
- `2023-05` **[Evaluating and Modeling Attribution for Cross-Lingual Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.10/)** — Muller et al. · *EMNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14332)]<br>First study of attribution in cross-lingual QA with data in 5 languages; many answers are unattributable to retrieved passages, and NLI models or lightly fine-tuned PaLM 2 detect attribution accurately.

## Low-Resource & Endangered Languages

- `2026-04` **[AFRILANGTUTOR: Advancing Language Tutoring and Culture Education in Low-Resource Languages with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.546/)** — Belay et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20996)]<br>AFRILANGDICT (194.7K African language-English dictionary entries) seeds AFRILANGEDU, 78.9K multi-turn tutoring examples; SFT plus DPO on Llama-3-8B-IT and Gemma-3-12B-IT improves tutoring across 10 African languages.
