- `2026-02` **[Under-resourced studies of under-resourced languages: lemmatization and POS-tagging with LLM annotators for historical Armenian, Georgian, Greek and Syriac](https://aclanthology.org/2026.loreslm-1.28/)** — Vidal-Gorène et al. · *LORESLM 2026 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15753)]<br>Benchmarks GPT-4 and Mistral models on lemmatization and POS tagging for Ancient Greek, Classical Armenian, Old Georgian and Syriac; few-shot, they match or beat an RNN baseline for most languages.
- `2026-01` **[Better as Generators Than Classifiers: Leveraging LLMs and Synthetic Data for Low-Resource Multilingual Classification](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.148/)** — Pecher et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16278)]<br>Uses a multilingual LLM to generate synthetic data for 11 languages and 4 classification tasks; small models trained on it outperform the generator, especially in low-resource languages.
- `2026` **[Want Better Synthetic Data? Steer It: Activation Steering for Low-Resource Language Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1290/)** — Cegin et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Tests Language and Quality activation steering for synthetic data generation in 11 languages with four LLMs; early-layer steering improves data diversity and often downstream classifiers, especially for low-resource languages.
- `2026` **[SynthLLM: An LLM-based Scalable Synthetic Data Generation Pipeline for Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-844/)** — Panahi et al. · *LREC 2026*<br>SynthLLM: generate-then-annotate pipeline yielding a 100,000-instance synthetic Farsi NLI dataset; open Aya-23-35B annotation matches GPT-4o-mini downstream, and synthetic warm-up beats data mixing.
- `2026` **[Script Is Not Preprocessing: Tokenizer-Mediated Transfer in Endangered Language Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1715/)** — Singh et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Releases a speaker-curated Hindi-English-X corpus of about 20k sentences per language for seven Indian tribal languages; fine-tuning beats prompted LLMs by 16-25 chrF++.
- `2026` **[Measuring Linguistic Competence of LLMs on Indigenous Languages of the Americas](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-short.21/)** — Vasselli et al. · *EACL 2026 (short)*<br>Prompts GPT, Gemini, DeepSeek, Qwen and LLaMA models on language identification, cloze and grammatical tasks for 13 Indigenous languages of the Americas; many perform near chance.
- `2026` **[Massively Multilingual Joint Segmentation and Glossing](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1657/)** — Ginn et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10925)]<br>PolyGloss: massively multilingual seq2seq models for joint interlinear glossing and morphological segmentation, outperforming GlossLM on glossing and open LLMs on segmentation, glossing and alignment.
- `2026` **[MUDIDI: A Two-Stage Framework for Multilingual Dictionary Digitization with Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2501/)** — Setiawan et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09435)]<br>MUDIDI: two-stage framework and dataset from 30 public-domain dictionaries for digitizing multilingual dictionaries of low-resource languages; LLMs beat OCR systems and VLMs on most scripts.
- `2026` **[LLM Agents as Computational Typologists](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.514/)** — Yang et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>AutoTypologist: ReAct-style LLM agent that codes typological features and tests universals over 25 reference grammars; it synthesizes grammar prose but struggles with interlinear glossed text alone.
- `2026` **[A RAG Approach for Typological Database Completion](https://aclanthology.org/2026.sigtyp-main.7/)** — Hus & Anastasopoulos · *SIGTYP 2026 (workshop)*<br>Uses grammar books with RAG to have LLMs identify Grambank typological features for five typologically diverse low-resource languages, showing reference material can be used effectively.
- `2025-11` **[LingGym: How Far Are LLMs from Thinking Like Field Linguists?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.69/)** — Yang et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00343)]<br>LingGym: benchmark of meta-linguistic reasoning using interlinear glossed text and grammatical descriptions from 18 typologically diverse reference grammars; structured linguistic cues consistently improve LLM performance.
- `2025-09` **[CUTE: A Multilingual Dataset for Enhancing Cross-Lingual Knowledge Transfer in Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.670/)** — Zhuang & Sun · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16914)]<br>CUTE: open Chinese, Uyghur, Tibetan and English corpus with two 25GB machine-translated sets (parallel and non-parallel), shown to improve LLM handling of Uyghur and Tibetan.
- `2025-08` **[UNVEILING: What Makes Linguistics Olympiad Puzzles Tricky for LLMs?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11260)** — Choudhary et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>Labels 629 Linguistics Olympiad problems in 41 low-resource languages with linguistic features; LLMs struggle with high morphological complexity, and morpheme splitting improves solvability.
- `2025-08` **[It’s All About In-Context Learning! Teaching Extremely Low-Resource Languages to LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1502/)** — Li et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19089)]<br>Analyzes teaching 20 extremely low-resource languages to three multilingual LLMs: zero-shot ICL with language alignment works well, while PEFT struggles when language and script are under-represented.
- `2025-06` **[Read it in Two Steps: Translating Extremely Low-Resource Languages with Code-Augmented Grammar Books](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.202/)** — Zhang et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01796)]<br>ZhuangRules: modular grammar-rule dataset showing rule retrieval bottlenecks grammar-based translation of extremely low-resource languages; encoding rules as code functions improves retrieval and application, giving a 13.1% BLEU gain.
- `2025-06` **[A Rigorous Evaluation of LLM Data Generation Strategies for Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.418/)** — Anikina et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.12158)]<br>Compares LLM synthetic-data generation strategies across 11 typologically diverse languages and three tasks; target-language demonstrations with LLM revision narrow the gap to gold data to as little as 5%.
- `2025-05` **[A Case Study of Cross-Lingual Zero-Shot Generalization for Classical Languages in LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.141/)** — Akavarapu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13173)]<br>Studies LLM zero-shot NER, MT and QA in Sanskrit, Ancient Greek and Latin with a new Sanskrit factoid QA dataset; RAG helps, while smaller models struggle.
- `2025-03` **[Explicit Learning and the LLM in Machine Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1599/)** — Marmonier et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.09454)]<br>Tests whether LLMs learn cipher-derived constructed languages from grammar explanations; explicit learning exists but weakens with complexity, and chain-of-thought SFT generalizes poorly to typologically new features.
- `2025` **[GAIfE: Using GenAI to Improve Literacy in Low-resourced Settings](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.442/)** — Tapo et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025*<br>GAIfE: generative-AI toolchain producing children's reading materials in Bambara while correcting Global-North-centric LLM bias; online Bambara content grew 10 times and pilots cut non-readers 67%.
- `2025` **[FSTs vs ICL: Generalisation in LLMs for an under-resourced language](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.867/)** — Gutierrez-Vasques et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025*<br>Compares finite-state transducers with in-context learning for orthographic normalization of Otomi variants; FSTs win zero-shot, but ICL lets LLMs surpass them.
- `2025` **[Does Synthetic Data Help Named Entity Recognition for Low-Resource Languages?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.ijcnlp-short.15/)** — Kamath & Vajjala · *IJCNLP 2025 (short)*<br>Tests LLM-generated synthetic NER data for 11 low-resource languages; it helps when no labels exist, but small high-quality data plus transfer from a related language works better.
- `2025` **[Can Large Language Models Translate Spoken-Only Languages through International Phonetic Transcription?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1195/)** — Chen et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>UNILANG: in-context framework translating spoken-only languages through IPA transcription with dictionary construction and retrieval; introduces SOLAN, a Bai dataset with high-resource-language translations.
- `2024-12` **[Can LLMs Help Create Grammar?: Automating Grammar Creation for Endangered Languages with In-Context Learning](https://aclanthology.org/2025.coling-main.681/)** — Spencer & Kongborrirak · *COLING 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10960)]<br>Uses in-context learning with bilingual dictionaries and parallel sentences to generate XLE grammar rules and lexical entries for endangered Moklen; LLMs capture key structures but show English grammatical bias.
- `2024-09` **[Can LLMs Really Learn to Translate a Low-Resource Language from One Grammar Book?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19151)** — Aycock et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>Finds that gains from prompting LLMs with a grammar book for Kalamang translation come almost entirely from its parallel examples, not grammatical explanations; similar for Nepali and Guarani.
- `2024-06` **[Multiple Sources are Better Than One: Incorporating External Knowledge in Low-Resource Glossing](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.261/)** — Yang et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11085)]<br>Improves automatic glossing for six low-resource languages by adding translations, LLM linguistic knowledge and dictionaries, gaining 5 points word-level accuracy on average and 10 points for Gitksan.
- `2024-05` **[Evaluating the Potential of Language-family-specific Generative Models for Low-resource Data Augmentation: A Faroese Case Study](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.576/)** — Scalvini & Debess · *LREC-COLING 2024*<br>Prompts the Nordic GPT-SW3 for Faroese-English translation and Faroese trick sentences, comparing with GPT-3.5/GPT-4, and builds an automatically annotated Faroese STS dataset.
- `2024-04` **[LLMs in the Loop: Leveraging Large Language Model Annotations for Active Learning in Low-Resource Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02261)** — Kholodna et al. · *ECML/PKDD 2024*<br>Uses GPT-4-Turbo as annotator in an active-learning loop for low-resource language classification, reaching near-best performance with estimated cost savings of at least 42.45 times.
- `2024-03` **[GlossLM: A Massively Multilingual Corpus and Pretrained Model for Interlinear Glossed Text](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.683/)** — Ginn et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06399)]<br>GlossLM: corpus of over 450k interlinear glossed text examples across 1.8k languages, plus a multilingual model pretrained on it for automatic IGT generation in documentation projects.
- `2024-02` **[Teaching Large Language Models an Unseen Language on the Fly](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.519/)** — Zhang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19167)]<br>DiPMT++: in-context framework using a dictionary and 5K parallel sentences to teach LLMs unseen Zhuang, raising GPT-4 from 0 to 16 BLEU (Chinese-to-Zhuang); also validated on Kalamang.
- `2024-02` **[LexC-Gen: Generating Data for Extremely Low-Resource Languages with Large Language Models and Bilingual Lexicons](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.818/)** — Yong et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14086)]<br>LexC-Gen: generates lexicon-compatible task data in high-resource languages with LLMs, then word-translates via bilingual lexicons; across 17 extremely low-resource languages it rivals expert-translated gold data.
- `2024-02` **[Hire a Linguist!: Learning Endangered Languages with In-Context Linguistic Descriptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18025)** — Zhang et al.<br>LingoLLM: training-free prompting with dictionaries, grammar books and morphologically analyzed text for endangered languages; across 8 languages it lifts GPT-4 translation from 0 to 10.5 BLEU.
- `2024-02` **[High-quality Data-to-Text Generation for Severely Under-Resourced Languages with Out-of-the-box Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-eacl.98/)** — Lorandi & Belz · *Findings of EACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12267)]<br>Tests out-of-the-box LLMs on data-to-text generation for Irish, Welsh, Breton and Maltese, matching humans in human evaluation while BLEU collapses relative to English.
- `2024-02` **[GPTs Are Multilingual Annotators for Sequence Generation Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-eacl.2/)** — Choi et al. · *Findings of EACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05512)]<br>Proposes autonomous LLM-based annotation for multilingual sequence generation datasets, cost-efficient and applicable to low-resource languages, and releases an image captioning dataset built this way.
- `2024` **[Hire a Linguist!: Learning Endangered Languages in LLMs with In-Context Linguistic Descriptions](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.925/)** — Zhang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024*<br>LingoLLM: training-free prompting with dictionaries, grammar books and morphological analyses for unseen endangered languages; raises GPT-4 translation from 0 to 10.5 BLEU over 10 directions.
- `2024` **[Fixing Rogue Memorization in Many-to-One Multilingual Translators of Extremely-Low-Resource Languages by Rephrasing Training Samples](https://aclanthology.org/2024.naacl-long.253/)** — Cavalin et al. · *NAACL 2024*<br>Fine-tunes mBART50 and WMT19 into many-to-one translators for 39 Brazilian Indigenous languages; multilingual models memorize and retrieve training outputs, which rephrasing training targets appears to fix.
- `2024` **[ChatGPT Label: Comparing the Quality of Human-Generated and LLM-Generated Annotations in Low-Resource Language NLP Tasks](https://doi.org/10.1109/ACCESS.2024.3402809)** — Nasution & Onan · *IEEE Access 2024*<br>Compares human and LLM-generated annotations for topic, sentiment and emotion classification in Turkish, Indonesian and Minangkabau; LLM labels compete on sentiment but trail humans on harder tasks.
- `2023-09` **[NusaWrites: Constructing High-Quality Corpora for Underrepresented and Extremely Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2023.ijcnlp-main.60/)** — Cahyawijaya et al. · *IJCNLP 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.10661)]<br>NusaWrites: benchmark for 12 underrepresented Indonesian local languages; paragraphs written by native speakers beat online scraping and human translation in lexical diversity and cultural content.
- `2023-09` **[A Benchmark for Learning to Translate a New Language from One Grammar Book](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16575)** — Tanzer et al. · *ICLR 2023*<br>MTOB: benchmark for English-Kalamang translation (under 200 speakers) learned from one grammar book; LLM baselines reach 44.7/45.8 chrF versus 51.6/57.0 for a human learner.

[⬆ Back to top](#)

## Multilingual Speech & Audio LLMs

- `2026-10` **[Qwen-Audio-3.1-ASR Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28711)** — Bian et al.<br>Qwen-Audio-3.1-ASR: audio-language model family for ASR in 30 languages and 16 Chinese dialects, adding speaker-diarized transcription and general audio understanding.
- `2026-09` **[Qwen-Audio-3.0-ASR Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07549)** — Bian et al.<br>Qwen-Audio-3.0-ASR: MoE LLM-based ASR on the Qwen backbone trained on tens of millions of hours, transcribing 30 languages and 16 Chinese dialects, with a streaming variant.
- `2026-09` **[MuLA-Bench: A Multilingual Long-Form Audio Understanding Benchmark via Multi-Tier Auditing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23416)** — Yang et al.<br>MuLA-Bench: 5,038 open-ended questions over 1,769 recordings (1,377.9 hours) in 16 languages and eight domains for long-form audio understanding; language rankings change across domains.
- `2026-09` **[Kraken: LLM-based Speech-to-Speech Translation via Low-bitrate VQ and Dual-path Source Conditioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13045)** — Futami et al.<br>Kraken: speech-to-speech translation model built on Qwen3-8B with low-bitrate VQ speech tokens and a source-conditioned vocoder, trained on 150k hours of multilingual speech.
- `2026-09` **[Beyond Word Error Rate: A Switch Aware Evaluation of ASR and Audio Language Models on English Yoruba Code-Switched Speech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11786)** — Okocha & Grant<br>Switch-aware evaluation of six ASR models and five audio LMs on 2000 English-Yoruba code-switched utterances: aggregate WER hides switching behavior, and Yoruba token recognition collapses.
- `2026-08` **[Language-Specialized Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation for Multilingual LLM-Based ASR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03610)** — Xie et al.<br>LS-MOPD: RL-trained language-specialized teachers distilled on-policy into a multilingual LLM-based ASR student, outperforming RL baselines on Mandarin, Mandarin subdialects, Cantonese and English.
- `2026-07` **[MEUSLI: a Multilingual Projector for LLM-based ASR and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22100)** — Concina et al.<br>MEUSLI: open multilingual projectors linking a Whisper encoder to open multilingual LLMs for end-to-end ASR in 28 European languages, also enabling speech translation and topic identification.
- `2026-07` **[Indic DiarBench: A Multilingual Joint Diarization and ASR Benchmark for Indian Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23808)** — Mehendale et al. · *Interspeech 2026*<br>Indic DiarBench: about 108 hours of human-annotated multi-speaker audio across all 22 scheduled Indian languages for joint diarization and ASR, with baselines from speech APIs and multimodal LLMs.
- `2026-06` **[Sarashina2.2-TTS: Tackling Kanji Polyphony in Japanese Speech Generation via Data Scaling and Targeted Data Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25369)** — Liu et al.<br>Sarashina2.2-TTS: Japanese-centric LLM-based TTS trained on about 361k hours, with targeted kanji data synthesis and the Joyo Kanji Yomi Benchmark covering 2,136 kanji and 4,378 readings.
- `2026-06` **[IndicContextEval: A Benchmark for Evaluating Context Utilisation in Audio Large Language Models Across 8 Indic Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19157)** — Joshi et al. · *Interspeech 2026*<br>IndicContextEval: 56-hour speech benchmark from 555 speakers in 8 Indian languages and 23 domains, testing via a 7-level prompting framework whether audio LLMs use textual context.
- `2026-06` **[GlobeAudio: A Multilingual Multicultural Benchmark for Naturalistic Evaluation of Large Audio-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1547/)** — Tan & Zhang · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08194)]<br>GlobeAudio: 5,637 native-speaker multiple-choice questions on naturally occurring audio in six languages; audio-language models lag under natural acoustics, especially open-source models and low-resource languages.
- `2026-05` **[Scalable Expansion of Multilingual Speech LLMs for ASR: A Continual Learning Approach](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-ws-speakable-07/)** — Concina et al. · *LREC 2026*<br>Expands multilingual speech LLMs for ASR to new languages via projector bootstrapping; rehearsal with small replay data prevents catastrophic forgetting, and the minimum budget is quantified.
- `2026-05` **[Mind the Pause: Disfluency-Aware Objective Tuning for Multilingual Speech Correction with LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.2137/)** — Kumar et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12242)]<br>Corrects disfluent ASR transcripts in Hindi, Bengali and Marathi by guiding LLM instruction tuning with tagger-marked disfluencies and a contrastive objective, beating multilingual sequence-to-sequence baselines.
- `2026-05` **[Bridging the Stability-Expressivity Gap: Synthetic Data Scaling and Preference Alignment for Low-Resource Spoken Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27383)** — Geng et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>Finds synthetic data for low-resource spoken language models improves phonetic accuracy but suppresses prosody (Stability-Expressivity Gap), and proposes self-alignment frameworks such as DGSA to recover expressivity.
- `2026-04` **[OmniVoice: Towards Omnilingual Zero-Shot Text-to-Speech with Diffusion Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00688)** — Zhu et al. · *Interspeech 2026*<br>OmniVoice: zero-shot TTS for over 600 languages, a diffusion-LM-style non-autoregressive model initialized from a pre-trained LLM and trained on 581k hours of open data.
- `2026-04` **[NaijaS2ST: A Multi-Accent Benchmark for Speech-to-Speech Translation in Low-Resource Nigerian Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.665/)** — Maltais et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16287)]<br>NaijaS2ST: about 50 hours per language of multi-accent parallel speech for Igbo, Hausa, Yorùbá and Nigerian Pidgin with English; few-shot audio LLMs lead speech-to-text translation.
- `2026-04` **[Efficient Training for Cross-lingual Speech Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.642/)** — Zhou et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11096)]<br>CSLM: trains cross-lingual speech LLMs on discrete speech tokens, aligning modalities and languages via continual pre-training and speech-text interleaved chain-of-modality instruction tuning without massive speech data.
- `2026-03` **[Voices of Civilizations: A Multilingual QA Benchmark for Global Music Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00533)** — Wu et al.<br>Voices of Civilizations: 1,190 multiple-choice questions on 380 full-length music tracks in 38 languages; audio LLMs miss cultural nuance without textual context and show cultural biases.
- `2026-03` **[Towards Orthographically-Informed Evaluation of Speech Recognition Systems for Indian Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00941)** — Bhogale et al. · *ICASSP 2026*<br>OIWER: LLM-assisted ASR metric for Indian languages that accepts permissible orthographic variants, lowering error rates 6.3 points on average and aligning better with human perception than WER-SN.
- `2026-03` **[PARSA-Bench: A Comprehensive Persian Audio-Language Model Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14456)** — Kalahroodi et al.<br>PARSA-Bench: 16-task Persian audio benchmark for LALMs; text-only baselines beat audio on most tasks except poetry, where audio wins and metre detection seems learnable only at the largest scale.
- `2026-02` **[WAXAL: A Large-Scale Multilingual African Language Speech Corpus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02734)** — Diack et al. · *NeurIPS 2026 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>WAXAL: over 14,100 hours of speech in 27 African languages, incl. 2,340 transcribed ASR hours in 19 languages; fine-tuning cuts Whisper-Large-v3 WER by up to 58%.
- `2026-02` **[Bias in the Ear of the Listener: Assessing Sensitivity in Audio Language Models Across Linguistic, Demographic, and Positional Variations](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.80/)** — Wei et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01030)]<br>BiasInEar: 70.8-hour speech version of Global MMLU Lite in English, Chinese and Korean; nine audio LLMs are robust to demographic factors but highly sensitive to language and option order.
- `2026-01` **[Towards Comprehensive Semantic Speech Embeddings for Chinese Dialects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07274)** — Chang et al.<br>Trains a speech encoder on ASR-only data to align Chinese dialects semantically with Mandarin and releases a benchmark of spoken Chinese varieties for speech-to-speech retrieval.
- `2026-01` **[RASST: Retrieval-Augmented Simultaneous Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1680/)** — Luo et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22777)]<br>RASST: retrieval-augmented simultaneous speech translation feeding a Speech LLM chunkwise terminology hints, improving terminology accuracy by nearly 40% and quality by up to 3 BLEU.
- `2026-01` **[Qwen3-ASR Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21337)** — Shi et al.<br>Qwen3-ASR: 1.7B and 0.6B models built on Qwen3-Omni for language identification and ASR in 52 languages and dialects, plus an LLM-based forced aligner for 11 languages.
- `2026-01` **[Multimodal In-context Learning for ASR of Low-resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1239/)** — Li & Niehues · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.05707)]<br>Shows speech LLMs Phi-4 and Qwen3-Omni learn three unseen endangered languages through multimodal in-context learning, and uses it to select acoustic-model hypotheses, consistently improving ASR.
- `2026-01` **[Multi-Task Instruction Tuning via Data Scheduling for Low-Resource Arabic SpeechLLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.aacl-long.157/)** — Bhatti et al. · *AACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12494)]<br>Compares data-scheduling strategies for multi-task instruction tuning of an Arabic-centric audio LLM and introduces AraMega-SSum, an Arabic speech summarization dataset; two-stage TPC->ADS balances tasks best.
- `2026` **[WenetSpeech-Wu: Datasets, Benchmarks, and Models for a Unified Chinese Wu Dialect Speech Processing Ecosystem](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1395/)** — Wang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>WenetSpeech-Wu: about 8,000 hours of annotated Wu dialect speech, a benchmark covering ASR, Wu-to-Mandarin translation, emotion recognition, TTS and instruct TTS, and open models.
- `2026` **[Text4Speech: Text-Driven Training for Massively Multilingual Speech Understanding](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2427/)** — Sterz et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Text4Speech: aligns a multilingual text encoder and then a speech encoder to an LLM via self-distillation, gaining up to 6% on Fleurs-SIB over a strong ASR cascade.
- `2026` **[TELEVAL: A Benchmark Designed for Spoken Language Models in Chinese Interactive Scenarios](https://openreview.net/forum?id=FkNmaFvAQA)** — Li et al. · *NeurIPS 2026 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>TELEVAL: Chinese spoken-interaction benchmark for spoken language models measuring content fulfillment and interactional appropriateness; identifies a 'Caption Trap' where models describe audio instead of responding.
- `2026` **[Simultaneous Speech-to-Speech Translation Without Aligned Data](https://openreview.net/forum?id=76XSBLdBdg)** — Labiausse et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>Hibiki-Zero: simultaneous speech-to-speech translation trained without word-level alignments, using sentence-level data then GRPO for latency; adapts to a new input language with under 1000h of speech.
- `2026` **[SimulS2ST-Omni: Data-Efficient Streaming Speech-to-Speech Translation via Explicit Trajectory Supervision](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1135/)** — He et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19810)]<br>SimulS2ST-Omni: training recipe for streaming speech-to-speech translation with a speech LM using ~2k hours of paired data, joint text-code trajectory supervision and Thinker-Talker factorization.
- `2026` **[SEA-SpeechBench: A Large-Scale Multitask Benchmark for Speech Understanding Across Southeast Asia](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1934/)** — Liao et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09672)]<br>SEA-SpeechBench: speech understanding benchmark for 11 Southeast Asian languages with 97,194 samples, 597 hours and 9 tasks; low-resource-language prompting lags English by up to 41 points.
- `2026` **[Post-ASR Correction in Hindi: Comparing Language Models and Large Language Models in Low-Resource Scenarios](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-short.45/)** — Kumar et al. · *EACL 2026 (short)*<br>Compares fine-tuned mT5/ByT5, Nanda 10B and instruction-tuned LLMs for Hindi post-ASR correction; smaller fine-tuned models win, and zero-shot ICL shows n-shaped inverse scaling.
- `2026` **[LLM-ForcedAligner: A Non-Autoregressive and Accurate LLM-Based Forced Aligner for Multilingual and Long-Form Speech](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1174/)** — Mu et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18220)]<br>LLM-ForcedAligner: recasts speech-LLM forced alignment as non-autoregressive slot filling of timestamp tokens, cutting accumulated shift by 69%-78% in multilingual, crosslingual and long-form speech.
- `2026` **[Hierarchical Policy Optimization for Simultaneous Translation of Unbounded Speech](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.80/)** — Ouyang et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.21045)]<br>HPO: hierarchical policy optimization with quality-latency rewards for LLM-based simultaneous speech translation, gaining over +7 COMET on English to Chinese/German/Japanese at 1.5 seconds latency.
- `2026` **[Hearing to Translate: The Effectiveness of Speech Modality Integration into LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.tacl-1.89/)** — Papi et al. · *TACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.16378)]<br>Hearing to Translate: compares 6 SpeechLLMs with 16 direct and cascade systems over 16 benchmarks and 13 language pairs; cascades stay most reliable, though recent SpeechLLMs can match them.
- `2026` **[DuraS2ST: Chain-of-Thought and Reinforcement Learning for Duration-Aligned Speech-to-Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1525/)** — Deng et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>DuraS2ST: speech language model that plans target wording and phonetic length via chain-of-thought before synthesizing speech, trained on DuraSet-440K and RL with a duration margin reward.
- `2026` **[DOA: Training-Free Decoder-Only Attention Policy for Long-Form Simultaneous Translation with SpeechLLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.323/)** — Papi & Bentivogli · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31432)]<br>DOA: training-free policy deriving alignment from SpeechLLM self-attention for long-form simultaneous speech translation, approaching offline quality on Phi4-Multimodal and Qwen3-Omni without retraining.
- `2026` **[CRESiST: Self-Enhancing Exploritive Policy Learning for Simultaneous Speech Translation](https://openreview.net/forum?id=omv2djtEFF)** — Le et al. · *NeurIPS 2026*<br>CRESiST: simultaneous speech translation on an omni-modal LLM with interleaved read-write streams and full KV-cache reuse, trained via an RNN-T actor and LLM critic; sets quality-latency frontiers on four benchmarks.
- `2026` **[Arab Voices: Mapping Standard and Dialectal Arabic Speech Technology](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.575/)** — Sullivan et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>Arab Voices: analyzes dialectness and audio quality of dialectal Arabic speech datasets and offers a standardized ASR framework over 31 datasets and 14 dialects, benchmarking tools and multimodal LLMs.
- `2026` **[AfriVox: Probing Multilingual and Accent Robustness of Speech LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.122/)** — Awobade et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>AfriVox: open benchmark spanning 20 African languages, African-accented French, Arabic and 100+ African English accents, comparing speech LLMs with ASR and speech translation models.
- `2026` **[Adapting Where It Matters: Depth-Aware Adaptation for Efficient Multilingual Speech Recognition in Low-Resource Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.827/)** — Xiao et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>Finds multilingual ASR models' early and late layers are language-specific (a U-shaped pattern); DAMA allocates adaptation by depth, matching prior accuracy on 18 low-resource languages with 80% fewer trainable parameters.
- `2025-11` **[VoiceCraft-X: Unifying Multilingual, Voice-Cloning Speech Synthesis and Speech Editing](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.137/)** — Zheng et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12347)]<br>VoiceCraft-X: autoregressive codec language model using Qwen3 for phoneme-free text processing, unifying zero-shot TTS and speech editing across 11 languages.
- `2025-11` **[SeaLLMs-Audio: Large Audio-Language Models for Southeast Asia](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01670)** — Liu et al.<br>SeaLLMs-Audio: large audio-language model for Indonesian, Thai, Vietnamese, English and Chinese covering ASR, speech translation, speech QA and voice dialogue, plus the SeaBench-Audio benchmark.
- `2025-11` **[PolyNorm: Few-Shot LLM-Based Text Normalization for Text-to-Speech](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-industry.6/)** — Wong et al. · *EMNLP 2025 (industry)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03080)]<br>PolyNorm: few-shot LLM prompting for TTS text normalization with a language-agnostic data curation pipeline; lowers WER versus a production-grade system across eight languages and releases PolyNorm-Benchmark.
- `2025-11` **[Omnilingual ASR: Open-Source Multilingual Speech Recognition for 1600+ Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09690)** — Keren et al.<br>Omnilingual ASR: open ASR system scaling self-supervised pretraining to 7B with an LLM-inspired decoder, covering over 1,600 languages, including over 500 never before served by ASR.
- `2025-11` **[CantoASR: Prosody-Aware ASR-LALM Collaboration for Low-Resource Cantonese](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04139)** — Chen et al.<br>CantoASR: combines forced-alignment acoustic features, LoRA-finetuned Whisper and instruction-tuned Qwen-Audio for prosody-aware error correction, improving CER over Whisper-Large-V3 on spontaneous Cantonese.
- `2025-10` **[VoiceAgentBench: Are Voice Assistants ready for agentic tasks?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.07978)** — Jain et al.<br>VoiceAgentBench: 6,000+ synthetic spoken agentic queries (tool calls, multi-turn, safety) in English and six Indic languages; ASR-LLM pipelines beat end-to-end SpeechLMs, which degrade more on Indic languages.
- `2025-09` **[WenetSpeech-Yue: A Large-scale Cantonese Speech Corpus with Multi-dimensional Annotation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03959)** — Li et al. · *AAAI 2025*<br>WenetSpeech-Yue: 21,800-hour Cantonese speech corpus with multi-dimensional annotations for ASR and TTS, plus the WSYue-eval benchmark.
- `2025-09` **[UniSS: Unified Expressive Speech-to-Speech Translation with Your Voice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.21144)** — Cheng et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>UniSS: single-stage expressive speech-to-speech translation built on a text LLM with cross-modal chain-of-thought prompting; releases UniST, a 44.8k-hour expressive S2ST dataset.
- `2025-09` **[Summary on The Multilingual Conversational Speech Language Model Challenge: Datasets, Tasks, Baselines, and Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.13785)** — Mu et al. · *ICASSP 2025*<br>Summarizes the Interspeech2025 MLC-SLM challenge on multilingual conversational speech LLMs, releasing about 1,604 hours of real conversational speech; 78 teams submitted 489 valid leaderboard results.
- `2025-08` **[Speech LLMs in Low-Resource Scenarios: Data Volume Requirements and the Impact of Pretraining on High-Resource Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.05149)** — Fong et al. · *Interspeech 2025*<br>Studies SLAM-ASR speech LLMs for low-resource ASR, measuring data needed to match Whisper and showing projectors pretrained on high-resource languages offset data scarcity; uses EuroLLM and Salamandra.
- `2025-08` **[CCFQA: A Benchmark for Cross-Lingual and Cross-Modal Speech and Text Factuality Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07295)** — Du et al. · *AAAI 2025*<br>CCFQA: parallel speech-text factual questions in 8 languages testing cross-lingual, cross-modal MLLM factuality; also proposes 5-shot transfer of English QA to multilingual spoken QA.
- `2025-07` **[StreamUni: Achieving Streaming Speech Translation with a Unified Large Speech-Language Model](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1859/)** — Guo et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07803)]<br>StreamUni: streaming speech translation with a unified large speech-language model using speech CoT to handle segmentation, policy decisions and translation without massive policy-specific training.
- `2025-06` **[mSTEB: Massively Multilingual Evaluation of LLMs on Speech and Text Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.08400)** — Beyene et al. · *Automatic Speech Recognition & Understanding 2025*<br>mSTEB: benchmark of language identification, text classification, QA and translation over speech and text; finds wide gaps for low-resource languages, especially those of Africa and Americas/Oceania.
- `2025-06` **[S2ST-Omni: Hierarchical Language-Aware SpeechLLM Adaptation for Multilingual Speech-to-Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1004/)** — Pan et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11160)]<br>S2ST-Omni: multilingual speech-to-speech translation linking a Whisper encoder and Qwen3 LLM with language-aware dual-CTC and prompting, plus a modular plug-and-play TTS backend.
- `2025-05` **[SpokenNativQA: Multilingual Everyday Spoken Queries for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19163)** — Alam et al. · *Interspeech 2025*<br>SpokenNativQA: about 33,000 naturally spoken questions and answers in multiple languages, including low-resource and dialect-rich ones, benchmarking ASR systems and LLMs on spoken QA.
- `2025-05` **[CosyVoice 3: Towards In-the-wild Speech Generation via Scaling-up and Post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17589)** — Du et al.<br>CosyVoice 3: LLM-based zero-shot multilingual speech synthesis scaled to 1.5B parameters and one million hours across 9 languages and 18 Chinese dialects, with a new speech tokenizer.
- `2025-04` **[SimulS2S-LLM: Unlocking Simultaneous Inference of Speech LLMs for Speech-to-Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.817/)** — Deng et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15509)]<br>SimulS2S-LLM: trains speech LLMs offline and uses a test-time policy with boundary-aware speech prompts for simultaneous speech-to-speech translation, improving ASR-BLEU by 3 points at similar latency on CVSS.
- `2025-04` **[SIFT-50M: A Large-Scale Multilingual Dataset for Speech Instruction Fine-Tuning](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.681/)** — Pandey et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09081)]<br>SIFT-50M: 50M-example speech instruction dataset in five languages built from 14K hours of public speech, used to train SIFT-LLM, plus the EvalSIFT instruction-following benchmark.
- `2025-04` **[Efficient and Adaptive Simultaneous Speech Translation with Fully Unidirectional Architecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.11809)** — Fu et al. · *AAAI 2025*<br>EASiST: fully unidirectional speech encoder and LLM for simultaneous speech translation with read/write tokens and a policy head, improving latency-quality trade-offs on En-De and En-Es.
- `2025-03` **[Zero-AVSR: Zero-Shot Audio-Visual Speech Recognition with LLMs by Learning Language-Agnostic Speech Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06273)** — Yeo et al. · *IEEE International Conference on Computer Vision 2025*<br>Zero-AVSR: zero-shot audio-visual speech recognition that predicts Roman text and uses LLMs to produce language-specific graphemes, with the 2,916-hour, 82-language MARC corpus.
- `2025-03` **[Whisper-LM: Improving ASR Models with Language Models for Low-Resource Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23542)** — Zuazo et al.<br>Whisper-LM: combines fine-tuned Whisper with statistical and large language models for low-resource languages, improving results up to 51% in-distribution and 34% out-of-distribution; LLMs give moderate, consistent gains.
- `2025-03` **[InfiniSST: Simultaneous Translation of Unbounded Speech with Large Language Model](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.157/)** — Ouyang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.02969)]<br>InfiniSST: frames simultaneous translation of unbounded speech as multi-turn dialogue with KV-cache management; cuts computation-aware latency by 0.5 to 1 second on MuST-C En-Es, En-De, En-Zh.
- `2025-03` **[From Tower to Spire: Adding the Speech Modality to a Translation-Specialist LLM](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.1071/)** — Ambilduke et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.10620)]<br>Spire: adds speech to the Tower translation LLM via discretized speech and continued pretraining on 42.5K hours, translating and transcribing English speech into 10 languages while keeping text performance.
- `2025-01` **[Fleurs-SLU: A Massively Multilingual Benchmark for Spoken Language Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06117)** — Schmidt et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>Fleurs-SLU: spoken language understanding benchmark with topical classification in 102 languages and listening-comprehension QA in 92; cascaded systems are most robust, closed speech-LLMs match them.
- `2025-01` **[FireRedASR: Open-Source Industrial-Grade Mandarin Speech Recognition Models from Encoder-Decoder to LLM Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14350)** — Xu et al.<br>FireRedASR: open Mandarin ASR models, an 8.3B Encoder-Adapter-LLM variant with 3.05% average CER and a 1.1B AED variant, also competitive on Chinese dialects and English.
- `2025` **[OWLS: Scaling Laws for Multilingual Speech Recognition and Translation Models](https://openreview.net/forum?id=xnPW7yYomF)** — Chen et al. · *ICML 2025*<br>OWLS: open suite of multilingual ASR and speech translation models from 0.25B to 18B parameters trained on up to 360K hours in 150 languages, used to derive scaling laws.
- `2025` **[MERaLiON-AudioLLM: Advancing Speech and Language Understanding for Singapore](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-demo.3/)** — He et al. · *ACL 2025 (demo)*<br>MERaLiON-AudioLLM: audio LLM for Singlish trained on 62 million multimodal instruction samples over 260,000 hours, covering ASR, spoken QA, speech translation and paralinguistics.
- `2025` **[Locate-and-Focus: Enhancing Terminology Translation in Speech Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.556/)** — Wu et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>Locate-and-Focus: locates speech clips containing terminology, links that translation knowledge to audio and text, and raises terminology translation success in speech translation models.
- `2025` **[High-Fidelity Simultaneous Speech-To-Speech Translation](https://openreview.net/forum?id=fgjN8B6xVX)** — Labiausse et al. · *ICML 2025*<br>Hibiki: decoder-only multistream language model for simultaneous speech-to-text and speech-to-speech translation, learning per-word delays from weak supervision; strong French-English quality, speaker fidelity and naturalness.
- `2025` **[From Tens of Hours to Tens of Thousands: Scaling Back-Translation for Speech Recognition](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.629/)** — Wang et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>Speech Back-Translation: TTS models trained on tens of hours generate over 500,000 hours of synthetic speech in ten languages; continued Whisper-large-v3 pretraining cuts average transcription errors by over 30%.
- `2025` **[EuroSpeech: A Multilingual Speech Corpus](https://openreview.net/forum?id=26VLybEQ2h)** — Pfisterer et al. · *NeurIPS 2025 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>EuroSpeech: pipeline extracting over 61k hours of aligned speech from 22 European parliaments, with 19 languages over 1k hours; fine-tuning an ASR model cuts WER by 41.8% on average.
- `2025` **[Cross-Lingual Transfer Learning for Speech Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-short.4/)** — Ma et al. · *NAACL 2025 (short)*<br>Shows Whisper's encoder maps different languages to a shared semantic space; fine-tuning its decoder on English-to-Chinese speech translation data alone improves Chinese translation from multiple source languages.
- `2025` **[CoVoGER: A Multilingual Multitask Benchmark for Speech-to-text Generative Error Correction with Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.320/)** — Yang et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>CoVoGER: generative error correction benchmark for ASR and speech translation across 15 languages and 28 pairs, built from Whisper and SeamlessM4T 5-best lists, evaluating instruction-tuned LLMs.
- `2025` **[AfriSpeech-MultiBench: A Verticalized Multidomain Multicountry Benchmark Suite for African Accented English ASR](https://aclanthology.org/2025.ijcnlp-long.190/)** — Ashungafac et al. · *IJCNLP 2025*<br>AfriSpeech-MultiBench: ASR evaluation suite for over 100 African English accents across 10+ countries and seven domains; multimodal LLMs are more accent-robust but struggle with domain-specific named entities.
- `2024-12` **[MERaLiON-AudioLLM: Bridging Audio and Language with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09818)** — He et al.<br>MERaLiON-AudioLLM: speech-text model tailored for Singapore's multilingual and multicultural setting, handling local accents and dialects, with improved speech recognition and task-specific understanding.
- `2024-11` **[Fish-Speech: Leveraging Large Language Models for Advanced Multilingual Text-to-Speech Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01156)** — Liao et al.<br>Fish-Speech: open-source TTS framework with a Dual-AR architecture that uses LLMs for linguistic feature extraction instead of grapheme-to-phoneme conversion, aiding multilingual synthesis and voice cloning.
- `2024-09` **[Making LLMs Better Many-to-Many Speech-to-Text Translators with Curriculum Learning](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.610/)** — Du et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19510)]<br>Three-stage curriculum adapting LLM translation ability to many-to-many speech-to-text translation, reaching top average results on 15x14 language pairs and competitive results with under 10 hours per language.
- `2024-09` **[Ideal-LLM: Integrating Dual Encoders and Language-Adapted LLM for Multilingual Speech-to-Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11214)** — Xue et al.<br>Ideal-LLM: speech LLM combining Whisper and MMS encoders with a language-adapted connector; cuts average ASR word error rate 32.6% relative and reaches 36.78 BLEU on speech translation.
- `2024-08` **[Speech-MASSIVE: A Multilingual Speech Dataset for SLU and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03900)** — Lee et al. · *Interspeech 2024*<br>Speech-MASSIVE: spoken counterpart of part of MASSIVE in 12 languages with intent and slot annotations, plus SLU baselines and uses for transcription, language identification and speech translation.
- `2024-07` **[Towards Achieving Human Parity on End-to-end Simultaneous Speech Translation via LLM Agent](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21646)** — Cheng et al.<br>CLASI: LLM-based simultaneous speech translation system with retrieval for terminology; reaches 81.3% and 78.0% valid information proportion for Chinese-English directions versus 35.4% and 41.6% for others.
- `2024-07` **[LLaST: Improved End-to-end Speech Translation System Leveraged by Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.416/)** — Chen et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15415)]<br>LLaST: LLM-based speech-to-text translation framework with ASR-augmented training, multilingual data augmentation and dual-LoRA optimization, evaluated on CoVoST-2.
- `2024-07` **[FunAudioLLM: Voice Understanding and Generation Foundation Models for Natural Interaction Between Humans and LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04051)** — An et al.<br>FunAudioLLM: SenseVoice for ASR in over 50 languages, emotion and audio events, and CosyVoice for multilingual speech generation with cross-lingual voice cloning, integrated with LLMs.
- `2024-07` **[CosyVoice: A Scalable Multilingual Zero-shot Text-to-speech Synthesizer based on Supervised Semantic Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05407)** — Du et al.<br>CosyVoice: multilingual zero-shot TTS using supervised semantic tokens from a multilingual ASR encoder, an LLM for text-to-token generation, and flow matching for synthesis.
- `2024-03` **[Aligning Speech to Languages to Enhance Code-switching Speech Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.05887)** — Liu et al.<br>Language alignment loss for Mandarin-English code-switching ASR plus LLM generative error correction guided by linguistic hints; 8.6% relative improvement on ASRU 2019 data.
- `2024-01` **[Multilingual and Fully Non-Autoregressive ASR with Large Language Model Fusion: A Comprehensive Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.12789)** — Huang et al. · *ICASSP 2024*<br>Fuses USM ASR with PaLM 2 in non-autoregressive per-segment scoring, improving multilingual WER 10.8% relative on FLEURS and 3.6% on YouTube captions; ablates LLM size 128M-340B.
- `2024` **[PolyVoice: Language Models for Speech to Speech Translation](https://openreview.net/forum?id=hCrFG9cyuC)** — Dong et al. · *ICLR 2024*<br>PolyVoice: speech-to-speech translation with three decoder-only language models over unsupervised semantic units, applicable to unwritten languages; evaluated on Chinese-English and English-Spanish.
- `2024` **[OWSM-CTC: An Open Encoder-Only Speech Foundation Model for Speech Recognition, Translation, and Language Identification](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.549/)** — Peng et al. · *ACL 2024*<br>OWSM-CTC: open encoder-only CTC speech foundation model trained on 180k hours for multilingual ASR, speech translation and language identification, 3 to 4 times faster than encoder-decoder OWSM.
- `2024` **[Make-A-Voice: Revisiting Voice Large Language Models as Scalable Multilingual and Multitask Learners](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.589/)** — Huang et al. · *ACL 2024*<br>Make-A-Voice: voice LLM trained on about 200K hours of 6-language data for 4 voice generation tasks, where rich-resource languages ease data scarcity for low-resource ones.
- `2024` **[IndicVoices: Towards building an Inclusive Multilingual Speech Dataset for Indian Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.639/)** — Javed et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024*<br>IndicVoices: 7348 hours of read, extempore and conversational speech from 16237 speakers in 22 Indian languages (1639 hours transcribed), plus an open data-collection blueprint and IndicASR.
- `2023-12` **[Speech Translation with Large Language Models: An Industrial Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.13585)** — Huang et al.<br>LLM-ST: speech translation model joining a pretrained LLM with a speech encoder via multi-task instruction tuning, producing timestamped transcriptions and translations from long audio, tested on English and Chinese.
- `2023-10` **[SALM: Speech-augmented Language Model with In-context Learning for Speech Recognition and Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.09424)** — Chen et al. · *ICASSP 2023*<br>SALM: frozen text LLM with audio encoder, modality adapter and LoRA for ASR and speech translation, matching Conformer baselines and showing zero-shot in-context learning.
- `2023-08` **[SeamlessM4T: Massively Multilingual & Multimodal Machine Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.11596)** — Communication et al.<br>SeamlessM4T: single model for speech-to-speech, speech-to-text, text-to-speech and text-to-text translation plus ASR in up to 100 languages, built on 1 million hours of open speech audio.
- `2023-07` **[Prompting Large Language Models with Speech Recognition Abilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.11795)** — Fathullah et al. · *ICASSP 2023*<br>Prepends conformer audio embeddings to LLaMA-7B for ASR, outperforming monolingual baselines by 18% relative WER on Multilingual LibriSpeech despite LLaMA's overwhelmingly English training.
- `2023-06` **[AudioPaLM: A Large Language Model That Can Speak and Listen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.12925)** — Rubenstein et al.<br>AudioPaLM: fuses PaLM-2 and AudioLM into one speech-text LLM; text-LLM initialization improves speech processing, and it performs zero-shot speech-to-text translation for unseen language combinations and cross-lingual voice transfer.
- `2023-05` **[ComSL: A Composite Speech-Language Model for End-to-End Speech-to-Text Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14838)** — Le et al. · *NeurIPS 2023*<br>ComSL: composite speech-language model from pretrained speech-only and language-only models, reaching 31.5 average BLEU on CoVoST2 speech-to-English translation for 21 languages.
- `2023-03` **[Speak Foreign Languages with Your Own Voice: Cross-Lingual Neural Codec Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.03926)** — Zhang et al.<br>VALL-E X: multilingual conditional codec language model for zero-shot cross-lingual TTS and speech-to-speech translation, keeping the speaker's voice from one source-language utterance and reducing foreign accent.
- `2023-02` **[Massively Multilingual Shallow Fusion with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.08917)** — Hu et al. · *ICASSP 2023*<br>Trains a GLaM mixture-of-experts LM for ASR shallow fusion across up to 84 languages; it improves 41 of 50 languages with a 3.85% average relative WER reduction.
- `2022-12` **[Robust Speech Recognition via Large-Scale Weak Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.04356)** — Radford et al.<br>Trains speech recognition models on 680,000 hours of multilingual, multitask weak supervision from web transcripts; they generalize zero-shot to standard benchmarks and approach human accuracy and robustness.
- `2020-12` **[MLS: A Large-Scale Multilingual Dataset for Speech Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.03411)** — Pratap et al. · *Interspeech 2020*<br>MLS: Multilingual LibriSpeech corpus from LibriVox audiobooks in 8 languages, about 44.5K hours of English and 6K hours of other languages, with LMs and baseline ASR models.

[⬆ Back to top](#)

## Multilingual & Multicultural Vision-Language Models

- `2026-08` **[Analyzing and Mitigating Cross-Lingual Degradation in Multilingual Medical VQA](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.920/)** — Wang et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22363)]<br>Builds an eight-language medical VQA benchmark revealing scenario-dependent cross-lingual degradation in LVLMs, and MedVL-XLRepE, training-free steering toward English representations, with gains up to 6.33%.
- `2026-07` **[MORE: A Multilingual Document Parsing Benchmark and Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02956)** — Xu et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>MORE: multilingual document parsing benchmark covering 149 languages that evaluates VLMs beyond plain text on structural elements such as code blocks.
- `2026-07` **[Evaluation of Multilingual Ability to Use Spatial Deictic Expressions in Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-srw.106/)** — Watanabe et al. · *ACL 2026 (SRW)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07251)]<br>Benchmark of spatial deictic expressions (demonstratives like 'this' and 'that') for VLMs in four languages; models choose demonstratives differently from humans, especially by object distance.
- `2026-07` **[Debiasing Text-to-Image Evaluation via Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15740)** — Chang & Chen<br>Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Model on a 4.2B MLLM scores text-to-image cultural authenticity, reaching 83.49% pairwise accuracy on 3,323 CulturalFrames image pairs at 0.21 seconds per evaluation.
- `2026-06` **[When Meaning Travels: A Granular Lens on Hybrid-MoE’s Role in Idiomatic Understanding for Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1661/)** — Das et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01671)]<br>Varnika: multimodal corpus of 3,533 idioms in languages such as Hindi, Bengali and Thai with seven idiomatic tones; a HybridMoE method gives 5–6% gains across vision-language models.
- `2026-06` **[Not Truly Multilingual: Script Consistency as a Missing Dimension in VLM Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.232/)** — Singh et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17188)]<br>PuMVR: 1,000 parallel image-text instances in Punjabi's Gurmukhi, Shahmukhi and Roman scripts; 10 VLMs show script gaps up to 16% that visual input does not close.
- `2026-06` **[Almieyar-Oryx-BloomBench: A Bilingual Multimodal Benchmark for Cognitively Informed Evaluation of Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1416/)** — Abootorabi et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05531)]<br>BloomBench: English-Arabic multimodal benchmark grounded in Bloom's Taxonomy across six cognition levels, finding VLMs weak in factual recall and creative synthesis and an Arabic-English gap.
- `2026-05` **[Multilingual OCR-Aware Fine-Tuning and Prompt-Guided Chain-of-Thought Reasoning for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16409)** — Xu et al.<br>OCR-aware multilingual post-training of an MLLM with about 5M extra samples, LoRA SFT and OCR-oriented CoT; raises OCR completeness from 71.3 to 84.6 and cuts hallucination from 18.3% to 5.5%.
- `2026-05` **[FTibSuite: A Comprehensive Resource Suite for Tibetan Vision–Language Modeling](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.903/)** — Xu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26601)]<br>FTibSuite: Tibetan vision-language training corpora, FTibBench (five adapted multimodal benchmarks) and FTibVLM on Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, raising MMBench accuracy from 42.97 to 67.78.
- `2026-04` **[INDOTABVQA: A Benchmark for Cross-Lingual Table Understanding in Bahasa Indonesia Documents](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1105/)** — Gautam et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11970)]<br>INDOTABVQA: 1,593 Bahasa Indonesia document images with table QA in Indonesian, English, Hindi and Arabic; VLMs struggle on complex tables and low-resource languages, and fine-tuning helps.
- `2026-03` **[Unified Vision-Language Modeling via Concept Space Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01096)** — Qiu et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>V-SONAR: maps a vision encoder into the SONAR embedding space (1500 text languages); V-LCM, built on it, matches top vision-language models on image and video captioning and question answering.
- `2026-03` **[Many Dialects, Many Languages, One Cultural Lens: Evaluating Multilingual VLMs for Bengali Culture Understanding Across Historically Linked Languages and Regional Dialects](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.2129/)** — Sayeedi et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21165)]<br>BanglaVerse: 1,152 curated images across nine domains expanded into four languages and five Bangla dialects (~32.2K artifacts); evaluating only standard Bangla overestimates VLM capability.
- `2026-03` **[BanglaProtha: Evaluating Vision Language Models in Underrepresented Long-tail Cultural Contexts](https://doi.org/10.1109/WACV61042.2026.00119)** — Fahim et al.<br>BanglaProtha: VQA dataset of Bengali cultural images with native-Bengali questions, used to analyze VLMs across prompting and fine-tuning strategies, cultural aspects, model size and augmentation.
- `2026-02` **[Once Correct, Still Wrong: Counterfactual Hallucination in Multilingual Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.234/)** — Mousi et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05437)]<br>M2CQA: benchmark from images of 17 MENA countries with true and counterfactual statements in English, Arabic and dialects; counterfactual hallucination rises sharply in Arabic, especially dialects.
- `2026-02` **[Annotation-Efficient Vision-Language Model Adaptation to the Polish Language Using the LLaVA Framework](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-srw.44/)** — Statkiewicz et al. · *EACL 2026 (SRW)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14073)]<br>Adapts LLaVA-Next to Polish using automatically translated and filtered multimodal data plus synthetic OCR and cultural data; gains 9.5% over LLaVA-1.6-Vicuna-13B on a Polish MMBench.
- `2026-01` **[VULCA-Bench: A Multicultural Vision-Language Benchmark for Evaluating Cultural Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07986)** — Yu et al.<br>VULCA-Bench: 7,410 image-critique pairs across eight cultural traditions with Chinese-English critiques and a five-layer framework; higher-layer cultural reasoning is harder for VLMs than visual analysis.
- `2026-01` **[MemeLens: Multilingual Multitask VLMs for Memes](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2290/)** — Shahroor et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.12539)]<br>MemeLens: multilingual multitask VLM for memes trained on 38 public meme datasets mapped to a shared taxonomy of 20 tasks; finds robust meme understanding requires multimodal training.
- `2026-01` **[M3Kang: Evaluating Multilingual Multimodal Mathematical Reasoning in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.16218)** — Torres-Camps et al.<br>M3Kang: 1,747 Kangaroo Math Competition multiple-choice problems, some with diagrams, translated into 108 languages; VLMs still struggle with basic math and diagram-based reasoning.
- `2026` **[Where Do Multilingual Vision-Language Encoders Fail on Low-Resource Languages?](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.840/)** — Han et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Traces the low-resource retrieval gap in multilingual vision-language encoders to diverging per-language EOS hidden states, not a linear language direction; swapping in English EOS lifts Swahili 22.1% to 69.1%.
- `2026` **[When Does Language Matter? Multilingual Instructions Reveal Step-wise Language Sensitivity in Vision-Language-Action Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.2066/)** — Dong et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>Translates the LIBERO benchmark into ten languages, showing VLA success rates drop 30-50% under non-English instructions, and proposes a step-wise inference-time intervention aligned to step language sensitivity.
- `2026` **[VisTW: Benchmarking Vision-Language Models for Taiwanese Mandarin in Taiwan](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1830/)** — Tam et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>VisTW: Taiwanese Mandarin VLM benchmark with 3,795 academic MCQs and 141 free-form pairs, measuring Simplified-character contamination (3%-19%) with a human-grounded purity penalty.
- `2026` **[VMMU: A Vietnamese Multitask Multimodal Understanding and Reasoning Benchmark](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-aacl.44/)** — Dang et al. · *Findings of AACL 2026*<br>VMMU: 2,548 Vietnamese multimodal MCQs across seven domains rendered as page images; test-time compute lifts VLMs from 57% to 78% versus 99% for experts.
- `2026` **[VLURes: Benchmarking Long-Text Grounding and Cross-Lingual Robustness in Vision Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1367/)** — Atuhurra et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>VLURes: 4,000 image and long-text pairs in English, Japanese, Swahili and Urdu with eight tasks; GPT-4o reaches 90.8% overall while cross-lingual sensitivity persists.
- `2026` **[VFA: Empowering Multilingual MLLMs via Vision-Free Adaptation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.407/)** — Li et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26155)]<br>VFA: merges a multilingual text task vector with an MLLM's vision-aligned one, improving five MLLMs on six benchmarks, staying competitive with full multimodal training using under 2% of text data.
- `2026` **[SoS: Analysis of Surface over Semantics in Multilingual Text-To-Image Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.185/)** — Holtermann et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>Prompts seven text-to-image models with 171 cultural identities in 14 languages; all but one show strong surface-over-semantics tendencies in at least two languages, often with stereotypical depictions.
- `2026` **[RAVENEA: A Benchmark for Multimodal Retrieval-Augmented Visual Culture Understanding](https://openreview.net/forum?id=4zAbkxQ23i)** — Li et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>RAVENEA: benchmark for retrieval-augmented visual culture understanding (culture-focused VQA and captioning) with 11,396 human-ranked Wikipedia documents, evaluating seven multimodal retrievers and fifteen VLMs.
- `2026` **[Qolda: A Small Vision–Language Model for the Kazakh Language](https://doi.org/10.1109/ACCESS.2026.3676919)** — Arystanbekov et al. · *IEEE Access 2026*<br>Qolda: 4.3B Kazakh vision-language model built on Qwen3-4B and InternVL3.5 via SFT, projection alignment, CoT tuning and MPO, with trilingual Kazakh-Russian-English multimodal data and Kazakh benchmarks.
- `2026` **[POLYCHARTQA: Benchmarking Large Vision-Language Models with Multilingual Chart Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.2043/)** — Xu et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11939)]<br>PolyChartQA: 22,606 charts and 26,151 QA pairs in 10 languages; LVLMs trail English in other languages, and fine-tuning on the companion PolyChartQA-Train set yields gains.
- `2026` **[On the Cultural Anachronism and Temporal Reasoning in Vision Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1514/)** — Ranjan et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026*<br>TAB-VLM: 600 questions on 1,600 Indian cultural artifacts testing cultural anachronism in VLMs; the best of ten models (GPT-5.2) reaches only 58.7% accuracy.
- `2026` **[MemeCULT-1K: Benchmarking South Asian Cultural Context and Humor Understanding of Multimodal Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.550/)** — Dipto et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01772)]<br>MemeCULT-1K: 1,000 South Asian memes in Bengali, English and Hindi with cultural context notes and explanations; minimal cultural context improves all thirteen evaluated VLMs.
- `2026` **[MPR-GUI: Benchmarking and Enhancing Multilingual Perception and Reasoning in GUI Agents](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1375/)** — Chen et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.00756)]<br>MPR-GUI-Bench: strictly aligned GUI-agent environments in six languages with eight perception and reasoning tasks; GUI-XLI aligns non-English hidden states with English at language-sensitive layers to reduce gaps.
- `2026` **[Large-scale Multilingual News Image Captioning with LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1209/)** — Chen et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>LaMuN: news image captioning benchmark with more than 800,000 images in 59 languages including low-resource ones, plus a chain-of-thought captioning method evaluated across its languages.
- `2026` **[Finding Culture-Sensitive Neurons in Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.155/)** — Zhao et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>Finds culture-sensitive neurons in three VLMs using CVQA across 25 cultural groups; ablating them selectively harms questions about the corresponding cultures, and the ConAct selector finds them best.
- `2026` **[DuwatBench: Bridging Language and Visual Heritage through an Arabic Calligraphy Benchmark for Multimodal Understanding](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.243/)** — Patle et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>DuwatBench: 1,272 Arabic calligraphy samples across 6 styles with detection annotations; 13 Arabic and multilingual multimodal models handle clean text but struggle with calligraphic variation and distortions.
- `2026` **[DiM$^3$: Bridging Multilingual and Multimodal Models via Direction- and Magnitude-Aware Merging](https://openreview.net/forum?id=CF3jmTcKdp)** — Wang et al. · *NeurIPS 2026*<br>DiM3: training-free direction- and magnitude-aware merging that injects multilingual updates into multimodal models, improving performance across 57 languages on LLaVA and Qwen backbones.
- `2026` **[CultureVidBench: Benchmarking Cultural Understanding in Text-to-Video Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.2191/)** — Han et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>CultureVidBench: 1,000 prompts covering 12 countries and 14 cultural aspects for text-to-video models; seven models show strong semantic adherence but often miss fine-grained cultural details.
- `2026` **[Culture in Action: Evaluating Text-to-Image Models through Social Activities](https://openreview.net/forum?id=opG4m2U0Oo)** — Malakouti et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>CULTIVate: 576 social activities in 9 categories with over 19,000 images from 16 countries for text-to-image cultural faithfulness; images are more faithful for Global North cultures.
- `2026` **[Culturally-Aware Alignment for Multimodal Models via Group Reward Optimization for Southeast Asia](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.863/)** — Zhang et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>SEA-Omni Benchmark Suite and MERaLiON-Omni-10B, a Southeast Asian omni-perception model aligned with GRPO using a fact-anchored dual reward balancing cultural richness and multimodal consistency.
- `2026` **[Cultural Moment Benchmark: Evaluating Video Cultural Reasoning and Grounding in Southeast Asia](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1913/)** — Satar et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>Cultural Moment Benchmark: 306 expert-curated cultural concepts from seven Southeast Asian countries tested in three video stages; the strongest closed models score below 30% when all stages must be correct.
- `2026` **[CAIRE: Cultural Attribution of Images with Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.389/)** — Yayavaram et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>CAIRE: retrieval-grounded metric scoring an image's cultural relevance to user-defined labels; beats baselines by 22% F1 and reaches 0.56 and 0.66 Pearson correlation with human ratings.
- `2026` **[Beyond Static Symbols: Evidence-Grounded Visual Checklists for Cultural Activity Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.663/)** — Deng et al. · *EMNLP 2026*<br>CARE: evidence-grounded visual checklists with VLM checks and prompt repair for cultural activity image generation, plus CARE-1360, 1,360 activity prompts across eight countries.
- `2026` **[Beyond Atomic Characters: Glyph-Aware Sub-character Alignment for Low-Resource Multilingual OCR](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1392/)** — Zhu et al. · *ACL 2026*<br>BASA: OCR framework with a glyph-aware adapter aligning sub-character strokes and radicals for low-resource multilingual scripts, plus BASA-Bench covering 11 languages and 23 authentic scenarios.
- `2026` **[BLEnD-Vis: Benchmarking Multimodal Cultural Understanding in Vision Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2026.eacl-long.215/)** — Tan et al. · *EACL 2026*<br>BLEnD-Vis: 313 cultural question templates across 16 regions with 4,916 images and over 21,000 MCQs; VLM cultural knowledge drops under linguistic rephrasing and shows low cross-modal consistency.
- `2025-12` **[dots.ocr: Multilingual Document Layout Parsing in a Single Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.02498)** — Li et al.<br>dots.ocr: single vision-language model jointly learning layout detection, text recognition and relational understanding from a synthesized multilingual corpus; introduces XDocParse, a 126-language benchmark, with about 10% relative gain.
- `2025-11` **[IndicVisionBench: Benchmarking Cultural and Multilingual Understanding in VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04727)** — Faraz et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>IndicVisionBench: ~5K images and 37K+ QA pairs in English and 10 Indian languages covering OCR, multimodal MT and VQA on 13 cultural topics; 8 VLMs show gaps.
- `2025-10` **[SignLLM: Sign Language Production Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICCVW69036.2025.00690)** — Fang et al. · *ICCVW 2025 (workshop)*<br>SignLLM: multilingual sign language production LLM with two generation modes and an RL-based Priority Learning Channel, trained on the Prompt2Sign dataset covering ASL and seven other sign languages.
- `2025-10` **[PaddleOCR-VL: Boosting Multilingual Document Parsing via a 0.9B Ultra-Compact Vision-Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14528)** — Cui et al.<br>PaddleOCR-VL: 0.9B document-parsing VLM pairing a NaViT-style dynamic-resolution encoder with ERNIE-4.5-0.3B, supporting 109 languages and recognizing text, tables, formulas and charts.
- `2025-10` **[MERLIN: A Testbed for Multilingual Multimodal Entity Recognition and Linking](https://aclanthology.org/2026.tacl-1.19/)** — Ramamoorthy et al. · *TACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14307)]<br>MERLIN: multilingual multimodal entity linking testbed pairing BBC headlines and images in Hindi, Japanese, Indonesian, Vietnamese and Tamil, with over 7,000 mentions linked to 2,500 Wikidata entities.
- `2025-10` **[Culture In a Frame: C$^3$B as a Comic-Based Benchmark for Multimodal Culturally Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.00041)** — Song et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>C3B: over 2000 comic images and 18000 QA pairs in three tasks from visual recognition to cultural conflict understanding and content generation; 11 open MLLMs trail humans.
- `2025-09` **[DRISHTIKON: A Multimodal Multilingual Benchmark for Testing Language Models’ Understanding on Indian Culture](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.68/)** — Maji et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.19274)]<br>DRISHTIKON: multimodal benchmark on Indian culture with over 64,000 aligned text-image pairs in 15 languages; VLMs struggle, especially for low-resource languages and less-documented traditions.
- `2025-09` **[Benchmarking Vision-Language Models on Chinese Ancient Documents: From OCR to Knowledge Reasoning](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1438/)** — Yu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09731)]<br>AncientDoc: benchmark of about 3,000 pages from over 100 Chinese ancient books with five tasks from page-level OCR to knowledge and linguistic-variant QA for VLMs.
- `2025-09` **[A Structured Framework for Evaluating and Enhancing Interpretive Capabilities of Multimodal LLMs in Culturally Situated Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.103/)** — Yu et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23208)]<br>Builds a quantitative framework from expert critiques of traditional Chinese painting and evaluates persona-guided critique generation by VLMs such as Llama, Qwen and Gemini.
- `2025-08` **[WebMMU: A Benchmark for Multimodal Multilingual Website Understanding and Code Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1276/)** — Awal et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16763)]<br>WebMMU: multilingual benchmark for website VQA, HTML/CSS/JavaScript code editing and mockup-to-code generation; MLLMs handle basic extraction but struggle with reasoning, grounding and multilingual content.
- `2025-08` **[MEENA (PersianMMMU): Multimodal-Multilingual Educational Exams for N-level Assessment](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.340/)** — Ghahroodi et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.17290)]<br>MEENA (PersianMMMU): about 7,500 Persian and 3,000 English questions evaluating VLMs on science, reasoning, charts and Persian art and literature, with a bilingual structure for cross-lingual comparison.
- `2025-08` **[Language-Specific Layer Matters: Efficient Multilingual Enhancement for Large Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.666/)** — Fan et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18381)]<br>PLAST: fine-tunes only language-specific shallow layers of LVLMs, found via language-specific neuron activations, on question-translation pairs, improving MM-Bench and MMMB with 14% of parameters tuned.
- `2025-08` **[KRETA: A Benchmark for Korean Reading and Reasoning in Text-Rich VQA Attuned to Diverse Visual Contexts](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1696/)** — Hwang et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19944)]<br>KRETA: Korean text-rich VQA benchmark spanning 15 domains and 26 image types, built with a semi-automated generation pipeline and seven-metric quality protocol.
- `2025-08` **[Grounding Multilingual Multimodal LLMs With Cultural Knowledge](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1232/)** — Nyandwi et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07414)]<br>CulturalGround: 22 million synthetic culturally rich VQA pairs over 42 countries and 39 languages from Wikidata; CulturalPangea trained on it gains 5.0 on culture-focused multilingual multimodal benchmarks.
- `2025-07` **[Meta CLIP 2: A Worldwide Scaling Recipe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22062)** — Chuang et al. · *NeurIPS 2025*<br>Meta CLIP 2: recipe training CLIP from scratch on worldwide image-text data so English and non-English data help each other; beats its English-only counterpart on zero-shot ImageNet.
- `2025-07` **[CONCAP: Seeing Beyond English with Concepts Retrieval-Augmented Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.20411)** — Ibrahim et al. · *COLM 2025*<br>CONCAP: multilingual image captioning model combining retrieved captions with image-specific concepts; on XM3600 it performs strongly on low- and mid-resource languages with much less data.
- `2025-06` **[MUCAR: Benchmarking Multilingual Cross-Modal Ambiguity Resolution for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.760/)** — Wang et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.17046)]<br>MUCAR: multilingual benchmark for cross-modal ambiguity resolution with ambiguous text resolved by images and dual-ambiguity pairs; 19 multimodal models trail humans substantially.
- `2025-06` **[CLAIM: Mitigating Multilingual Object Hallucination in Large Vision-Language Models with Cross-Lingual Attention Intervention](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.640/)** — Ye et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.11073)]<br>CLAIM: near training-free attention intervention for multilingual object hallucination in LVLMs, using English-to-target language shift vectors; averages 13.56% gains on POPE and 21.75% on MME hallucination subsets.
- `2025-05` **[Uncovering Cultural Representation Disparities in Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-ijcnlp.131/)** — Kadiyala et al. · *Findings of IJCNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14729)]<br>Probes VLMs on Country211 country identification with open-ended, multiple-choice, multilingual and adversarial prompts, finding large accuracy disparities across countries, suggesting biases inherited from pretraining data.
- `2025-05` **[TCC-Bench: Benchmarking the Traditional Chinese Culture Understanding Capabilities of MLLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.1219/)** — Xu et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11275)]<br>TCC-Bench: bilingual Chinese-English VQA benchmark on traditional Chinese culture with museum artifacts, daily scenes and comics; MLLMs struggle with culturally grounded visual reasoning.
- `2025-05` **[LGVLM-mIoT: A Lightweight Generative Visual-Language Model for Multilingual IoT Applications](https://doi.org/10.1109/JIOT.2025.3532494)** — Weng et al. · *IEEE Internet of Things Journal 2025*<br>LGVLM-mIoT: lightweight generative visual-language model for multilingual IoT edge devices that maps English and non-English features into one space aligned with a visually distilled model.
- `2025-05` **[CCHall: A Novel Benchmark for Joint Cross-Lingual and Cross-Modal Hallucinations Detection in Large Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1485/)** — Zhang et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19108)]<br>CCHall: benchmark for hallucination detection in joint cross-lingual and cross-modal scenarios; mainstream open- and closed-source LLMs still struggle on it.
