- `2025-05` **[Aya Vision: Advancing the Frontier of Multilingual Multimodality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.08751)** — Dash et al.<br>Aya Vision: 8B and 32B multilingual vision-language models built with synthetic multilingual multimodal instruction data and cross-modal merging; Aya-Vision-8B beats Qwen-2.5-VL-7B and Llama-3.2-90B-Vision.
- `2025-04` **[Kaleidoscope: In-language Exams for Massively Multilingual Vision Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07072)** — Salazar et al. · *ICLR 2026*<br>Kaleidoscope: in-language multimodal exam benchmark of 20,911 multiple-choice questions in 18 languages and 14 subjects; VLMs perform poorly on low-resource languages.
- `2025-03` **[KwaiChat: A Large-Scale Video-Driven Multilingual Mixed-Type Dialogue Corpus](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.121/)** — Shi et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06899)]<br>KwaiChat: video-driven multilingual mixed-type dialogue corpus of 93,209 videos and 246,080 dialogues in 4 languages; GPT-4o performs best but still poorly.
- `2025-03` **[JEEM: Vision-Language Understanding in Four Arabic Dialects](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-eacl.18/)** — Kadaoui et al. · *Findings of EACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21910)]<br>JEEM: image captioning and VQA benchmark in four Arabic dialects (Jordan, Emirates, Egypt, Morocco); Arabic VLMs underperform and GPT-4V's competence varies across dialects.
- `2025-03` **[Benchmarking and Boosting Multilingual Capabilities of LVLMs via OCR-Centric Reinforcement Learning](https://aclanthology.org/2026.emnlp-main.666/)** — Gao et al. · *EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18484)]<br>PM4Bench: multimodal multi-task benchmark on a strictly parallel 10-language corpus; finds OCR drives cross-lingual gaps in 10 LVLMs and proposes OCR-centric GRPO training on synthetic data.
- `2025-02` **[When Cultures Meet: Multicultural Text-to-Image Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1783/)** — Bhalerao et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15972)]<br>Introduces multicultural text-to-image generation with a 9,000-image benchmark over five countries and five languages, plus MosAIG, a multi-agent framework using LLMs with cultural personas.
- `2025-02` **[SigLIP 2: Multilingual Vision-Language Encoders with Improved Semantic Understanding, Localization, and Dense Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14786)** — Tschannen et al.<br>SigLIP 2: multilingual vision-language encoders (86M-1B) adding captioning-based pretraining, self-supervised losses and online data curation; a more diverse, de-biased data mixture improves multilingual understanding and fairness.
- `2025-02` **[Scaling Pre-training to One Hundred Billion Data for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07617)** — Wang et al. · *CVPR 2025*<br>Pre-training vision-language models on 100 billion web examples saturates Western-centric benchmarks but improves cultural-diversity tasks and low-resource languages; CLIP-based quality filtering can reduce cultural diversity.
- `2025-02` **[MVL-SIB: A Massively Multilingual Vision-Language Benchmark for Cross-Modal Topical Matching](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.838/)** — Schmidt et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12852)]<br>MVL-SIB: cross-modal and text-only topical matching benchmark in 205 languages; LVLMs fall to chance on languages like N'Koo and their VL support lags text support for low-resource languages.
- `2025-02` **[GIMMICK: Globally Inclusive Multimodal Multitask Cultural Knowledge Benchmarking](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.500/)** — Schneider et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13766)]<br>GIMMICK: six-task multimodal benchmark on 728 cultural events or facets from 144 countries; evaluating 20 LVLMs and 11 LLMs reveals strong Western bias and size-performance correlation.
- `2025-02` **[Beyond Words: Exploring Cultural Value Sensitivity in Multimodal Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.422/)** — Yadav et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.14906)]<br>Evaluates multimodal models of different scales on cultural value alignment; like LLMs, VLMs are sensitive to cultural values, but alignment is highly context-dependent and images help unevenly.
- `2025-02` **[AIN: The Arabic INclusive Large Multimodal Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00094)** — Heakl et al.<br>AIN: English-Arabic bilingual multimodal model trained on 3.6 million Arabic-English samples; the 7B model beats GPT-4o by 3.4% on CAMEL-Bench averaged over eight domains.
- `2025-01` **[Centurio: On Drivers of Multilingual Ability of Large Vision-Language Model](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.143/)** — Geigle et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05122)]<br>Studies training mixes for LVLMs over 13 tasks and 43 languages; 100 training languages with 25-50% non-English data improve multilinguality while keeping English, yielding Centurio, a 100-language LVLM.
- `2025` **[What Is Missing in Multilingual Visual Reasoning and How to Fix It](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.144/)** — Song et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025*<br>Evaluates multilingual multimodal models on a visual reasoning task: GPT-4V performs similarly across languages while open models lag; translate-test, visual programming and captioning interventions boost LLaVA and Qwen-VL.
- `2025` **[TaiwanVQA: Benchmarking and Enhancing Cultural Understanding in Vision-Language Models](https://openreview.net/forum?id=atofIc3x1q)** — Hsieh et al. · *NeurIPS 2025 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>TaiwanVQA: 2,736 images and 5,472 questions on Taiwanese culture; VLMs recognize visuals well but reason poorly about culture, and fine-tuning with human and synthesized dialogues helps.
- `2025` **[TVQACML: Benchmarking Text-Centric Visual Question Answering in Multilingual Chinese Minority Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.705/)** — Jiu et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>TVQACML: contamination-free text-centric VQA benchmark in eight languages, including Standard Chinese, Korean and six Chinese minority languages, with 32,000 QA pairs over 8,000 images.
- `2025` **[Seeing Culture: A Benchmark for Visual Reasoning and Grounding](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1131/)** — Satar et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>Seeing Culture Benchmark: 1,065 images of 138 cultural artifacts from seven Southeast Asian countries with 3,178 questions requiring VLMs to pick and then segment cultural evidence.
- `2025` **[RusCode: Russian Cultural Code Benchmark for Text-to-Image Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.425/)** — Vasilev et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025*<br>RusCode: 1,250 Russian prompts with English translations across 19 categories of Russian visual culture, with human side-by-side evaluation of how text-to-image models represent Russian cultural concepts.
- `2025` **[PunMemeCN: A Benchmark to Explore Vision-Language Models’ Understanding of Chinese Pun Memes](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.944/)** — Xu et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>PunMemeCN: 1,959 Chinese memes (653 pun memes) plus 2,008 multi-turn chats for detection, sentiment and response tasks; VLMs struggle, especially with homophone wordplay, even with chain-of-thought.
- `2025` **[Persian in a Court: Benchmarking VLMs In Persian Multi-Modal Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2025.evalmg-1.5/)** — Farsi et al. · *EVALMG 2025 (workshop)*<br>Introduces Persian multimodal datasets (Persian-OCR-QA, Persian-VQA, world-image puzzles, Visual-Abstraction-Reasoning, Iran-places) and evaluates GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet and Llama 3.2 90B Vision on them.
- `2025` **[Pearl: A Multimodal Culturally-Aware Arabic Instruction Dataset](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.1254/)** — Alwajih et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025*<br>PEARL: over 309K Arabic multimodal examples on ten cultural domains across all Arab countries, with two benchmarks and a PEARL-X subset; reasoning-centric instruction alignment improves cultural grounding more than scaling.
- `2025` **[Navigating Cultural Chasms: Exploring and Unlocking the Cultural POV of Text-To-Image Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.tacl-1.10/)** — Ventura et al. · *TACL 2025*<br>Probes cultural knowledge in text-to-image models via cultural dimensions, domains and concepts, and introduces CulText2I, images from six TTI models across ten languages.
- `2025` **[MuLan: Adapting Multilingual Diffusion Models for Hundreds of Languages with Negligible Cost](https://openreview.net/forum?id=ENSibPAjks)** — Xing et al. · *ICML 2025*<br>MuLan: a language adapter under 20M parameters on a frozen text encoder and diffusion model enables text-to-image generation in over 110 languages from English data.
- `2025` **[MCS-Bench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Evaluating Multimodal Large Language Models in Chinese Classical Studies](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.515/)** — Liu et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>MCS-Bench: multimodal benchmark of 45 tasks on Chinese Classical Studies (ancient text, calligraphy, oracle bone script and more); the best of 37 MLLMs averages below 50.
- `2025` **[Hanfu-Bench: A Multimodal Benchmark on Cross-Temporal Cultural Understanding and Transcreation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1251/)** — Zhou et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>Hanfu-Bench: expert-curated benchmark on traditional Chinese garments testing temporal cultural understanding and image transcreation; closed VLMs trail human experts by 10%, best transcreation success is 42%.
- `2025` **[Evaluating Visual and Cultural Interpretation: The K-Viscuit Benchmark with Human-VLM Collaboration](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1066/)** — Park et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>K-Viscuit: Korean-culture multiple-choice VQA benchmark built via human-VLM collaboration and native-speaker verification; open-source VLMs lag behind proprietary ones on Korean culture.
- `2025` **[Diffusion Models Through a Global Lens: Are They Culturally Inclusive?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.1503/)** — Bayramli et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>CULTDIFF: benchmark testing whether text-to-image diffusion models generate culturally specific images for ten countries; they often fail on architecture, clothing and food of underrepresented regions; adds CULTDIFF-S metric.
- `2025` **[Culture-TRIP: Culturally-Aware Text-to-Image Generation with Iterative Prompt Refinement](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.483/)** — Jeong et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16902)]<br>Culture-TRIP: iteratively refines text-to-image prompts with retrieved cultural context and LLM-based criteria; a 66-participant survey from eight countries shows better alignment for underrepresented culture nouns.
- `2025` **[CulturalFrames: Assessing Cultural Expectation Alignment in Text-to-Image Models and Evaluation Metrics](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.1141/)** — Nayak et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025*<br>CulturalFrames: 983 prompts and 3,637 images from 4 text-to-image models across 10 countries; models miss cultural expectations 44% of the time, and existing metrics correlate poorly with human judgments.
- `2025` **[Crowdsource, Crawl, or Generate? Creating SEA-VL, a Multicultural Vision-Language Dataset for Southeast Asia](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.916/)** — Cahyawijaya et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>SEA-VL: 1.28 million culturally relevant Southeast Asian images collected by crowdsourcing, crawling and generation; crawling reaches ~85% cultural relevance while synthetic images miss cultural nuances.
- `2025` **[Cross-Lingual Adaptation for Vision-Language Model via Multimodal Semantic Distillation](https://doi.org/10.1109/TMM.2025.3557678)** — Weng et al. · *IEEE transactions on multimedia 2025*<br>SMSA: syntax-aware adapter plus multimodal semantic distillation extends an MoE vision-language model to 8 languages with a small translation dataset, showing strong zero-shot transfer on IGLUE.
- `2025` **[Creating a Lens of Chinese Culture: A Multimodal Dataset for Chinese Pun Rebus Art Understanding](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1155/)** — Zhang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>Pun Rebus Art Dataset: Chinese pun rebus artworks with element identification, symbol matching and explanation tasks; VLMs often give biased, hallucinated explanations and gain little from in-context learning.
- `2025` **[CROPE: Evaluating In-Context Adaptation of Vision and Language Models to Culture-Specific Concepts](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.402/)** — Nikandrou et al. · *NAACL 2025*<br>CROPE: VQA benchmark probing culture-specific concepts in VLMs, separating parametric from contextual knowledge; open VLMs show large gaps between culture-specific and common concepts.
- `2025` **[AlignMMBench: Evaluating Chinese Multimodal Alignment in Large Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.327/)** — Wu et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>AlignMMBench: 1,054 images and 4,978 QA pairs across 13 tasks evaluating VLM alignment in Chinese visual contexts, with the CritiqueVLM evaluator and a prompt-robustness alignment score.
- `2024-12` **[Maya: An Instruction Finetuned Multilingual Multimodal Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07112)** — Alam et al.<br>Maya: open multilingual vision-language model with an eight-language image-text pretraining set based on LLaVA, plus a toxicity analysis and toxicity-free version of that data.
- `2024-11` **[No Culture Left Behind: ArtELingo-28, a Benchmark of WikiArt with Captions in 28 Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.1165/)** — Mohamed et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.03769)]<br>ArtELingo-28: WikiArt vision-language benchmark with about 200,000 emotional caption annotations in 28 languages; cross-lingual transfer works better between culturally related languages.
- `2024-10` **[WorldCuisines: A Massive-Scale Benchmark for Multilingual and Multicultural Visual Question Answering on Global Cuisines](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.167/)** — Winata et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12705)]<br>WorldCuisines: multicultural VQA benchmark on global dishes with over 1 million data points across 30 languages and dialects; VLMs struggle with adversarial contexts and specific regional cuisines.
- `2024-10` **[Quantifying the Gaps Between Translation and Native Perception in Training for Multimodal, Multilingual Retrieval](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.335/)** — Buettner & Kovashka · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02027)]<br>Shows multilingual vision-language retrieval models trained on captions translated from English into German underperform those trained on native German captions; caption augmentation narrows but does not close the gap.
- `2024-10` **[Pangea: A Fully Open Multilingual Multimodal LLM for 39 Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16153)** — Yue et al.<br>Pangea: multilingual multimodal LLM trained on PangeaIns, 6M instructions in 39 languages, with PangeaBench (14 datasets, 47 languages); outperforms open-source models in multilingual and cultural settings.
- `2024-10` **[MultiVENT 2.0: A Massive Multilingual Benchmark for Event-Centric Video Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11619)** — Kriz et al. · *CVPR 2024*<br>MultiVENT 2.0: multilingual event-centric video retrieval benchmark with over 218,000 news videos and over 3,900 queries; vision-language models struggle significantly.
- `2024-10` **[JMMMU: A Japanese Massive Multi-discipline Multimodal Understanding Benchmark for Culture-aware Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.43/)** — Onohara et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17250)]<br>JMMMU: Japanese expert-level multimodal benchmark with a culture-agnostic subset translated from MMMU and a culture-specific subset, revealing LMM drops in Japanese and shallow cultural understanding.
- `2024-10` **[Do Vision-Language Models Represent Space and How? Evaluating Spatial Frame of Reference Under Ambiguities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17385)** — Zhang et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>COMFORT: protocol testing spatial frames of reference in nine VLMs; models lack robustness and fail to follow language- or culture-specific conventions, with English dominating other languages.
- `2024-10` **[Can MLLMs Understand the Deep Implication Behind Chinese Images?](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.700/)** — Zhang et al. · *ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13854)]<br>CII-Bench: Chinese Internet images, including traditional paintings, testing MLLMs on implied meaning; the best MLLM reaches 64.4% versus 78.2% average human accuracy.
- `2024-10` **[CAMEL-Bench: A Comprehensive Arabic LMM Benchmark](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.105/)** — Ghaboura et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18976)]<br>CAMEL-Bench: Arabic multimodal benchmark of around 29,036 questions over eight domains and 38 sub-domains, verified by native speakers; GPT-4o reaches only 62% overall.
- `2024-09` **[Towards Cross-Lingual Explanation of Artwork in Large-scale Vision Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.209/)** — Ozaki et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01584)]<br>MultiExpArt: multilingual artwork-explanation dataset built without machine translation; LVLMs explain worse in non-English languages and struggle to use knowledge learned from English data.
- `2024-07` **[M5 – A Diverse Benchmark to Assess the Performance of Large Multimodal Models Across Multilingual and Multicultural Vision-Language Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.250/)** — Schneider & Sitaram · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03791)]<br>M5: benchmark of eight datasets covering five vision-language tasks and 41 languages; shows disparities between high- and low-resource languages, and larger models not necessarily better.
- `2024-07` **[From Local Concepts to Universals: Evaluating the Multicultural Understanding of Vision-Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.385/)** — Bhatia et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00263)]<br>GlobalRG: benchmark of retrieval across universals with images from 50 countries and cultural visual grounding across 15 countries, showing VLM performance varies significantly across cultures.
- `2024-07` **[Dallah: A Dialect-Aware Multimodal Large Language Model for Arabic](https://aclanthology.org/2024.arabicnlp-1.27/)** — Alwajih et al. · *ARABICNLP 2024 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18129)]<br>Dallah: LLaMA-2-based Arabic multimodal assistant fine-tuned on six Arabic dialects, evaluated on Modern Standard Arabic and dialectal response benchmarks.
- `2024-07` **[CVLUE: A New Benchmark Dataset for Chinese Vision-Language Understanding Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01081)** — Wang et al. · *AAAI 2024*<br>CVLUE: Chinese vision-language benchmark with native-speaker-selected, culturally representative images over four tasks; reveals English-Chinese gaps and missing Chinese cultural knowledge in VLMs.
- `2024-07` **[Beyond Aesthetics: Cultural Competence in Text-to-Image Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06863)** — Kannen et al. · *NeurIPS 2024 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>CUBE: benchmark of cultural artifacts from 8 countries across cuisine, landmarks and art to evaluate cultural awareness and diversity of text-to-image models.
- `2024-07` **[Benchmarking Vision Language Models for Cultural Understanding](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.329/)** — Nayak et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10920)]<br>CulturalVQA: 2,378 image-question pairs on cultures from 11 countries across 5 continents; VLMs are stronger for North America and significantly weaker for Africa.
- `2024-06` **[See It from My Perspective: How Language Affects Cultural Bias in Image Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11665)** — Ananthram et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>Finds VLMs do better on Western than East Asian splits of culturally diverse image tasks; prompting in a culture's language reduces bias most when it was well represented in pretraining.
- `2024-06` **[Parrot: Multilingual Visual Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.02539)** — Sun et al. · *ICML 2025*<br>PARROT: aligns visual tokens per language using textual guidance and Mixture-of-Experts to counter English-centric SFT; also introduces MMMB, a 6-language, 12,000-question multilingual multimodal benchmark.
- `2024-06` **[On Scaling Up a Multilingual Vision and Language Model](https://doi.org/10.1109/CVPR52733.2024.01368)** — Chen et al. · *CVPR 2024*<br>Scales a multilingual vision-language model in component size and task mixture, advancing 20+ benchmarks and showing emergent abilities such as multilingual object detection.
- `2024-06` **[M3GIA: A Cognition Inspired Multilingual and Multimodal General Intelligence Ability Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05343)** — Song et al.<br>M3GIA: cognition-inspired benchmark of five CHC intelligence factors for multimodal LLMs in six languages; the best model reaches the lower boundary of human intelligence in English.
- `2024-06` **[FoodieQA: A Multimodal Dataset for Fine-Grained Understanding of Chinese Food Culture](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.1063/)** — Li et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11030)]<br>FoodieQA: manually curated image-text dataset on regional Chinese food cultures; open VLMs trail humans by 41% on multi-image and 21% on single-image VQA.
- `2024-06` **[CVQA: Culturally-diverse Multilingual Visual Question Answering Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05967)** — Abzaliev et al. · *NeurIPS 2024 (Datasets & Benchmarks)*<br>CVQA: culturally driven VQA benchmark built with native speakers and cultural experts, 10k questions from 30 countries in 31 languages and 13 scripts, challenging for current MLLMs.
- `2024-05` **[MTVQA: Benchmarking Multilingual Text-Centric Visual Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.404/)** — Tang et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11985)]<br>MTVQA: text-centric VQA benchmark with expert annotations in 9 languages (6,778 QA pairs, 2,116 images); Qwen2-VL scores 30.9 versus 79.7 for humans.
- `2024-04` **[Heron-Bench: A Benchmark for Evaluating Vision Language Models in Japanese](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07824)** — Inoue et al.<br>Japanese Heron-Bench: image-question-answer benchmark tailored to Japanese context for VLMs, released with a baseline Japanese VLM trained on Japanese visual instruction data; shows a gap to GPT-4V.
- `2024-04` **[An image speaks a thousand words, but can everyone listen? On image transcreation for cultural relevance](https://aclanthology.org/2024.emnlp-main.573/)** — Khanuja et al. · *EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01247)]<br>Introduces image transcreation for cultural relevance with three generative pipelines and a 600-image concept set plus 100-image application set; best pipelines translate only 5% of images for some countries.
- `2024-03` **[X-LLaVA: Optimizing Bilingual Large Vision-Language Alignment](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.158/)** — Shin et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11399)]<br>X-LLaVA: builds a 91K English-Korean-Chinese multimodal training set with GPT4-V and uses vocabulary expansion and pretraining for a bilingual Korean-English vision-language model.
- `2024-03` **[Peacock: A Family of Arabic Multimodal Large Language Models and Benchmarks](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.689/)** — Alwajih et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01031)]<br>Peacock: family of Arabic multimodal LLMs showing emerging dialectal potential, plus Henna, a benchmark assessing MLLMs on aspects of Arabic culture.
- `2024-03` **[EXAMS-V: A Multi-Discipline Multilingual Multimodal Exam Benchmark for Evaluating Vision Language Models](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.420/)** — Das et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.10378)]<br>EXAMS-V: 20,932 multimodal school-exam questions across 20 disciplines in 11 languages from 7 families; challenging even for GPT-4V and Gemini.
- `2024-02` **[PALO: A Polyglot Large Multimodal Model for 5B People](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14818)** — Rasheed et al.<br>PALO: multilingual multimodal model for 10 languages covering ~5B people, trained at 1.7B, 7B and 13B on semi-automatically translated instructions, plus a multilingual multimodal benchmark.
- `2024` **[PAELLA: Parameter-Efficient Lightweight Language-Agnostic Captioning Model](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-naacl.225/)** — Ramos et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2024*<br>PAELLA: retrieval-augmented multilingual image captioning model that trains only a 34M-parameter mapping network; on XM3600 (36 languages) it competes with models using 3-77x more parameters.
- `2024` **[No Filter: Cultural and Socioeconomic Diversity in Contrastive Vision-Language Models](https://openreview.net/forum?id=UmW9BYj761)** — Pouget et al. · *NeurIPS 2024*<br>Shows filtering training data to English image-text pairs hurts cultural understanding of contrastive VLMs; pretraining on global data first helps, and geo-localization is proposed as an evaluation.
- `2024` **[Multilingual Diversity Improves Vision-Language Representations](https://openreview.net/forum?id=1WtEqReCyS)** — Nguyen et al. · *NeurIPS 2024*<br>Translating non-English web image-text pairs to English and re-filtering improves vision-language models on DataComp, with GeoDE gains in all regions, largest for Africa.
- `2023-11` **[Violet: A Vision-Language Model for Arabic Image Captioning with Gemini Decoder](https://aclanthology.org/2023.arabicnlp-1.1/)** — Mohamed et al. · *ARABICNLP 2023 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.08844)]<br>Violet: Arabic image-captioning vision-language model pairing a vision encoder with a Gemini text decoder, trained on data auto-derived from English datasets; reaches 61.2 CIDEr on a new manual test set.
- `2023-10` **[Qilin-Med-VL: Towards Chinese Large Vision-Language Model for General Healthcare](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.17956)** — Liu et al.<br>Qilin-Med-VL: Chinese medical vision-language model combining a ViT with an LLM via feature alignment and instruction tuning, released with ChiMed-VL, over 1M image-text pairs.
- `2023-08` **[Large Multilingual Models Pivot Zero-Shot Multimodal Learning across Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.12038)** — Hu et al. · *ICLR 2024*<br>MPM: trains non-English multimodal models on English-only image-text data atop a multilingual LLM, which generalize (quasi-)zero-shot to other languages; yields Chinese VisCPM models for image-to-text and text-to-image.
- `2023-07` **[mBLIP: Efficient Bootstrapping of Multilingual Vision-LLMs](https://aclanthology.org/2024.alvr-1.2/)** — Geigle et al. · *ALVR 2024 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.06930)]<br>mBLIP: re-aligns an English-tuned image encoder to a multilingual LLM with a few million examples machine-translated into 95 languages; competitive on IGLUE and XM3600, beating Llava 1.5.
- `2023-06` **[Multilingual Conceptual Coverage in Text-to-Image Models](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-long.266/)** — Saxon & Wang · *ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.01735)]<br>CoCo-CroLa: benchmarks multilingual parity of text-to-image models by comparing images generated for tangible nouns in a source language and their translations.
- `2023-06` **[M$^3$IT: A Large-Scale Dataset towards Multi-Modal Multilingual Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.04387)** — Li et al.<br>M3IT: 40 curated vision-language datasets with 2.4 million instances and 400 manually written instructions, key tasks translated into 80 languages, plus the Ying-VLM model.
- `2023-05` **[PaLI-X: On Scaling up a Multilingual Vision and Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.18565)** — Chen et al.<br>PaLI-X: scaled-up multilingual vision and language model advancing 25+ benchmarks, with emerging capabilities such as complex counting and multilingual object detection.
- `2023-05` **[LMCap: Few-shot Multilingual Image Captioning by Retrieval Augmented Language Model Prompting](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-acl.104/)** — Ramos et al. · *Findings of ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.19821)]<br>LMCap: image-blind few-shot multilingual captioner prompting XGLM with captions retrieved by multilingual CLIP; competitive with supervised captioning models on XM3600 without captioning training.
- `2023` **[mCLIP: Multilingual CLIP via Cross-lingual Transfer](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-long.728/)** — Chen et al. · *ACL 2023*<br>mCLIP: retrieval-efficient dual-stream multilingual vision-language model aligning CLIP with a multilingual text encoder via Triangle Cross-modal Knowledge Distillation, updating only two light projectors.
- `2022-09` **[PaLI: A Jointly-Scaled Multilingual Language-Image Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.06794)** — Chen et al. · *ICLR 2022*<br>PaLI: jointly scaled language-image model combining pretrained encoder-decoder LMs and a 4B-parameter ViT-e, trained on a multilingual task mix with 10B image-texts in over 100 languages.
- `2021-09` **[xGQA: Cross-Lingual Visual Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2022.findings-acl.196/)** — Pfeiffer et al. · *Findings of ACL 2022* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.06082)]<br>xGQA: extends GQA visual question answering to 7 typologically diverse languages, with adapter-based methods; zero-shot cross-lingual transfer drops around 38 accuracy points in target languages.
- `2021-09` **[Visually Grounded Reasoning across Languages and Cultures](https://aclanthology.org/2021.emnlp-main.818/)** — Liu et al. · *EMNLP 2021* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.13238)]<br>MaRVL: multicultural visually grounded reasoning dataset with native-speaker-selected concepts and images in Indonesian, Mandarin Chinese, Swahili, Tamil and Turkish; cross-lingual transfer lags far behind English supervised performance.

## Code & Structured Data

- `2025-09` **[TigerCoder: A Novel Suite of LLMs for Code Generation in Bangla](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-238/)** — Raihan et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09101)]<br>TigerCoder: Bangla code LLM family (1B and 9B) with Bangla code instruction datasets and the MBPP-Bangla benchmark, gaining ~11-18% Pass@1 over multilingual and general-purpose Bangla LLMs.
- `2025-09` **[Multilingual Text-to-SQL: Benchmarking the Limits of Language Models with Collaborative Language Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.24405)** — Pham et al. · *Australasian Database Conference 2025*<br>MultiSpider 2.0: Spider 2.0 text-to-SQL in eight languages; top LLMs reach 4% execution accuracy, raised to 15% by a collaborative language-agent baseline.
- `2025-08` **[M3TQA: Massively Multilingual Multitask Table Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1134/)** — Shu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16265)]<br>m3TQA: table QA benchmark in 97 languages built from 50 Chinese and English tables via an LLM translation pipeline, with 2,916 professionally annotated QA pairs over four tasks.
- `2025-06` **[TableEval: A Real-World Benchmark for Complex, Multilingual, and Multi-Structured Table Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.363/)** — Zhu et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03949)]<br>TableEval: TableQA benchmark of real-world concise, hierarchical and nested tables from four domains in Simplified Chinese, Traditional Chinese and English, with SEAT, a sub-question-level evaluation framework.
- `2025-05` **[CodeMixBench: Evaluating Large Language Models on Code Generation with Code-Mixed Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.05063)** — Sheokand & Sawant<br>CodeMixBench: BigCodeBench with controlled code-mixing in Hinglish, Spanish-English and Chinese Pinyin-English prompts; code-mixed prompts consistently lower Pass@1 for 1.5B-15B code models.
- `2025-05` **[A Qualitative Investigation into LLM-Generated Multilingual Code Comments and Automatic Evaluation Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15469)** — Katzy et al.<br>Open-coding study of code comments generated by five code LLMs in Chinese, Dutch, English, Greek and Polish, releasing 12,500 labeled generations and a 26-category error taxonomy.
- `2025-02` **[MultiTEND: A Multilingual Benchmark for Natural Language to NoSQL Query Translation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.1265/)** — Qin et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11022)]<br>MultiTEND: natural-language-to-NoSQL benchmark in six languages showing low accuracy and a 4%-6% non-English gap, plus MultiLink, a parallel-linking generation framework.
- `2024-12` **[The Rise and Down of Babel Tower: Investigating the Evolution Process of Multilingual Code Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07298)** — Chen et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>Babel Tower Hypothesis from code LLM pretraining: languages first share a knowledge system dominated by the primary language, then develop language-specific ones; used to optimize multilingual code corpora.
- `2024-10` **[mHumanEval - A Multilingual Benchmark to Evaluate Large Language Models for Code Generation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.naacl-long.570/)** — Raihan et al. · *NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15037)]<br>mHumanEval: extends HumanEval to prompts in over 200 natural languages via machine translation with quality checks, plus expert translations for 15, to evaluate code LLMs across languages.
- `2024-06` **[StatBot.Swiss: Bilingual Open Data Exploration in Natural Language](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.326/)** — Nooralahzadeh et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03170)]<br>StatBot.Swiss: bilingual English-German Text-to-SQL benchmark of 455 NL/SQL pairs over 35 databases; GPT-3.5-Turbo and Mixtral-8x7B struggle to generalize with in-context learning.
- `2024-04` **[Exploring Multi-Lingual Bias of Large Code Models in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19368)** — Wang et al.<br>X-HumanEval-X: benchmark revealing pronounced multi-NL and multi-PL bias in nine large code models, which do well with English instructions but may falter with Chinese ones.
- `2024-03` **[Need a Programming Exercise Generated in Your Native Language? ChatGPT's Got Your Back: Automatic Generation of Non-English Programming Exercises Using OpenAI GPT-3.5](https://doi.org/10.1145/3626252.3630897)** — Jordan et al. · *Technical Symposium on Computer Science Education 2024*<br>Prompts GPT-3.5 in English, Tamil, Spanish and Vietnamese to generate programming exercises; most are sensible except Tamil ones, which are mostly non-sensible with lower test pass rates.
- `2024-02` **[HumanEval-XL: A Multilingual Code Generation Benchmark for Cross-lingual Natural Language Generalization](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.735/)** — Peng et al. · *LREC-COLING 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16694)]<br>HumanEval-XL: code generation benchmark linking 23 natural languages and 12 programming languages with 22,080 parallel prompts averaging 8.33 test cases each.
- `2023-10` **[CodeFuse-13B: A Pretrained Multi-lingual Code Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06266)** — Di et al.<br>CodeFuse-13B: open-source code LLM for English and Chinese prompts covering over 40 programming languages, evaluated on HumanEval-x and CodeFuseEval for Chinese prompts.
- `2023-07` **[Multilingual Code Co-Evolution Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.14991)** — Zhang et al. · *ESEC/SIGSOFT FSE 2023*<br>Codeditor: LLM modeling code changes as edit sequences to translate changes across programming languages, evaluated on 6,613 aligned Java-C# code changes from 8 project pairs.
- `2023-06` **[XSemPLR: Cross-Lingual Semantic Parsing in Multiple Natural Languages and Meaning Representations](https://aclanthology.org/2023.acl-long.887/)** — Zhang et al. · *ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.04085)]<br>XSemPLR: cross-lingual semantic parsing benchmark with 22 natural languages and 8 meaning representations from 9 datasets, testing encoder, encoder-decoder and decoder models including Codex and BLOOM.
- `2022-12` **[ERNIE-Code: Beyond English-Centric Cross-lingual Pretraining for Programming Languages](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-acl.676/)** — Chai et al. · *Findings of ACL 2023* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.06742)]<br>ERNIE-Code: pretrained model for 116 natural and 6 programming languages using span-corruption and pivot-based translation language modeling, with zero-shot multilingual code summarization and translation.
- `2022-10` **[XRICL: Cross-lingual Retrieval-Augmented In-Context Learning for Cross-lingual Text-to-SQL Semantic Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.13693)** — Shi et al.<br>XRICL: retrieves English exemplars and adds translation exemplars to prompt LLMs for zero-shot cross-lingual Text-to-SQL; introduces XSpider and XKaggle-dbqa in Chinese, Vietnamese, Farsi and Hindi.
- `2022-10` **[Multi-lingual Evaluation of Code Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.14868)** — Athiwaratkun et al. · *ICLR 2022*<br>MBXP, Multilingual HumanEval and MathQA-X: code generation benchmarks transpiled from Python into over 10 programming languages, showing multilingual models beat monolingual ones and few-shot prompting teaches new languages.
- `2022-08` **[Repair Is Nearly Generation: Multilingual Program Repair with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.11640)** — Joshi et al. · *AAAI 2022*<br>RING: Codex-powered multilingual program repair engine using localization, transformation and candidate-ranking prompts; evaluated on 6 programming languages, beating language-specific engines on three.
- `2022-06` **[Multilingual Code Snippets Training for Program Translation](https://doi.org/10.1609/aaai.v36i10.21434)** — Zhu et al. · *AAAI 2022*<br>CoST: snippet-level parallel code translation dataset across 7 programming languages, plus multilingual snippet denoising and MuST pre-training that helps especially low-resource languages.

## Domain Applications

- `2026-09` **[HerHealthEval: Evaluating Multilingual and Register-Sensitive Understanding of Women's Health Communication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20684)** — Albattra et al.<br>HerHealthEval: matched English, French and Modern Standard Arabic women's-health cases in six communicative forms, testing concern classification, risk calibration, clarification and cross-form consistency.
- `2026-06` **[BRIDGE: benchmarking large language models for understanding real-world clinical practice texts.](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41551-026-01719-2)** — Wu et al. · *Nature 2026*<br>BRIDGE: multilingual clinical benchmark of 87 tasks from 59 real-world sources in 9 languages; evaluating 95 LLMs shows large variation across languages, tasks and clinical specialties.
- `2026-05` **[BioELX: Context-Aware Cross-lingual Biomedical Entity Linking without Task-Specific Supervision](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-emnlp.1109/)** — Wang et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27380)]<br>BioELX: retrieve-rerank cross-lingual biomedical entity linking with Wikidata alias supervision and mention-anchored LLM reranking, improving Recall@1 by 4.8 to 18.2 points on four benchmarks.
- `2026-04` **[Performance benchmarking of LLMs on Chinese national medical licensing education: Cross-lingual and question-type effects](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0346518)** — Tang et al. · *PLoS ONE 2026*<br>Tests six LLMs on 198 English-Chinese question pairs from the Chinese medical licensing exam, finding a significant effect of question type but no main effect of language.
- `2026-02` **[The CLEF-2026 FinMMEval Lab: Multilingual and Multimodal Evaluation of Financial AI Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.10886)** — Xie et al. · *European Conference on Information Retrieval 2026*<br>FinMMEval: CLEF 2026 lab for multilingual and multimodal evaluation of financial LLMs with three tasks: financial exam QA, multilingual financial QA (PolyFiQA) and financial decision making.
- `2026-02` **[MedErrBench: A Fine-Grained Multilingual Benchmark for Medical Error Detection and Correction with Clinical Expert Annotations](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.573/)** — Ma et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05692)]<br>MedErrBench: clinician-annotated benchmark for medical error detection, localization and correction in English, Arabic and Chinese over ten error types, evaluated on general, language-specific and medical LMs.
- `2026-02` **[Large language models for frontline healthcare support in low-resource settings](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44360-025-00038-1)** — Rutunda et al. · *Nature 2026*<br>Collects 5,609 clinical questions from 101 Rwandan community health workers; five LLMs outperformed local clinicians, with slight degradation when communicating in Kinyarwanda.
- `2026-02` **[Large Language Models for Mental Health: A Multilingual Evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2026.loreslm-1.29/)** — Raihan et al. · *LORESLM 2026 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02440)]<br>Evaluates proprietary and open LLMs on eight multilingual mental health datasets and their machine-translated versions; performance on MT data is lower, varying by language and typology.
- `2026-02` **[Cross-Lingual Empirical Evaluation of Large Language Models for Arabic Medical Tasks](https://aclanthology.org/2026.healing-1.13/)** — Abouzahir et al. · *HEALING 2026 (workshop)* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05374)]<br>Compares LLMs on Arabic and English medical QA, finding a language-driven gap that widens with task complexity, Arabic tokenization fragmentation, and confidence weakly correlated with correctness.
- `2026-01` **[Toward Global Large Language Models in Medicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.02186)** — Yang et al.<br>GlobMed: over 800,000 medical entries in 20 languages; GlobMed-Bench evaluates 56 LLMs in 12 languages, and GlobMed-LLMs (1.7B-8B) improve on baselines by nearly 40% on average.
- `2026-01` **[From RAG to Agentic RAG for Faithful Islamic Question Answering](https://aclanthology.org/2026.findings-acl.1317/)** — Bhatia et al. · *Findings of ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07528)]<br>IslamicFaithQA: 3,810-item bilingual Arabic/English generative benchmark measuring hallucination and abstention, with Arabic SFT data, preference data, a Qur'an retrieval corpus and agentic RAG.
- `2026-01` **[CURE-Med: Curriculum-Informed Reinforcement Learning for Multilingual Medical Reasoning](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.1416/)** — Onyame et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13262)]<br>CUREMED-BENCH: medical reasoning dataset in 13 languages, plus CURE-MED, curriculum RL with code-switching-aware SFT and GRPO, reaching 94.96% language consistency and 70.04% logical correctness at 32B.
- `2026` **[MultiFinBen: Benchmarking Large Language Models for Multilingual and Multimodal Financial Application](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.770/)** — Peng et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.14028)]<br>MultiFinBen: expert-annotated financial benchmark in five languages across text, vision and audio; GPT-4o reaches 46.01% overall and drops sharply in multilingual settings.
- `2026` **[Can Continual Pretraining Bridge the Performance Gap between General-purpose and Specialized Language Models in the Medical Domain?](https://aclanthology.org/2026.acl-long.17/)** — Doll et al. · *ACL 2026* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19394)]<br>Builds FineMed-de, a German medical corpus from FineWeb2, and continually pretrains and merges 7B-24B LLMs into DeFineMed; merging restores instruction following but adds language mixing.
- `2025-12` **[CLINIC: Evaluating Multilingual Trustworthiness in Language Models for Healthcare](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.11437)** — Ghosh et al. · *ICML 2026*<br>CLINIC: healthcare trustworthiness benchmark of 18 tasks in 15 languages covering truthfulness, fairness, safety, robustness and privacy; LMs struggle with factual correctness and show bias.
- `2025-10` **[Evaluating human-in-the-loop strategies for artificial intelligence-enabled translation of patient discharge instructions: a multidisciplinary analysis](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41746-025-02055-6)** — Brewster et al. · *npj Digital Medicine 2025*<br>Compares ChatGPT-4o, professional and human-in-the-loop translations of discharge instructions in six languages; ChatGPT-4o rates poorest for Armenian and Somali, while post-editing matches professionals.
- `2025-10` **[Cross-Lingual Stability and Bias in Instruction-Tuned Language Models for Humanitarian NLP](https://aclanthology.org/https://lrec.elra.info/lrec2026-main-346/)** — Nemkova et al. · *LREC 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.22823)]<br>Compares six commercial and open-weight LLMs on human-rights-violation detection in seven languages (78,000 inferences); aligned models stay stable across languages, open-weight models drift.
- `2025-10` **[Consistent performance of large language models in rare disease diagnosis across ten languages and 4917 cases](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ebiom.2025.105957)** — Chimirri et al. · *EBioMedicine 2025*<br>Tests GPT-4o and Meditron3-70B on 4917 rare-disease clinical vignettes (360 genetic diseases) prompted in ten languages, finding consistent differential-diagnosis performance across languages.
- `2025-09` **[Evaluating the Limits of Large Language Models in Multilingual Legal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22472)** — Ioannou et al.<br>Evaluates LLaMA and Gemini on multilingual legal and general benchmarks with adversarial perturbations; legal accuracy often falls below 50%, and per-language performance correlates with syntactic similarity to English.
- `2025-07` **[Linguistic Diversity and Mental Well-Being: Co-Designing Custom AI Chatbots with Multilingual Mothers](https://doi.org/10.1145/3719160.3736615)** — El-Wahsh et al.<br>Co-design sessions with multilingual mothers on mental well-being chatbots reveal code-switching, preference for first-language emotion regulation, and criticism of translation errors and missing dialect nuances.
- `2025-06` **[UniversalCEFR: Enabling Open Multilingual Research on Language Proficiency Assessment](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.491/)** — Imperial et al. · *EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01419)]<br>UniversalCEFR: 505,807 CEFR-labeled texts in 13 languages for proficiency assessment; benchmarks feature-based classifiers, fine-tuned LLMs and prompted instruction-tuned LLMs, favoring features and fine-tuning.
- `2025-06` **[Performance of Large Language Models in the Non-English Context: Qualitative Study of Models Trained on Different Languages in Chinese Medical Examinations](https://doi.org/10.2196/69485)** — Yao et al. · *JMIR Medical Informatics 2025*<br>Evaluates 7 LLMs (2 English-trained, 5 Chinese-trained) on the Chinese National Medical Licensing Examination; Baichuan scores highest at 86.67% zero-shot, ahead of ChatGPT at 83.83%.
- `2025-05` **[Speech translation for multilingual medical education leveraged by large language models](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2025.103147)** — Iranzo-Sánchez et al. · *Artif. Intell. Medicine 2025*<br>Adapts ASR and MT models, including multilingual decoder-only LLMs, to translate English oncology training videos into French, Spanish, German and Slovene; releases the 745-hour ESO speech dataset.
- `2025-05` **[A Japanese Language Model and Three New Evaluation Benchmarks for Pharmaceutical NLP](https://aclanthology.org/2025.ijcnlp-long.72/)** — Ono et al. · *IJCNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16661)]<br>Japanese pharmaceutical LLM continually pretrained on 2 billion Japanese and 8 billion English tokens, with YakugakuQA, NayoseQA and SogoCheck benchmarks; even GPT-4o struggles on SogoCheck.
- `2025-04` **[Multilingual Performance Biases of Large Language Models in Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17720)** — Gupta et al.<br>Evaluates LLMs on four educational tasks in English plus eight languages; performance roughly tracks training-data representation, with frequent significant drops from English for lower-resource languages.
- `2025-03` **[MKG-Rank: Enhancing Large Language Models with Knowledge Graph for Multilingual Medical Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.16131)** — Li et al.<br>MKG-Rank: integrates English medical knowledge graphs into LLMs via word-level translation and cached ranking, raising medical QA accuracy by up to 35.03% in Chinese, Japanese, Korean and Swahili.
- `2025-03` **[Low-resource Information Extraction with the European Clinical Case Corpus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.20568)** — Ghosh et al.<br>E3C-3.0: clinical cases annotated with diseases and test-result relations, native in five languages and LLM-projected into five more; fine-tuning LLMs helps and cross-lingual transfer offsets data scarcity.
- `2025-01` **[Multilingual Performance of a Multimodal Artificial Intelligence System on Multisubject Physics Concept Inventories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06143)** — Kortemeyer et al. · *Physical Review Physics Education Research 2025*<br>Tests GPT-4o on image-based physics concept inventories in multiple languages; English and European languages score highest, while item difficulty is largely independent of survey language.
- `2025-01` **[Do LLMs Provide Consistent Answers to Health-Related Questions across Languages?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14719)** — Schlicht et al. · *European Conference on Information Retrieval 2025*<br>Extends HealthFC with disease categories and Turkish and Chinese translations, finding significant inconsistencies in LLM health answers across English, German, Turkish and Chinese.
- `2025` **[MFinMeeting: A Multilingual, Multi-Sector, and Multi-Task Financial Meeting Understanding Evaluation Dataset](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-acl.14/)** — Zhu et al. · *Findings of ACL 2025*<br>MFinMeeting: English, Chinese and Japanese financial-meeting benchmark across GICS sectors with summarization, QA-pair extraction and QA tasks; seven tested LLMs, even long-context ones, show large room for improvement.
- `2025` **[Beyond Human Labels: A Multi-Linguistic Auto-Generated Benchmark for Evaluating Large Language Models on Resume Parsing](https://aclanthology.org/2025.emnlp-main.1626/)** — Ling et al. · *EMNLP 2025*<br>ResumeBench: 2,500 synthetic resumes across 50 templates, 30 career fields and 5 languages; evaluating 24 LLMs shows even GPT-4o struggles with cross-lingual structural alignment.
- `2025` **[AfriMed-QA: A Pan-African, Multi-Specialty, Medical Question-Answering Benchmark Dataset](https://aclanthology.org/2025.acl-long.96/)** — Nimo et al. · *ACL 2025*<br>AfriMed-QA: 15,000 Pan-African English medical questions from over 60 medical schools in 16 countries across 32 specialties; evaluating 30 LLMs shows wide variation across specialties and geographies.
- `2024-12` **[Polish-English medical knowledge transfer: A new benchmark and results](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.480/)** — Grzybowski et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00559)]<br>Benchmark of over 24,000 Polish medical licensing and specialization exam questions with a professionally translated Polish-English parallel subset, evaluating general, medical and Polish LLMs.
- `2024-12` **[Multi-OphthaLingua: A Multilingual Benchmark for Assessing and Debiasing LLM Ophthalmological QA in LMICs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14304)** — Restrepo et al. · *AAAI 2024*<br>Multi-OphthaLingua: ophthalmology QA benchmark parallel across 7 languages; 6 LLMs show substantial cross-language bias, which CLARA, a cross-lingual reflective agentic method with RAG, reduces.
- `2024-12` **[Large Language Model Ability to Translate CT and MRI Free-Text Radiology Reports Into Multiple Languages.](https://doi.org/10.1148/radiol.241736)** — Meddeb et al. · *Radiology 2024*<br>Evaluates ten LLMs translating 100 synthetic CT and MRI radiology reports into nine languages against radiologist translations; GPT-4 gives the best overall translation quality.
- `2024-12` **[Evaluating ChatGPT's Multilingual Performance in Clinical Nutrition Advice Using Synthetic Medical Text: Insights from Central Asia.](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.tjnut.2024.12.018)** — Adilmetova et al. · *Journal of NutriLife 2024*<br>Evaluates ChatGPT-4 nutrition advice for 50 mock patient profiles in English, Russian and Kazakh; English and Russian score moderately while Kazakh outputs were unsuitable for evaluation.
- `2024-12` **[BiMediX2 : Bio-Medical EXpert LMM for Diverse Medical Modalities](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.756/)** — Mullappilly et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07769)]<br>BiMediX2: bilingual Arabic-English medical multimodal model trained on BiMed-V (1.6M samples), with BiMed-MBench, the first Arabic-English medical LMM benchmark; beats prior methods by over 9% (English) and 20% (Arabic).
- `2024-10` **[WorldMedQA-V: a multilingual, multimodal medical examination dataset for multimodal language models evaluation](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.402/)** — Matos et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12722)]<br>WorldMedQA-V: 568 multiple-choice medical exam questions with images from Brazil, Israel, Japan and Spain, in original languages and clinician-validated English, for evaluating VLMs.
- `2024-10` **[Unlocking Legal Knowledge: A Multilingual Dataset for Judicial Summarization in Switzerland](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-emnlp.832/)** — Rolshoven et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13456)]<br>SLDS: 20K Swiss Federal Supreme Court rulings with headnotes in German, French and Italian; fine-tuned open models score well lexically, while larger LLMs write more legally accurate summaries.
- `2024-10` **[Performance of ChatGPT on Nursing Licensure Examinations in the United States and China: Cross-Sectional Study](https://doi.org/10.2196/52746)** — Wu et al. · *JMIR Medical Education 2024*<br>Compares ChatGPT 4.0, ChatGPT 3.5 and Bard on US and Chinese nursing licensure questions in original and translated English/Chinese versions, assessing LLMs as multilingual nursing learning aids.
- `2024-10` **[LlamaLens: Specialized Multilingual LLM for Analyzing News and Social Media Content](https://aclanthology.org/2025.findings-naacl.313/)** — Kmainasi et al. · *Findings of NAACL 2025* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15308)]<br>LlamaLens: multilingual LLM for news and social media analysis covering 18 tasks and 52 datasets in Arabic, English and Hindi; beats previous best results on 23 test sets.
- `2024-10` **[HEALTH-PARIKSHA: Assessing RAG Models for Health Chatbots in Real-World Multilingual Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13671)** — Gumma et al.<br>HEALTH-PARIKSHA: evaluates 24 LLMs with RAG on real patient queries in Indian English and 4 Indic languages; Indic instruction-tuned models underperform and factual correctness drops for Indic queries.
- `2024-10` **[Efficiently Democratizing Medical LLMs for 50 Languages via a Mixture of Language Family Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10626)** — Zheng et al. · *ICLR 2025*<br>Builds a medical dataset and Post-MoE routing with language-family experts to scale medical LLMs to 50 languages, finding cross-lingual flow in early layers and language-specific divergence later.
- `2024-08` **[GPT is an effective tool for multilingual psychological text analysis](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2308950121)** — Rathje et al.<br>Shows GPT accurately detects psychological constructs in text across 12 languages with simple prompts and no training data, including several lesser-spoken languages.
- `2024-07` **[IL-TUR: Benchmark for Indian Legal Text Understanding and Reasoning](https://aclanthology.org/2024.acl-long.618/)** — Joshi et al. · *ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05399)]<br>IL-TUR: Indian legal understanding and reasoning benchmark with monolingual (English, Hindi) and multilingual (9 Indian languages) tasks, baselines including LLMs, and a public leaderboard.
- `2024-06` **[Transcending Language Barriers: Can ChatGPT Be the Key to Enhancing Multilingual Accessibility in Healthcare?](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jacr.2024.05.009)** — Gulati et al. · *Journal of the American College of Radiology 2024*<br>Has ChatGPT-4 simplify and translate 50 CT reports into Spanish, Hindi and Russian; Hindi versions score significantly worse than English in all four categories.
- `2024-06` **[Performance of ChatGPT and Google Translate for Pediatric Discharge Instruction Translation.](https://doi.org/10.1542/peds.2023-065573)** — Brewster et al. · *Pediatrics 2024*<br>Compares ChatGPT-4.0 and Google Translate with professional translation of 20 pediatric discharge instructions into Spanish, Brazilian Portuguese and Haitian Creole; both make more clinically significant errors for Haitian Creole.
- `2024-05` **[MedMT5: An Open-Source Multilingual Text-to-Text LLM for the Medical Domain](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.974/)** — García-Ferrero et al. · *LREC-COLING 2024*<br>Medical mT5: open-source multilingual text-to-text medical model trained on a medical corpus in English, French, Italian and Spanish, with two new benchmarks for all four languages.
- `2024-05` **[Bridging large language model disparities: Skill tagging of multilingual educational content](https://doi.org/10.1111/bjet.13465)** — Kwak & Pardos · *British Journal of Educational Technology 2024*<br>Measures LLM disparities in tagging K-12 content with curriculum skills across the USA, Ireland, South Korea and India-Maharashtra; fine-tuning GPT-3.5 on few examples helps even underrepresented languages.
- `2024-04` **[Medical mT5: An Open-Source Multilingual Text-to-Text LLM for The Medical Domain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07613)** — García-Ferrero et al.<br>Medical mT5: open-source text-to-text model trained on a medical corpus in English, French, Italian and Spanish, with two new evaluation benchmarks for the four languages.
- `2024-04` **[MedExpQA: Multilingual Benchmarking of Large Language Models for Medical Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05590)** — Alonso et al. · *Artif. Intell. Medicine 2024*<br>MedExpQA: multilingual medical-exam QA benchmark with doctor-written gold explanations for correct and incorrect options; experiments with gold explanations and RAG show best LLM accuracy around 75.
- `2024-04` **[Introducing L2M3, A Multilingual Medical Large Language Model to Advance Health Equity in Low-Resource Regions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08705)** — Gangavarapu<br>L2M3: medical LLM integrated with machine translation models for Community Health Workers in low- and middle-income countries, fine-tuned on open-source datasets with safety features against misinformation.
- `2024-03` **[No Language is an Island: Unifying Chinese and English in Financial Large Language Models, Instruction Data, and Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06249)** — Hu et al.<br>ICE-PIXIU: bilingual Chinese-English financial suite combining the ICE-INTENT model, cross-lingual and multimodal instruction data, and the ICE-FLARE benchmark of 10 NLP and 20 bilingual tasks.
- `2024-03` **[Apollo: A Lightweight Multilingual Medical LLM towards Democratizing Medical AI to 6B People](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03640)** — Wang et al.<br>Apollo: medical LLMs (0.5B–7B) for the six most spoken languages, with the ApolloCorpora dataset and XMedBench benchmark; Apollo-7B leads multilingual medical models up to 70B.
- `2024-02` **[Towards Building Multilingual Language Model for Medicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13963)** — Qiu et al. · *Nature 2024*<br>Builds MMedC, a 25.5B-token medical corpus in 6 main languages, and MMedBench, a multilingual medical QA benchmark with rationales; the 8B MMed-Llama 3 rivals GPT-4.
- `2024-02` **[MoZIP: A Multilingual Benchmark to Evaluate Large Language Models in Intellectual Property](https://aclanthology.org/2024.lrec-main.1018/)** — Ni et al. · *LREC-COLING 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16389)]<br>MoZIP: multilingual intellectual-property benchmark (IPQuiz, IPQA, PatentMatch) plus MoZi, a BLOOMZ model fine-tuned on multilingual IP text that beats BLOOMZ, BELLE and ChatGLM but trails ChatGPT.
- `2024-02` **[Few-shot clinical entity recognition in English, French and Spanish: masked language models outperform generative model prompting](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.400/)** — Naguib et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12801)]<br>Compares 13 prompted autoregressive LLMs with 16 fine-tuned masked models on 14 NER datasets in English, French and Spanish; masked models win in the clinical domain.
- `2024-02` **[BioMistral: A Collection of Open-Source Pretrained Large Language Models for Medical Domains](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-acl.348/)** — Labrak et al. · *Findings of ACL 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10373)]<br>BioMistral: Mistral-based biomedical LLM pretrained on PubMed Central, evaluated on 10 English medical QA tasks automatically translated into 7 other languages for multilingual evaluation.
- `2024-02` **[BiMediX: Bilingual Medical Mixture of Experts LLM](https://aclanthology.org/2024.findings-emnlp.989/)** — Pieri et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2024* · [[arXiv](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13253)]<br>BiMediX: English-Arabic medical mixture-of-experts LLM trained on BiMed1.3M, a 1.3 million-example bilingual medical instruction set, with an Arabic medical evaluation benchmark.
- `2023-12` **[How does ChatGPT-4 preform on non-English national medical licensing examination? An evaluation in Chinese language](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pdig.0000397)** — Fang et al. · *PLOS Digital Health 2023*<br>Tests ChatGPT-4 on the Chinese National Medical Licensing Examination: it scores 442/600 on original Chinese questions, and translating case questions into English yields only marginal gains.
- `2023-11` **[Taiyi: A Bilingual Fine-Tuned Large Language Model for Diverse Biomedical Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.11608)** — Luo et al. · *J. Am. Medical Informatics Assoc. 2023*<br>Taiyi: bilingual English-Chinese biomedical LLM fine-tuned on instruction data from 140 datasets (102 English, 38 Chinese) across over 10 task types, outperforming general LLMs.
- `2023-10` **[Better to Ask in English: Cross-Lingual Evaluation of Large Language Models for Healthcare Queries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.13132)** — Jin et al. · *The Web Conference 2023*<br>XlingEval: evaluates LLM answers to health questions in English, Spanish, Chinese and Hindi for correctness, consistency and verifiability, finding large cross-language disparities; proposes the XLingHealth benchmark.
- `2023-09` **[Artificial Intelligence in Medical Education: Comparative Analysis of ChatGPT, Bing, and Medical Students in Germany](https://doi.org/10.2196/46482)** — Roos et al. · *JMIR Medical Education 2023*<br>Compares GPT-4, Bing and GPT-3.5-Turbo with students on 630 questions from the 2022 German Medical State Examinations; GPT-4 scores 88.1% and Bing 86.0%, beating students' 74.6%.
- `2023-08` **[Uncovering Language Disparity of ChatGPT on Retinal Vascular Disease Classification: Cross-Sectional Study](https://doi.org/10.2196/51926)** — Liu et al. · *Journal of Medical Internet Research 2023*<br>Tests ChatGPT on 1226 Chinese fundus angiography reports: English prompts beat Chinese prompts for diagnosis (F1 80.05% vs 70.47%) and give reasoning with less incompleteness, misinformation and hallucination.
- `2023-06` **[Performance of GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 on the Japanese Medical Licensing Examination: Comparison Study](https://doi.org/10.2196/48002)** — Takagi et al. · *JMIR Medical Education 2023*<br>Compares GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 on 254 questions from the 2023 Japanese Medical Licensing Examination; GPT-4 is more accurate and meets the passing criteria.
- `2023-06` **[One Law, Many Languages: Benchmarking Multilingual Legal Reasoning for Judicial Support](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09237)** — Stern et al.<br>Swiss legal benchmark in five languages with documents up to 50K tokens, covering retrieval, court view generation, summarization, citation extraction and classification; multilingual models struggle on most tasks.
- `2023-05` **[Large Language Models Leverage External Knowledge to Extend Clinical Insight Beyond Language Boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10163)** — Wu et al. · *J. Am. Medical Informatics Assoc. 2023*<br>KFE: in-context learning with Chinese medical books and question banks lifts ChatGPT from 51 to 70.04 and GPT-4 to 82.59 on the CNMLE-2022 licensing exam.
- `2023-04` **[Performance of Generative Pretrained Transformer on the National Medical Licensing Examination in Japan](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pdig.0000433)** — Tanaka et al. · *medRxiv 2023*<br>GPT-4 with tuned prompts passes Japan's 2023 National Medical Licensing Examination, scoring 82.7% on essential and 77.2% on basic and clinical questions.
- `2023-04` **[DoctorGLM: Fine-tuning your Chinese Doctor is not a Herculean Task](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.01097)** — Xiong et al.<br>DoctorGLM: ChatGLM-6B fine-tuned on Chinese medical dialogues collected with ChatGPT's help, trained on a single A100 80G in 13 hours.
- `2023` **[LEXTREME: A Multi-Lingual and Multi-Task Benchmark for the Legal Domain](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.200/)** — Niklaus et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023*<br>LEXTREME: multilingual legal benchmark of 11 datasets in 24 languages with dataset and language aggregate scores; baselines achieve modest results and ChatGPT struggles on many tasks.
- `2023` **[Arabic Mini-ClimateGPT : A Climate Change and Sustainability Tailored Arabic LLM](https://aclanthology.org/2023.findings-emnlp.941/)** — Mullappilly et al. · *Findings of EMNLP 2023*<br>Arabic Mini-ClimateGPT: an open LLM fine-tuned on Clima500-Instruct, over 500k Arabic climate instructions, with retrieval; beats its baseline in 88.3% of ChatGPT-judged cases, with 81.6% expert preference.
- `2022-12` **[Legal Prompt Engineering for Multilingual Legal Judgement Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.02199)** — Trautmann et al.<br>Zero-shot prompting of LLMs for legal judgement prediction on European Court of Human Rights (English) and Swiss Federal Supreme Court (German, French, Italian) beats baselines but trails supervised models.
